One undo step, as an `ActionChange` (whole-item snapshots, which include containment, relations, and the by-hand records). **Through another container:** a mirrored containment field the item gets via a parent whose type the mirrored type contains (a book's Universe when it is in a Series and the theme declares `contains(Universe, Series)`) is read-only on the item, with why (`read_only_reason`, `FieldRow.hint`, the editor's tooltip), and is brought up to date, as the user's, whenever an edit moves the item or its container (`refresh_indirect`, run on the edited item and its contents).
- **Ratings** (#419, API version 16; decided in review): `field(..., rating=5)` (or `rating=10`, `RATING_SCALES`) makes a `float` field a rating out of that many, stored as the number (a REAL column, §5) and shown as **five stars in half-star steps** wherever values are shown (display `"stars/5"` or `"stars/10"`, which `field()` sets; `core.formats.format_stars`: 3.5 out of 5 and 7 out of 10 both read `★★★½☆`, a value between steps rounds to the nearest half star). The built-in themes declare their ratings `card=True` and as the last of the type's `card_lines`, so they show in lists (a column shown at first) and on grid cards from the start (asked in review); a rating cell's tooltip gives the number too (`★★★½☆  3.5 out of 5`). On an item's page it is a star widget (`ui/stars.py`): clicking a star sets it, its left half the half star, and clicking the rating it has clears it, one undoable edit like any field's. Filters and searches use the numbers (the range box says "any number out of 5"). The loader refuses a rating on a field that isn't `float`, and a theme below `api_version = 16`. **One rating, the user's** (decided in review): a file's rating fills it to start with and the user's edit is never replaced (rule 9); there is no separate rating "from the files". `read_rating(value, out_of=5)` (an API helper) reads one as written (`4`, `"4.5"`, `"8/10"`, `"★★★½"`), out of 5 to the nearest half, 0 meaning unrated.

### Roles

`role(name, *, kinds, many=False, primary=False, thumbnail=False, label=None)`:

- `kinds`: accepted resource kinds (`image`, `audio`, `video`, `font`, `archive`, `dir`, `any`). Drag-and-drop onto a role slot rejects other kinds.
- `many`: one or many resources.
- `primary`: the entity's main content; defines "open file" (§11). At most one primary role per type.
- `thumbnail`: this role is tried early in thumbnail resolution.
- `gallery` and `folders` (#387, API version 14; planned as version 8, which `ctx.note` took): a picture role shown as a gallery, which the core fills from the named subfolders of the item's folder and archives of those names (§9 *Galleries*). The loader checks a gallery role is `many=True` with the `image` kind, that `folders` comes with `gallery=True` and are names, not paths, and that the theme states `api_version = 14`.

### Containment and relationships

- `contains(Parent, Child)` declares containment. The ingester creates edges via `ctx.contain(parent, child)`; the core maintains the closure table.
- `related(name, A, B, *, label=None, reverse_label=None, many=True, ordered=False)` declares a non-containment relationship (Actor ↔ Movie cast). `label` titles the section on an A's page (an actor's Filmography), `reverse_label` the one on a B's page (a movie's Cast). The core builds a link table `(a_id, b_id)`; `many=False` limits each `B` to at most one `A`. The ingester creates links via `ctx.relate(name, a, b)`. `DetailView` sections reference relationships by name, from either side.
- **Containers made by hand** (#329, API version 4; chosen in review over a relationship): an entity type with `made_by_hand = True` (the research theme's Project) is one the user makes and fills. Its views get **New \<type>…** in their header (asks a name, makes the item, an item without an ingest key that scans never delete, and opens its page); items of the types it can contain (its declared containment) get **Add to \<type>…** on their right-click menus (for the selection: choose one, or name a new one, in the Add dialog); and its page's contents list gets **Remove from \<title>**. Each is one undo step (`core/containers.py`); what the user put in or took out is recorded in `user_contains`, so scans never undo it. Being ordinary containment, a project's tags count for its papers with inherit tags, and "Within" a project lists its papers.
- **A type with itself** (#322, API version 4): `related("cites", Paper, Paper, label="Cites", reverse_label="Cited by")` has a direction: an item's page shows two related sections, its `a` side under `label` and its `b` side under `reverse_label`, each an embedded search of its side only (`SearchSpec.related_side`, `"a"` or `"b"`, saved in the spec's JSON as the related object's `side`), with its own **Add…** (adding to "Cited by" makes the other item cite this one) and **Remove**; an item can't be related to itself, and Add… offers the item as already there. `symmetric=True` (only for a type with itself) has no direction: one section lists the items related either way ("Related").
- **Ordered relationships** (#317, API version 4): `ordered=True` (which needs `many=True`) keeps each `B`'s `A` items in an order, such as a paper's authors. The link table gains a nullable `position` (added to an existing keep's table by the additive sync); items without one sort after the rest. `ctx.relate(name, a, b, position=n)` places `a` among `b`'s items (0 first; gaps are fine); without a position a new item goes last and an existing one stays. A position on an unordered relationship is an `IngestError`. `ctx.related(name, b)` returns `b`'s items in order (an `A`'s side stays in id order). A `B`'s page lists them in order: its related section's list, and its embedded search, which sorts by the special key `@position` (`core.search.POSITION`, valid only in a related search of an ordered relationship). By hand: **Add…** puts the new item last, and **Move up** / **Move down** on the items' menus (several at once, keeping their order among themselves, stopping at the ends; one undo step, `core.relations.move_related`) record a `user_order` row, after which scans keep that order (new items from files go last; removals still follow #260). Merging: an `A` merged into another keeps its place on each `B`; a merged `B`'s items go after the kept `B`'s, and its `user_order` comes along.

### Ingest context

Themes read and write keep data only through the context object `ctx` passed to `ingest()` (and to actions and `migrate()`). Entities are referred to by opaque `EntityRef` handles, not ORM objects. The core applies all writes through the DB writer (§6) and enforces provenance: extracted values never overwrite fields whose provenance is `user`.

- `ctx.upsert(Type, key, *, title=None, **fields) -> EntityRef`: find the entity of that type with this `ingest_key` (§5) or create it, then set extracted fields. An item the user merged into another (§13, `entity_merge`) isn't created again: the ref stands for the merged item, and links, edges, relations, updates, and deletes given for it are dropped (`get` reads the item it went into). Likewise `ctx.entities_of(file)` includes a merged item that had the file from its theme (`entity_merge_resource`), so a theme that finds items through their files doesn't make a new one. The key is the theme's stable natural key (for example, an album's folder path or a normalized artist name).
- `ctx.link(entity, resource, role, sort_order=0)`, `ctx.unlink(...)`. Links the user made by hand (`by_user`, #243) are the user's: `unlink` never removes them, and in a one-file role a theme's `link` is skipped while the user's file is there (removing the user's link lets the theme's come back at the next re-read).
- `ctx.contain(parent, child)`, `ctx.uncontain(parent, child)`. Within a batch the last call for an edge wins, so a theme may contain and later uncontain (or the reverse) as it works things out.
- `ctx.relate(name, a, b)`, `ctx.unrelate(name, a, b)` (the user's hand edits win, #260: `unrelate` leaves a relationship the user added, `relate` doesn't restore one the user removed, and in a `many=False` relationship it doesn't displace the user's partner), and `ctx.related(name, entity) -> list[EntityRef]` (the entities on the other side, from either end, in id order; #258), so a theme can see what it related before and let go of what's gone.
- `ctx.find(Type, **equals) -> list[EntityRef]` and `ctx.get(entity) -> Record` (read-only field values) for lookups.
- `ctx.keywords(entity, resource, keywords)` (#294; additive, so `API_VERSION` stays 2): the keywords one file gives an item (§7 *File keywords*), replacing what that file gave before (an empty list clears them; unlinking the file from the item clears them too); they become tags only by matching defined tags.
- `ctx.option(name)`: an option's value for the root being scanned (its override, else the keep's, else the default); `ctx.contents(entity)` (direct children, counting this batch's pending `contain`/`uncontain`) and `ctx.linked(entity, role=None)` (linked resource ids); `ctx.delete(entity)` deletes an entity the theme made (its links, containment, and tags go with it; resources stay), for example an artist left empty. In a scan, an item the user made by hand (no ingest key, such as an actor added to a cast as new, #260) is left alone.
- `ctx.prepared(resource)`: what `prepare()` returned for the resource, or `None` (nothing returned for it, or no prepare step, as in `migrate` and actions).
- `Theme.read_last` (#320, API version 4): extensions of files that describe other files (a bibliography export): a scan ingests them after its other pending files, and when it ingested others in that root, ingests every such file of the root again (`scanjob._read_last`). As a tuple, its extensions are read in that order (#321: bibliographies, then the notes that use their citation keys); as a set, by path.
- `ctx.resource_at(beside, relpath, assets=None)` (#297; `API_VERSION` 3, decided in review; `assets` #418, API version 9: with it, a file not at `relpath` is also looked for under the assets folders, §9 books *Assets folders*): the file or folder at a root-relative path in the same root as `beside`, as a `ResourceInfo` with this computer's path (made from `beside`'s), or `None` if it isn't scanned (not among the theme's extensions, skipped, or known missing). Case is ignored when nothing matches exactly. Resources are all recorded before any is ingested, so it finds a file whether or not that file has been ingested yet. It is how one file links another it names, such as a Markdown book's cover picture.
- `ctx.update(entity, title=None, **fields)`: set extracted values on a known entity, with the same provenance rules as `upsert`.
- `ctx.entities_of(resource, role=None) -> list[EntityRef]`: the entities linked to a resource. Because move detection (§6) carries links to a file's new path, this is how a file-based theme finds "the entity for this file"; keying such entities by path would let a new file at a vacated path take over a moved file's entity. Links the user made by hand aren't returned: the file isn't the theme's to read into that item, so re-reading it never renames or regroups the user's item (the theme may make an item of its own for it). `ctx.linked(entity)` does count them, so an item with only the user's files isn't "empty".
- `ctx.warn(resource, message)`: report a problem with a file to the activity panel.
- `ctx.note(entity, folder)` (#379, API version 8): the note inside `folder` (a folder resource) is the item's (§9 *Notes*); recorded in `entity_note.folder_id`, so later scans keep looking there. Naming another folder reads its note afresh.
- `ctx.not_found(entity, beside, relpath, *, role, written, assets=None, reason=None)` (#426, API version 13, decided in review over a lookup that returns its tries for the theme to pass on): a file `beside` names for the item's `role` wasn't found, such as a Markdown book's `cover`. The theme passes what it gave `resource_at` (`relpath`, `assets`) and what the file says (`written`); **the core works out the paths tried** (`IngestSession._candidates`, the same list `resource_at` walks), so Triage's list can't drift from the lookup, and what is at each path is worked out when Triage shows it (§12). With `relpath=None` nothing was looked for and `reason` says why (a web address, a drive's path, outside the watched folder). A file's reports are replaced each time it is ingested (`forget_not_found`, before the theme's `ingest`), so a theme reports only what is still missing; a report isn't listed once the item has a file in `role` (a picture scanned later), and goes with the item. The books theme (version 6) reports Markdown covers. A theme that finds files its own way could later pass its own tries (an optional `tried=`).
- `ctx.extra(entity, values)` (#410, API version 11, decided in review): Extra fields from the file being read, such as a Markdown file's own front-matter keys the theme doesn't read into fields (`read_front_matter`'s `other`: the keys of `fields` it didn't read, `FRONT_MATTER_READ`). The core applies a note's rules (§9 *Notes*, `core/extra_keys.py`: text as `as_text` makes it; `tagalot-…`, `title`, `tags`, `keywords`, `aliases`, and ignored keys left out), with provenance `extracted` ("• from the file" on the page). Each call replaces what the item's file gave before (a key it stops giving is removed), so a theme calls it with every key each time it reads the file. A key the user removed (`note_key_removal`) or edited (`user`) is left alone, as is an Extra field of the user's own with that name. **The file wins over the item's note** (as for fields): a key both give is the file's (`apply_note` skips keys with provenance `extracted`), and when the file stops giving it, the item's note is marked to be read again (`entity_note.size` cleared) so the note's value comes back in the same scan. Only the file being read calls it, so another file of the same item (a book's EPUB) leaves the Markdown file's fields alone. **Ignored keys:** ignoring a key from a page removes file-given fields too (and Undo restores them with their provenance); a changed list re-reads every Markdown resource (`sync_ignored`, now run before a root's files are ingested) as well as every note. The books theme (version 5) passes a Markdown book's other keys; the research theme's literature notes don't (they describe papers, and reference-manager templates add many bookkeeping keys).
- Actions get an `ActionContext`, a superset of this one; see Actions below.

Batch items are read-only `ResourceInfo` values: root id, relative path, kind, extension, size, mtime, and a readable local path. Themes may open files for reading; they must never write under a root.

Implementation details (`core/ingest.py`, `IngestSession`):

- **Provenance:** `upsert` records `extracted` provenance for the title and every field it writes, and skips any the user edited (`user`), title included; the entity is still found by its ingest key after the user renames it.
- **Validation:** an unknown field, a role not declared on that type, undeclared containment, a relationship between the wrong types, or a type from another theme raises `IngestError` (a theme bug; the pipeline reports it per batch).
- **Single-valued roles** (`many=False`) keep one resource: linking another replaces it. Relinking updates `sort_order`. Likewise a `many=False` relationship keeps one `a` per `b`.
- **Batching:** containment edges and search-index updates are collected and applied by `flush()` (`closure.apply`, whose rejected edges become warnings, then `fts.sync_entities` with the theme's `search="text"` fields). The pipeline calls `flush()` before each transaction commits.
- `ctx.warn()` collects warnings (root, relative path, message) for the activity panel.

### Views

- `SearchView(name, types, *, inherit_tags=False, show_contained=False, aggregate_up=False, layout="grid"|"list"|"tree", default_sort=...)`.
- `DetailView(type, sections=[...])` with sections for fields, role slots, galleries, related entities, and, for containers, an **embedded search** of contents.
- Escape hatch: a view or section may return a custom `QWidget` factory. The core passes it a limited context object (selection, navigation, open-entity callbacks).
- `double_click` per type: `"page"` (default) or `"open_file"`; the other action moves to Ctrl+Enter.

### Actions

`@action(label, applies_to=[types or roles])` registers a context-menu and detail-page action, for example "Play album" (write a temporary `.m3u` in track order and open it with the OS). Actions must not modify user files.

Implementation (#106, `core/actions.py`, `core/entity_state.py`):

- **Applies to** an entity type listed, or a type that declares a role listed. `actions_for(schema, type_id)` lists them in declaration order.
- **Called as** `method(entities, ctx)`. `entities` are the selected items the action applies to (the clicked item if it isn't selected), or the page's item. `ctx` is an `ActionContext`: everything the ingest context offers (its writes keep the same provenance rules), plus `contents(entity)` in the container's `contents_sort` order, `resources(entity, role=None)` (files with this computer's paths, leaving out missing and offline ones), `temp_path(name)` (a file in a folder of the session's own, deleted when the keep closes; only the name's last part is used), and `open(path)`, `reveal(path)`, `message(text)`; and, in API version 4 (#322), `copy_text(text)` (the clipboard), `open_url(url)` (`http`/`https` only, in the browser; anything else is an `ActionError`), and `save_text(name, text)` (a file name without folders: after the action, a save dialog starting in the last folder chosen; see §4 *Saving an export*). Outputs are carried out in order after the commit.
- **Runs in the DB writer**, in one transaction: an exception rolls back everything it wrote, and the status bar says "\<label> failed: …". Outputs are collected and carried out on the GUI thread after the commit, in order. `open` honors the user's overrides (§11). The status bar shows the action's last message, else "\<label>: done."
- **Undo:** while an action runs, the ingest session tells a `ChangeRecorder` about every entity before its first write. That covers both ends of a containment edge or relationship, a deleted entity's neighbours, and a many=False relationship's previous partner. A created entity is recorded as not existing. Each is snapshotted whole: row, theme fields, provenance, file links, tags, parents and children, and relationships. Snapshots are taken again at the end. The run is one undo step (`ActionChange`, labelled with the action), recorded only if something changed. Undo and redo restore the snapshots exactly: created entities are deleted, deleted ones are recreated with their id, tags, and edges, and the closure and search index are brought up to date. `updated_at` and the thumbnail memo are not restored; they are set afresh.
- **Where:** a result's right-click menu lists the actions for its type, after Re-read. A detail page shows them as buttons beside More ▾. Contents searches on a page get the same menus.

### Online details (M22, #307; designed in review, core in #339)

Looking items' details up online by identifier: Research papers by DOI (Crossref) and arXiv ID (arXiv), Books by ISBN (Open Library). It is the first time Tagalot uses the network, so it is narrow on purpose (decided in review, 2026-10-05):

- **Only the core goes online** (chosen over themes fetching for themselves). A theme declares the services it uses, `online_sources = [OnlineSource("Crossref", "api.crossref.org", "DOIs")]` (a name, a host, and what is sent), and two methods: `online_requests(entity_type, item)` gives the addresses to fetch for an item (only `https` addresses on its declared hosts; others are refused), and `online_details(entity, responses, ctx)` turns the responses into the item's details in the DB writer. So the consent dialog can name every service and what it is sent, and nothing else leaves the computer. Theme API version 5 (version 4 shipped in 0.7.0).
- **Fetching** (`core/online.py`): the standard library's `urllib` (no new dependency), `https` only, a `User-Agent` naming Tagalot and its version, a timeout, at most one request a second to each host (three seconds for arXiv, as it asks), and backing off on `429` and server errors. Offline, or when a service fails, nothing changes and the items are tried again later. Responses are cached in the keep (a core table, keyed by address, with when it was fetched), so an identifier is fetched once; **Look up online** fetches again.
- **Consent, per keep:** nothing is looked up until the first time a lookup would happen. Then Tagalot asks once, naming the services the theme declares and what each is sent ("Look up details online? Tagalot would send DOIs to Crossref and arXiv IDs to arXiv. Nothing else about your files leaves this computer."), with **Allow for this keep**, **Not now** (ask again next time; nothing is sent), and **Never**. The answer is saved in `keep.toml` (`[online] lookups = "allow"` or `"never"`) and can be changed in Keep configuration (Keep tab). With **Never**, **Look up online** offers to turn lookups on.
- **When** (chosen in review): automatically, in the background after a scan, for items the scan made or changed whose identifiers have no cached response; and on demand, **Look up online** on selected items (or a page's More menu), which refreshes. Progress shows in the status bar and the activity panel; problems (a service down, an identifier it doesn't know) are listed there.
- **What wins** (chosen in review): looked-up details rank **below the files' own curated metadata** and above what Tagalot guesses from a file. In the research theme: a literature note, then a sidecar, then a library export, then online details, then the PDF, then the file name; the user's edits always win (fields written from online details have provenance `fetched`, §5). A theme without ranked sources fills in only fields that are empty.
- **Privacy:** only the identifiers in the addresses are sent; no account, key, or telemetry. The addresses fetched are logged.
- **As built** (#339): `OnlineSource(name, host, sends, interval=1.0)` checks the host is a bare lowercase host name; a theme with `online_sources` needs `api_version = 5`, and names each host once. Addresses must be plain `https` on a declared host (no other port, no user name); anything else is reported in the activity panel and never fetched. A redirect is followed only to `https` on a service the keep allows, its host or a subdomain of it (Open Library sends `/isbn/…` to `/books/…`, #340; its covers go to the Internet Archive and on to one of its servers, #484): the fetcher follows it (`Reply.location`, at most 5 hops, each spaced like any request), never urllib. One elsewhere is treated as the answer, never requested; a picture whose answer is such a redirect is tried again in later runs, so allowing the service brings it. `online_details(entity, responses, ctx)` runs once every address `online_requests` gave has an answer: `responses` maps each address to an `OnlineResponse` (`url`, `status`, `text`, `ok`, `json()`); an item with a kept answer for every address is applied again from the keep, without going online. A service is waited for 20 s; a busy one (`429` or `5xx`) is tried twice more, after its `Retry-After` or 5 then 10 s (never more than a minute); one that can't be reached or stays busy is given up on for the rest of that run, and its items are left for next time (other services go ahead). The theme's `ctx` is an ingest context whose values are marked `fetched`; reading a file again (extraction) replaces `fetched` values like any extracted ones, so a file's own details win unless the theme ranks its sources. An item the theme fails on is rolled back alone and reported. Lookups aren't undo steps, like scans: the user's edits are never touched. `KeepSession.look_up` refuses unless `keep.toml` says `lookups = "allow"`; the Keep tab has **Look up details online**, shown for themes with services.
- **In the window** (#340, `ui/lookups.py`): after each scan, the items it made or changed (created or updated since it began) are looked up; those with kept answers for every address are applied again from them, without going online. The question is asked only when a lookup would fetch something, so a keep whose theme finds no identifiers is never asked. **Allow for this keep** (or turning lookups on in the Keep tab) then looks up every item without kept answers, once, so what was already in the keep isn't left out. **Not now** isn't asked again until the keep is opened again, except by **Look up online**. **Look up online** is on every item's right-click menu (for the selection) and its page's More menu in a theme with services, whatever the type: the theme decides what each item has to look up, and "Nothing to look up online" says when nothing did. With **Never**, it offers to turn lookups on. One run at a time; requests made meanwhile wait their turn. The status bar shows "Looking up online: N left", then a summary ("Looked up 12 items online, 2 not found."); problems go to the activity panel. Closing the keep stops a run before its next request.
- **Pictures** (#483, M35; decided in discussion, 2026-10-20): in `online_details` a theme names a picture of an item with `ctx.online_picture(entity, url)` (API version 21), on one of its declared hosts (anything else fails the item, reported). After the details, the core fetches the pictures named for the items of the run that it hasn't fetched (all of them on **Look up online**), by the same rules (https, declared hosts, spacing, backing off), and keeps them in the keep (`online_picture`, never in the user's folders): a 404 is kept as "none", so it isn't asked for again. Naming another address replaces the item's picture. **Shown only when the item has no picture of its own:** the resolver tries it after the user's choice, the note's picture, the theme's chain, and the first gallery picture, before a title card or icon (§10); it is never remembered as the item's source (`thumb_resource_id`), so a local picture added later wins at once. The window redraws the items that got one.
- **Consent per service** (#483; decided in discussion, 2026-10-20: ask once more): `keep.toml` lists the services allowed (`[online] allowed`, by host) and those refused (`refused`). A keep that allowed lookups before, with no list, allowed the services the built-in themes had then (`LEGACY_HOSTS`: Open Library, Crossref, arXiv). When a lookup is about to run and the theme declares a service neither allowed nor refused (a new one), the window asks once, naming it and what it is sent, with **Allow**, **Not now** (asked again when the keep is opened again), and **Don't use it** (`refused`); the lookup goes ahead with what is allowed either way, and addresses on a service not allowed wait, without a problem reported. **Allow** then looks up everything, so items already in the keep get what the new service gives. Turning lookups on in the Keep tab allows every service the theme declares.
- **Tokens** (#485; decided in discussion, 2026-10-20): a service that needs one is declared with the environment variable holding it, `OnlineSource(..., token_env="TAGALOT_TMDB_TOKEN")` (API version 23). The core reads it each time it fetches and sends it as `Authorization: Bearer`; it is never written anywhere (not `keep.toml`, settings, the log, or the kept answers, which are keyed by address). While the variable isn't set, the service is left out silently: not fetched from, not named in the consent question, not allowed by turning lookups on, and not asked about; the Keep tab says which variable to set ("TMDb needs a token: set the TAGALOT_TMDB_TOKEN environment variable to yours, then start Tagalot again."). Once it is set, a keep that allows lookups asks about the service once, like any new one. **A refused token** (#487, found in review: a token pasted wrong left movies without details for good): a `401` or `403` refuses the request, not the identifier, so it is never kept (unlike a `404`); the service is given up on for the run, its items left for next time, and the Activity panel says "TMDb: refused the token in TAGALOT_TMDB_TOKEN (HTTP 401): check it in Keep configuration, Keep tab, Environment". Answers and pictures kept with those statuses by earlier builds count as not asked yet.
- **A keep's environment** (#544, M35; asked for in discussion, 2026-10-25): so a key needn't be a system-wide environment variable, each keep has named variables of its own for the user, used ahead of the real environment (which is the fallback; chosen in discussion: the keep's wins). They are kept in the user's `settings.toml`, under `[environment.<keep id>]` (chosen in discussion over the system keychain, which needs a new dependency and a running service on Linux, and over the keep folder, which a shared keep would share), never in the keep, and never logged (a bad entry is reported by name only). This revises #485's "never written anywhere": a token is written only there, and only when the user types it. The Keep tab's **Environment** box lists them (values hidden as dots unless **Show values**), then the variables the theme's services need that aren't set (`needed_env`: "TAGALOT_TMDB_TOKEN: not set (TMDb needs it)"), with **Set…** (the value typed hidden), **Add…** (a name, then its value), and **Remove**; an empty value removes it. Names are letters, digits, and `_`, not starting with a digit. Setting one updates the token note and the consent text at once; `KeepSession.environment()` reads the user's settings each time, so lookups use it from then on, and a service whose token is now set is asked about once (#483).
- **What may identify an item** (#485; decided in discussion, 2026-10-20), per keep: `keep.toml` `[online] find_by`, the Keep tab's **Find items by**: **IDs only** (`ids`, the default), **IDs, or a title with its year** (`title-year`), or **IDs, or any title** (`title`). **The core enforces it** (chosen in discussion, 2026-10-25, over telling the theme): a theme gives every address it has, an address that finds an item by title being an `OnlineRequest(url, by="title-year")` or `by="title"` (a plain string is by ID), on a service declared with `titles=True` (else the item fails, reported); `plan_lookups` drops those the keep doesn't allow, without a problem, so the consent's promise holds whatever the theme. What a search finds (an ID) the theme sets in `online_details`; the run's second round then looks the item up by it (the rounds now take items of the first round too, for addresses not asked for yet). The consent text says so for such a service: "TMDb and IMDb IDs, or titles with their years, to TMDb". The Find items by setting shows only for themes with a `titles` service.
- Movies look things up in TMDb (#487, §9 movies *TMDb*); music (MusicBrainz) can follow on the same hooks.

### Notes (M27; designed in review, 2026-10-10)

A **note** is a Markdown file that describes an item: its front matter gives details and tags, its body is shown on the item's page. Notes let the user say what no file says: a friendlier name for a collection's folder, an artist's background, facts about an actor. Tagalot reads notes and never changes them, except **Save as note…** into a notes folder the user made writable (below).

- **Where notes come from:**
  - **Folder notes:** an item whose primary file is a folder (assets' Artist, music's Album, movies' Collection) takes the note inside that folder: `<folder name>.md`, else `index.md`, else `README.md`, each also as `.markdown`, names compared ignoring case. When several are there, the first in that order is the note; the others stay ordinary files. Only inside the folder: a note beside it (`Parent/Folder.md`) isn't looked for.
  - **Notes a theme names:** `ctx.note(entity, folder)` (API version 8) applies the note inside `folder` (a folder resource, found by the same names) to an item whose primary file isn't a folder, such as a Movie and its folder. The core does the reading, so every theme's notes behave alike.
  - **The notes folder:** one folder per keep, chosen in Keep configuration, for items that have no folder (actors, authors, series, a music artist). See *The notes folder* below.
  - **In-app notes:** an item without a note file can have a note typed on its page, stored in the keep (below).
  - **The built-in themes** (#384): assets' Artist, music's Album, and movies' Collection take their folders' notes; a Movie takes its own folder's note (`ctx.note`, only for a movie's folder, not loose videos' folder; movies theme version 4, so keeps read their files again once), its `.nfo` ranking above it. Actors, authors, series, and music artists take theirs from the notes folder. Assets' Artist gains a **Website** field (`display="url"`, added by the additive sync), which its note can fill (chosen in review: the type had no fields for a note to fill), and a **Folder** field (not editable) holding its folder's name, so the Biggest artists card still counts the assets of an artist a note renamed (found while building; assets theme version 3, so keeps read their files again once). The demos (`make_demo_keep.py`) show each: Aurora Studio's note (title, website, a tag, Extra fields, a picture), Live Box's (a year its songs lack), Inception's (below its `.nfo`), and the movies and books demos' notes folders (chosen in `keep.toml`; chosen in review), with notes in a type's subfolder and typed by `tagalot-type`, and one that matches no item.
- **One note per item.** A folder note (or one a theme names) wins over a notes-folder note for the same item; Triage lists the other as a second note.
- **Scanning:** the scanner records Markdown files named as notes (`<folder name>.md`, `index.md`, `README.md`, and `.markdown`) in the folders the theme records (its `dirs` rule), whatever the theme's extensions, so notes are found in themes that don't take `.md` (assets, music, movies). (As built, #379: only those folders, not every folder, so a `README.md` elsewhere is neither recorded nor listed as unlinked.) A note-named file the theme doesn't take, and a file that is an item's note, is never handed to the theme as content; an item's note isn't listed as unlinked (a second note in the same folder is). A note that changes is read again at the next scan, and its item updated.
- **What a note gives** (`core/notes.py`), from its front matter (YAML or TOML, as `read_front_matter` reads it) and body:
  - **Title** (`title`): the item's title. For a folder note it wins over the title the theme took from the folder's name (the only other source); for other notes it fills an empty title, as a field does. The user's edit always wins.
  - **Tags** (`tags`, `keywords`): file keywords of the item from the note file (§7 *File keywords*): they become tags only by matching tags the user defined, and unmatched ones are listed in Triage like any file's.
  - **Fields:** a key matching one of the type's fields, by its name or its label (ignoring case, spaces, `_` and `-`), sets that field, converted to its type (a value that doesn't convert is reported in the activity panel and skipped). A note ranks **below the theme's own reading of files** (an `.nfo`, embedded tags, an EPUB's package) and **above online details**: it fills a field that no file gave, replaces a value looked up online, and gives way when a file later gives the field. The user's edits win over all of them. Values from a note have provenance `note` (§5): a note that changes updates them; a note that drops a key clears the field it set.
  - **The keep's own fields** (#584, M45; decided in discussion, 2026-10-28; `core/own_notes.py`): a key named like one of the type's own fields (its name or label, matched the same way, after the type's theme fields and before key aliases) fills it with provenance `note`; a Markdown file's own front matter (`ctx.extra`, #410) fills it as the file's (`extracted`), and the file's wins over the note's, as for fields. The user's value is never replaced (rule 9); a value the note or file stops giving is cleared. A value that doesn't read as the field's kind stays an Extra field, and the scan says so in the activity panel ("The front matter of 'The Story': finished: isn't a date (YYYY-MM-DD), so it stays an Extra field"). **Read again when they change:** the keep remembers the own fields its notes and Markdown files were last read with (`own_field_read`, core format 32), as for key aliases; when `keep.toml` says otherwise (a field added, relabelled, or removed, by Keep configuration, a promotion, or by hand), the next scan reads them all again (`sync_own_fields`, `extra_keys.read_again`). Decided while building: remembered in the database like the aliases, rather than passed to every scan step. **Found in review:** an Extra field a note or file gave under the key before it was a field's (`pages: soon`), and that the user had edited on the page, stayed beside the field once the key filled it, since edits are never replaced. Now, when a note or file fills an own field, Extra fields it or another note or file gave under that key go (`absorb_extras`); one the user edited moves into the field first, unless the field has a value of the user's or the text isn't of its kind. An Extra field the user typed (no provenance) stays.
  - **Everything else** becomes an **Extra field** (§12) with provenance `note`: lists are joined with `, `, dates written as `YYYY-MM-DD`, nested tables skipped. Deleting one on the item's page is remembered for that item (`note_key_removal`, one undo step), so later scans leave it off; editing one makes it the user's (provenance `user`), and scans no longer change it. A key the note no longer has is removed, unless the user edited it.
  - **Body:** the Markdown after the front matter (without a leading `# Title` heading that repeats the title), shown in the page's header (§12) and searchable as text (`entity_fts` body).
- **As built** (#379, `core/notes.py`):
  - **The notes step:** after a root's files are ingested, the scan finds the root's items that take notes (`note_sites`: those whose primary role is a folder, and those with an `entity_note.folder_id`), each folder's best-named note (`note_files`; a `.md` before a `.markdown` of the same name), and reads only those whose file, size, or time differ from `entity_note` (`read_notes`, in the scan's thread, never the DB writer's); one writer job applies them (`apply_notes`). The status bar says "Looking for notes in …". A note that can't be read (unreadable, front matter that doesn't parse) is reported in the activity panel and the item keeps what its note gave before; a value that doesn't convert is reported and left out.
  - **A value the note stops giving** (the note changed, or its file is gone) is cleared, its provenance dropped, and the item's primary files are marked pending and ingested again at once, so the theme's own value (the folder's name, a file's field) comes back.
  - **Files and a note's values** (`IngestSession._apply_extracted`): themes write every field each time they read a file, empty ones included, so a file's empty value never replaces a note's, a folder note's title is never replaced by the folder's name, and online details (`fetched`) never replace a note's value. A file's non-empty value does replace a note's (the theme's own reading ranks above the note).
  - **Missing isn't gone:** only items whose folder is there (`ok`) are looked at, so a folder or note found missing keeps the note it had (AGENTS.md rule 7); a deleted resource sets `resource_id` null and the note goes at the next scan.
  - **Snapshots** carry an item's `entity_note` row and its `note_key_removal` keys, so undoing a merge or an action brings them back.
  - **On the page** (#380, `core/note_markdown.py`, `ui/note_view.py`): the body is rendered with Qt's Markdown (`QTextBrowser.setMarkdown`), after Obsidian's `![[picture.png]]` embeds become Markdown pictures and `[[Note|shown]]` links their text (`renderable`). The document loads nothing itself (`loadResource` answers only from what was read), so the GUI thread never reads a disk or a share: the page's worker reads the note's pictures from its folder (`read_pictures`: relative paths only, at most 12, 10 MB each, picture extensions only; web pictures aren't fetched) and scales them to at most 480 px wide. Links open web addresses in the browser and relative paths (resolved from the note's folder, `resolve_link`; absolute paths and other schemes are ignored) with their program. A picture or link not beside the note is looked for in the item's assets folders (#418, §9 books *Assets folders*): the worker reads such pictures from there, and finds where up to 50 such links are (`find_links`), so following one doesn't touch a disk. **Open note** opens the note file through the usual file opener (`resource_to_open` finds a note file, which no role links). The cap is the thumbnail's 192 px less the breadcrumbs, title, and type above the note, at least about three lines.
- **Keys Tagalot reads itself** start with `tagalot-` (`tagalot-type`, below), are never Extra fields, and can't be ignored. `aliases` is read, not shown: a notes-folder note matches an item by them.
- **Ignored keys** never become Extra fields. Three lists apply together: built-in defaults for housekeeping that editors add (`cssclasses`, `cssclass`, `publish`, `permalink`, `position`); the user's own, in `settings.toml` (`[notes] ignore_keys`, for every keep on this computer); and the keep's, in `keep.toml` (`[notes] ignore_keys`, travelling with the keep). An Extra field from a note has **Ignore this key in every note…** (for this keep), and Keep configuration lists both editable lists. Ignoring a key removes its Extra fields from the keep's items (undoable).
  - **As built** (#381, `core/note_keys.py`): keys are compared case-folded; a list is written as typed, each key once. **Changing a list** (in Keep configuration's **Notes** tab, or by hand) applies at the next scan: the keep remembers the keys its notes were last applied with (`note_ignored_key`), and when the lists say otherwise every note file is read again, so a newly ignored key's fields go and a key no longer ignored comes back (the existing rule: a key the note doesn't give is removed unless the user edited it). Scans that don't pass the lists (re-reading chosen files) keep the remembered keys. **Ignore this key in every note…** is on the arrow beside a note field's ×, asks first, adds the key to `keep.toml`, and at once removes the Extra fields with provenance `note` under that key from every item; fields the user added or edited stay. Undo puts them back with their provenance and takes the key off `keep.toml` (unless the keep listed it already). The undo history lives in the DB writer's world, so the session gives `TagService` a hook (`on_note_key`) that rewrites `keep.toml` as the step is undone and redone.
- **Your own key aliases** (#411; decided in discussion, 2026-10-26): a key a theme doesn't read, which would otherwise be an Extra field, can fill a field instead (`synopsis` → Description). An alias names the field by its name or label (matched as notes match keys: case, spaces, `_` and `-` aside) and applies to every type that has such a field; for a type without one the key stays an Extra field. Two lists apply together, like ignored keys: the user's (`settings.toml`, `[notes.key_aliases]`) and the keep's (`keep.toml`, `[notes.key_aliases]`), the keep's winning for the same key. They apply to notes and to Markdown files' own front matter (`ctx.extra`), never to keys a theme reads itself, nor to `title`, `tags`, `keywords`, `aliases`, `cover`, `image`, or `tagalot-…` keys (refused when listed). A file's own key for the field (its name or label, or the theme's reading of it) wins, and the alias then stays an Extra field; an ignored key stays ignored.
  - **As built** (#411, `core/note_keys.py`, `core/extra_keys.py`): the keep remembers the aliases its notes and Markdown files were last read with (`note_key_alias`, format 30); when the lists say otherwise, the next scan reads every note and Markdown file again (`sync_aliases`, beside `sync_ignored`), so a newly aliased key fills its field and a key no longer aliased is an Extra field again. In a note (`map_note`) an aliased key fills the field with provenance `note`, after the note's own keys (which win, in either order); a value that doesn't convert is reported and kept as an Extra field. In a file (`IngestSession.extra`) it fills the field as the theme's values do (provenance `extracted`, the user's edits and the note ranking as for any file value), unless the theme gave that field a value from the file in the same read; themes write every field each time they read a file, so a value an alias stops giving is cleared. **Write to file…** writes an edited field the file holds under an aliased key (and not under its own name or label) back under that key (`writeback.aliased_keys`), as it keeps a theme's own alternatives (`synopsis`, #409).
  - **Adding one:** Keep configuration → Notes, **Keys to read as fields**, `key = field` a line, for this keep and for all the user's keeps (saved when the box loses the focus; a line without `=` or a refused key is said under the boxes). On an item's page, the arrow beside a note's or file's Extra field offers **Read this key as a field…**, which asks which of the item's type's fields it fills and adds the alias to `keep.toml`; it applies at the next scan, as the status bar says.

#### The notes folder

- **Choosing it** (Keep configuration, Notes; chosen in review over the Folders tab): any folder inside a watched folder (`Assets/Notes`), or a folder of its own, which is then added as a root used only for notes. Tagalot never recognizes a notes folder by its name (a music library may have an album called "Notes"). `keep.toml`: `[notes] root = "<root id>"`, `path = "Notes"` (inside that root; empty for the whole root).
- **Not content:** nothing under the notes folder is handed to the theme, as if it were on the root's Skip list; Tagalot reads it only for notes and their galleries (*Galleries*, below). The scanner records its notes and, since #388, its pictures and archives (`scanner.NOTES_FOLDER_EXTENSIONS`), for those galleries; Triage's Unlinked files leaves the notes folder out (`triage.unlinked_files`'s `notes`).
- **Which type a note is for:** its `tagalot-type` key (a type's name or label, singular or plural, ignoring case: `actor`, `Actors`), else its subfolder's name the same way (`Notes/Actors/Keanu Reeves.md`), else any type. The key wins when both are there, so a note in the wrong folder can be corrected from inside it. Notes may sit in one folder with `tagalot-type`, in a subfolder per type, or both.
- **Which item:** the note's title (`title`, else its file name without the extension) or one of its `aliases` equals the item's title or one of its own aliases, ignoring case and accents. One item of the type matching is the note's item; a renamed item keeps its note through `aliases` (or Triage asks).
- **Triage** gains **Notes** (renamed **Markdown** in #438): notes that match no item, notes matching several items (add `tagalot-type`, or an alias), and items with two notes; each with **Open file** and **Show in file manager**.
- **As built** (#382, `core/notes_folder.py`; stored in `notes_folder_note`, core format 17, chosen in review over re-reading every note at every scan):
  - **Scanning:** `walk_root(notes=…)` lists only the notes folder's Markdown files (no folders, nothing else; what was recorded there before is flagged skipped), and the ingest step keeps all of it from the theme. A notes folder that is a whole root (`path = ""`) is a root used only for notes: nothing in it is content.
  - **After the roots** (`scan_notes_folder`, at the end of every `scan_all`, so an item a later root creates finds a note already there): notes whose file, size, or time changed are read (in the worker); every note is matched again from its stored title, aliases, and type, against every item's title, case and accents folded as tag search folds them (`search_key`). The "item's own aliases" in the design aren't built: items have no aliases of their own yet, so a note's aliases match items' titles. A `tagalot-type` naming no type is reported and the folder (else any type) is used.
  - **Applying:** one writer job stores each note's row (`matches`, `item_id`, `applied`) and applies the notes that changed hands or changed, with `apply_note` as for a note a theme names (`folder_note=False`: a notes-folder note never renames its item; the item's `entity_note.folder_id` is kept). Of several notes matching one item, the first by path is its note. An item with a note from its own folder (or one a theme named) keeps it; `read_notes` leaves a notes-folder note alone when the item's folder has no note.
  - **Offline and missing:** an offline note keeps its item and its row; a missing one lets the item go (its values are cleared and its files read again) and its row is dropped.
  - **Triage → Markdown** (formerly Notes; `note_problems`): rows that matched no item, several, or one that has another note; notes-folder notes are left out of **Unlinked files**.
- **Front-matter problems** (#438, decided in review; `core/markdown_check.py`, `readers.front_matter_problems`): every Markdown file a scan reads (`.md`, `.markdown`: books, literature notes, folder notes, notes-folder notes; whatever the theme does with it) is checked first for a key given twice, counting keys that differ only in case (Tagalot reads front-matter keys ignoring case), with the file's line numbers, and for front matter that doesn't parse. YAML's own rule is kept (the last value wins, and the message says which value that is); TOML refuses a key given twice, so that's "doesn't parse", and its keys differing only in case are reported without lines. Repeated keys are the scan's warnings in the Activity panel; front matter that doesn't parse isn't (whatever reads the file reports it already). Both are kept in `markdown_problem` (core format 22), replaced each time the file is read and listed in **Triage → Markdown** beside the notes (one tab for Markdown files that need attention: decided in review). Format 22's upgrade marks Markdown files to be read again, so problems already there are found at the next scan. **Write to file…** refuses a file whose front matter gives a key twice (ruamel.yaml does), now saying which key and where.
  - **Keep configuration → Notes:** **Choose…** finds which watched folder holds the chosen folder (`notes_folder_for`, comparing as the OS does: case and either slash on Windows, UNC shares), else offers to watch it as a root of its own for notes only (`[notes] path = ""`); **None** stops using one. Removing the notes folder's root clears `[notes] root`.

#### In-app notes and Save as note…

- **Typing a note in Tagalot:** every item's page has a **Notes** box (Markdown, shown rendered, edited in place like a field). It is stored in the keep (`entity_note` with no file), one undo step per edit, and searchable. An item with a note file shows that file's body here, read-only, with **Open note**: the file is where it's edited, and the keep holds only what it last read from it (re-read at scans, kept while the file is offline, removed with the file), so every item has one place its note is edited.
- **Save as note…** (an item's More menu, offered when the notes folder is writable and the item has no note file) writes the in-app note as a new file in the notes folder: front matter with `title`, `tagalot-type` (in the one-folder layout), and the item's tags (as Write to file… writes them, §4), then the body. The next scan reads it, and the item's note is the file's from then on.
  - **Writable on its own:** the notes folder is marked writable separately (`[notes] writable = true`, Keep configuration asks first), so a notes folder inside a content folder can be written without making that content folder writable. Nothing else is written, and nothing is ever replaced: an existing file of that name is reported, not overwritten. File names are made safe for every system (`:`, `?`, `*`, `/`, `\`, `"`, `<`, `>`, `|` replaced).
  - **Layout** (`[notes] new_notes`): `"folders"` (a subfolder per type, named by its plural: `Actors/Keanu Reeves.md`) or `"one-folder"` (all in the notes folder, typed by `tagalot-type`). Notes Tagalot didn't write may follow either.
- **As built** (#383; `core/fields.py` `edit_note`, `core/save_note.py`, `ui/note_view.py`):
  - **On the page** (chosen in review: in the header, where a note file's body shows, rather than a Notes section): an item without a note file shows **Add a note**, or its typed note with ✎; ✎ or double-clicking the note opens a box (eight lines) with **Save** and **Cancel**; Ctrl+Enter or clicking away saves, Esc cancels, Enter is a new line. Emptying the box removes the note. Each save is one undo step ("Add a note to …", "Edit the note of …", "Remove the note of …"). A note file's note has no ✎: **Open note** edits it.
  - **Stored** as `entity_note` with no file (`resource_id`, `size`, `mtime_ns` null; `folder_id` kept when a theme named a folder), so it is searchable like a note file's body. Scans leave it alone while no note file is found.
  - **A note file that turns up wins** (chosen in review): its body replaces the typed note, and when the two differ (a leading `# Title` aside) the activity panel says so, quoting the typed note's first 200 characters. Undoing an edit of a note that is a file by then changes nothing.
  - **Save as note…** (More, shown when the notes folder is writable and the item has no note file) works out the file in a worker, shows its path to confirm, writes it, and scans the notes folder's root. It also refuses when a file is where the other layout would put the note (`Actors/Keanu Reeves.md` or `Keanu Reeves.md`), since a scan would make it a second note for the item (found in review). The front matter is `title`, `tagalot-type` (the type's label: in the one-folder layout, or when the subfolder's name could mean another type), and `tags` as Write to file… writes them; then the body. The subfolder is the type's plural label. File names: `:?*/\"<>|` and control characters become `_`, spaces are collapsed, leading and trailing dots and spaces go, a Windows device name (`CON`, `NUL`, `COM1`…) gets a leading `_`, at most 120 characters, `Untitled` when nothing is left.

### Galleries (M28; designed in review, 2026-10-10)

Pictures that belong to an item, browsed on its page: a movie's screenshots, an album's scanned booklet, an artist's previews, an actor's photos.

- **A gallery is a role** (`role(..., many=True, gallery=True)`, kinds `image` and `archive`), shown by `Section.gallery(name)` (§12). Besides the files a theme links to it, a gallery role can name **folders** (`folders=("Screenshots", "Screens")`, API version 14): the core fills it with the pictures and archives under those subfolders of the item's folder (its primary folder, else the folder of its primary file), at any depth, and with archives of those names beside them (`Screenshots.zip`, `.rar`, `.7z`). Names are compared ignoring case.
- **Notes-folder galleries:** a note in the notes folder may have a folder or archive of the same name beside it (`Notes/Actors/Keanu Reeves/`, `Keanu Reeves.zip`): its pictures and archives fill the item's first gallery role. This is how an item without a folder gets a gallery. **As built** (#388, `gallery_fill.fill_note_galleries`, run after the notes are matched in `scan_notes_folder`): for each note applied to an item (`notes_folder_note.applied`), the folder named like the note (its file name without the extension, case aside) beside it gives every picture and archive under it, at any depth, and archives so named beside it give themselves, all of kinds the item's first gallery role takes; links are `by_folder`, as for named folders. It follows the note: a note that stops matching, or is gone, takes its gallery with it at the next scan. The two fills keep apart by place: the notes folder's files are this fill's, the rest the named folders' (`fill_galleries`'s `notes_folder`), so a notes folder inside an item's named folder isn't claimed by both. Without a notes folder, scans of its old root take the links back. A gallery from both a folder and an archive groups as usual: the folder becomes a group named like the note.
- **What a gallery shows** (level 1, chosen in review): loose pictures first, then one group per subfolder (headed by its name, `The Matrix`), then one group per archive (headed by its file name), each group's pictures in natural name order. An archive's pictures are listed when the page opens, in a worker, with the thumbnails' archive reader and its budgets (§10 *Archives*); its pictures aren't resources and can't be tagged or linked on their own. An archive inside an archive isn't opened (it shows as one picture, its best). Opening a picture from an archive extracts it to the system temp folder and opens that copy; the archive is never touched.
- **Thumbnails of pictures in archives** are cached by the archive's resource, the member's path inside it, and the archive's size and time, so a changed archive makes them again.
- **As built** (#386, level 1; `core/gallery.py`, `ui/gallery_view.py`):
  - **Groups** (`gallery_groups`): "loose" means directly in the folder every file of the role shares (within one watched folder); each deeper folder is a group headed by its path under it (`Disc 2/Deleted`); archives (the archive kind's extensions) come last. Groups and pictures are in natural order (`page2` before `page10`). One folder alone is all loose, with no header.
  - **Listing an archive** (`list_archive`, in a worker when the page opens): its pictures (`is_candidate_image`: no folders, dotfiles, or `__MACOSX`) and the archives inside it, in natural order of their paths, at most `MAX_LISTED` (1,000), then "and N more" (decided while building, so a huge archive can't flood the page). A picture over the member budget, encrypted, or too deep in a solid archive (`SOLID_READ_BUDGET` before it) is listed without a thumbnail.
  - **An archive inside an archive** is one tile with the archive icon (decided while building: its "best picture" would mean opening it, which level 1 doesn't); opening it extracts it and hands it to its program like any member.
  - **Shown as the archive itself** (one tile, its file menu), with a line saying why: a rar archive with no tool installed (as before), one that can't be read ("Couldn't read this archive: …", and reported in the Activity panel), one with no pictures, or one offline or missing.
  - **Thumbnails** (`ThumbnailResolver.archive_thumbnails`): one worker job per archive, reading only the uncached members; a member that can't be drawn is remembered for the session and reported, the others still shown. Cache key: `thumb_key(archive id, its size, its time, "archive-member:<path>", 1, size)`.
  - **Opening** (double-click, Enter, or **Open** on its menu; `extract_member`, in a worker): the member is copied to `<session temp folder>/archives/<archive id>/<its path>` (each part cleaned of characters Windows refuses, `..` dropped) and opened with its program; members up to 256 MB. The temporary folder goes when the keep closes. **Show the archive in file manager** is the menu's other entry.
  - **Tiles** are one size (a grid cell; a thumbnail centered on a square), and each group's list is as tall as its rows, so the page scrolls rather than each group.
- **The built-in themes' galleries** (M28, built in #389): a Movie's **Screenshots** (`Screenshots`, `Screens`, `Stills`; the role keeps its name `screenshot`, so the theme's own screenshot links stay); an Album's **Scans** (`Scans`, `Artwork`, `Covers`, `Booklet`); an assets Artist's **Previews** (`Previews`, `Preview`, `Samples`); and, from the notes folder, an Actor's and a music Artist's **Photos** and a Book's **Covers**. Each takes pictures and archives; the movies and music themes now scan archives too (they ignore them otherwise), and an Actor's and a music Artist's thumbnails try their first photo. Decided while building: a folder several items of a type share (loose movies side by side) fills none of them (`gallery_fill._folders`); a type's page without a `DetailView` lists its gallery roles (`default_detail_view`); a gallery with no files isn't shown on the page; and gallery roles aren't an item's files to open (`file_kind`), so a music artist still has nothing to open.
- **Filling from folders, as built** (#387, `core/gallery_fill.py`): after a root's files are ingested (a scan, or Re-read from file), `fill_galleries` finds each item of a type with such a role whose primary file is in the root, takes its folder, and lists the files under it (a range query on the path). A file belongs when the first part of its path under the item's folder is one of the names (case aside; any depth below it), or when it sits in the item's folder named like one with an archive extension; it must be of a kind the role takes (pictures; archives only with the `archive` kind), not missing, and not skipped. These links are marked `by_folder` (core format 21): each fill adds the new ones and removes those no longer wanted. The theme's own links in the role and the user's are never removed, and a file already linked in the role isn't linked again; a theme linking a filled file makes the link its own (`by_folder` cleared), and a filled link the user also made stays theirs when the folder stops naming it. Themes don't see filled links from the file's side (`entities_of`), and undo's snapshots keep the flag. Changed items choose their thumbnail afresh. An item at the top of a root (a file there) uses the root's top as its folder.
- **The item's own folder** (#448, M32; decided in discussion, 2026-10-16; API version 19): `role(..., gallery=True, own_folder=True)` is filled by the core (`fill_galleries`, the same pass as named folders) with every picture and archive the role takes in the item's own folder, at any depth. Its own folder is the folder of its first primary file, when no other item of the type has a primary file in it or below it (counted in one pass over each item's files' folders and those above them), and never the top of a root. Not added (decided while building): files the item has in its other roles (a cover), a folder's image (`folder.jpg`, `FOLDER_IMAGE_NAMES`, #458), and another item's own (primary) file, such as a comic archive beside a book, unless that item's type is one the theme's folder items are made for (`folders_for`: pictures and archives of their own, which stay in the gallery). The loader refuses `own_folder` without `gallery=True`, with `folders`, or below `api_version = 19`. Built in: a Book's **Pictures** (after its notes-folder **Covers**, which stays its first gallery role). Found while building: a fill now looks at every item of the type for links to take back, so an item whose folder stopped being its own (or became shared) loses what was filled; it looked only at items still qualifying before.
- **Archive members as items** (level 2): read as resources when the user chooses (§6 *Archive members*, #391).

### Folder items (M32, #447; decided in discussion, 2026-10-16)

Folders of pictures as items that hold what's in them, so a tag on a folder counts for everything inside: `Home Movies/Alice/` with her videos and photos, `Alice/3rd Birthday/`, a story's folder with its renders.

- **Declared** by one entity type per theme (API version 18): `folders_for = (Picture, Archive)` makes it the theme's folder items. The loader checks there is at most one, that it has a primary role of kind `dir`, that the types it names are declared, that the theme scans folders (`dirs = True`), and `api_version = 18`.
- **Made by the core** (chosen in discussion over the theme's own ingest, which would depend on the order files are read: a movie read before the first picture beside it would stay outside its folder until read again). `core/folder_items.py`, `fill_folders`, runs in the writer after each root's files are ingested, as the galleries' fill does, and works from what the keep holds, so it is order-independent and a picture added or removed later updates the folders at the next scan.
- **Which folders** (decided in discussion: every folder above a picture): the folder each file of a `folders_for` type is in, and every folder above it, up to the top of the root (never a folder item). Not a folder that is already another item's primary folder (a movies Collection's): that item stands in for it. A movie's own folder isn't its primary folder (a movie's primary role is its videos), so a movie's folder with pictures is a folder item holding the movie and its pictures (asked in discussion: tag `3rd Birthday` and the movie and its photos count); so is a `Screenshots` folder with pictures (asked: treated like any other). A folder item no longer wanted is deleted, unless the user tagged it (then it stays while its folder exists, holding what is under it). Keyed `folder:<root id>:<path>`, titled by the folder's name, its folder linked in its primary role.
- **What each holds:** every item of a type the theme lets it contain (`contains(Folder, Movie)`…) whose primary files are in it or below it, with no nearer folder item between; an item with files in several places can be in several. A folder item is held by the nearest folder item above it. The walk up stops at a collection's folder for the items that collection's type holds (its movies stay the collection's, not also the folder above's); the collection itself, and a picture beside it, which a collection doesn't hold, go to the folder item above. Containment goes through the ingest session (`contain`, `uncontain`), so what the user put in or took out by hand (`user_contains`) stays, and closure and the search index are kept in step.
- **Notes:** a folder item's primary file is its folder, so it takes the note inside it like any such item (§9 *Notes*): the title renames it, tags tag it (and, through inherit tags, what it holds), other keys are Extra fields. In a movie's own folder the movie takes the same note (`ctx.note`), so both are described by it (kept shared, decided in discussion, 2026-10-16: its tags then reach the movie and, through the folder, its pictures); the movie keeps its title, since only an item that is the folder takes a note's title.
- **What its note says it belongs to** (#449): a folder item's fields that mirror a containment it is the child of (`people: str | None = field("People", mirrors=Person, many=True)` with `contains(Person, Folder)`) are kept as links by the core: `link_folder_fields`, after a root's notes are read, finds or makes each item the field names (`Theme.item_named`) and puts the folder in it, and takes it out of those of that type no longer named; through the ingest session, so what the user did by hand stays. Notes elsewhere still only fill fields (a book's note naming a series doesn't move the book: the theme's own reading of files decides that).
- **Built in:** the movies and books themes' `Folder` (`folders_for = (Picture, Archive)`), holding folders, pictures, archives, and their works (movies and collections; books and comics), with a **Folders** view; their Pictures, Movies, Books, and Comics views inherit tags, so a tag on a folder finds what's under it. A folder's thumbnail is its folder picture, else one of the first things in it.

### Dashboard cards

A theme adds cards to the dashboard (§12) in two ways (#114, `themes/api.py`, `core/dashboard.py`):

- **Declared**, in `Theme.dashboard`, computed by the core in SQL (fast; no theme code runs):
  - `stat(title, Type, field, how="sum")` gives one number over the type's items: `"sum"`, `"avg"`, `"min"`, `"max"`, or `"count"` (items with a value). It is shown in the field's display format: `stat("Total running time", Song, "duration")` reads `1:02:33`. Other numbers read `1,234`, or with one decimal.
  - `top_values(title, Type, field, limit=5)` gives the field's most common values with their counts, most first. A value links to Search all narrowed to the type, filtered by how the field is searched: a choice filter, an exact range for a range field, "starts with" for text. A field that isn't searchable shows its values without links.
  - The loader checks the type is declared, the field exists, and a sum, average, minimum, or maximum is over a number field.
- **Methods**, marked `@dashboard_card(title, description="")`, for anything the declared kinds can't express:
  - Called as `method(ctx)` with a read-only `DashboardContext`: `count(Type, **equals)`, `find(Type, **equals)`, `get(entity)`, and `stat(Type, field, how)`.
  - Returns `(label, value)` rows or a text.
  - They run in the dashboard's worker on every read, so they should be quick.
  - A method that raises shows the error on its card ("Couldn't work this out: …"), and the rest of the dashboard is unaffected.

Declared cards come first, then method cards in declaration order. A card's `description` is its tooltip. The built-in themes' cards:

- **music:** Total running time and Average song (from `duration`), Top genres, and Top years (albums).
- **assets2d:** Space used by images, Image types (by extension), and the method card **Biggest artists** (assets per artist).

### Theme schema versions

On opening a keep, the core compares the stored theme version with the loaded theme's version: equal → open; older stored → run `migrate()` after backing up `keep.db`; newer stored → refuse to open with a clear message.

Details (`core/theme_db.py`, `open_theme()`, run after the core schema is open):

- The theme version is kept in `keep.toml` (`[theme] version`) and in `schema_version` (`component` = theme id), like the core version (§5). A newer version in either is refused before anything changes; an older stored version raises the same "needs migration" error as the core (with `component` set to the theme id) so the launcher can ask. Once confirmed, `keep.db` is backed up (`keep.db.<theme>-v<old>-<timestamp>.bak`), and in one transaction `theme().migrate_schema(old_version, ops)` runs, the additive changes are applied, items of types whose fields were retyped or dropped are re-indexed for text search, `theme().migrate(old_version, ctx)` runs, and the version is recorded; `keep.toml` is updated afterwards. A failure rolls everything back, DDL included, and the backup is kept; a schema change that fails says so, naming the backup.
- **Additive changes are the core's job**, on every open: missing tables, columns, and indexes are created (new non-nullable columns use the defaults from §5, which `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` requires). So a theme that only adds entity types or fields needs no migration code, and `Theme.migrate()` defaults to doing nothing; it exists for data changes. A field the theme stops declaring is left in place (harmless) unless the theme drops it.
- **Renaming, retyping, and dropping fields are explicit** (#173), in `Theme.migrate_schema(from_version, ops)`, so the core never guesses that a missing field was renamed. Themes still never see SQL: `ops` (`SchemaOps`, implemented by `core/theme_migrate.py`) checks each request against the theme's declarations and the keep's tables, then:
  - `rename_field(Entity, old, new)`: `new` must be declared and `old` not. `ALTER TABLE … RENAME COLUMN`; the old column's indexes are dropped, and the additive step makes the declared ones under their new names. Field provenance moves to the new name, so user edits stay protected. Saved searches that can show that type (scoped to it, or to every type) have their filters and sorts on `old` renamed.
  - `change_type(Entity, name, convert=None)`: to the declared type, by copy and swap: a new table, the rows copied with the field converted, the old table dropped, the new one renamed, and its indexes made again. The `id` foreign key to `entity.id` is kept. The default conversion takes only what clearly converts: numeric text and whole floats to `int`, numbers to and from text, yes/no words and 0/1 to `bool`, and ISO text to dates and UTC datetimes. Blank text becomes `None` (or the type's default for a non-nullable field). `convert` replaces it, receiving SQLite's stored value. A value that doesn't convert fails the upgrade with the item id and value.
  - `drop_field(Entity, name)`: `name` must no longer be declared. Copy and swap without the column, and its provenance rows are deleted. Saved searches still naming it report an unknown field when opened, as before.
  - Each skips silently what the keep doesn't have (a field it never had, a type whose table the additive step will create), so `if from_version < N:` blocks work for keeps any number of versions behind. A field renamed onto one the keep already has is an error.
  - A theme defining `migrate_schema` must set `api_version = 2` explicitly (a validation problem otherwise), so a Tagalot with API version 1 refuses it rather than open the keep and skip its schema changes. Themes needing API version 1 use the interim pattern: keep the old field declared, hidden, for one version, and copy it in `migrate()`. The theme guide (docs/THEMES.md, "Changing a theme people already use") shows each case.

### Built-in themes

1. **generic** — one entity per file, no containment. Reference implementation and fallback; lets the core be built and tested before any rich theme exists. A `File` entity is titled with the file name and has `extension` (choice), `folder` (text), `size` and `modified` (range); its primary `file` role accepts any kind, and double-click opens the file. Views: "Files" and "Recently modified" (list layout). Ingest updates the entity already linked to a file, or creates one with a never-reused key.
2. **assets2d** — Artist ⊃ Image, Font, Archive (#81). Asset kinds are **separate entity types**, so each has its own fields and views can be scoped by type:
   - **Image** (every image extension, PSD included): `width`, `height` (range) and `dimensions` ("1920 × 1080", its card line). **Font** (ttf, otf, woff, woff2): `family`, `style` (choice; card lines). **Archive** (zip, 7z, rar, cbz, cbr, cb7): `images` (how many images it holds). All three share `artist` (choice), `extension`, `folder`, `size`, and `modified`; the title is the file name, and double-click opens the file.
   - One entity per file with a never-reused key, as in the generic theme, so tags follow a moved file. The **artist** is the folder at the `artist_level` option under the root (default 1, the first folder; a keep or a root can set 2 for `Store/<Artist>/…`); files above that level have no artist. Every folder is a resource (`dirs = True`), and ingest links only those at the level as artists' folders, so a changed level needs no rescan of the file system. After a level change the root is ingested again: assets move to their new artists, folders no longer at the level stop being artists' folders, and an artist left with no assets and no folder is deleted (its tags with it). Artists are keyed by folder name (case-insensitive), so the same artist's folders in two roots are one artist. Artists' folders are their primary `folder` role. A file moved to another artist's folder moves to that artist.
   - File details are read in `prepare()` with Pillow (the image header only; the font's names), `zipfile`, `py7zr`, and `rarfile` (the listing, not the contents). A file whose details can't be read is still an asset, with a warning.
   - Thumbnails: images and archives as usual; fonts by the core's **font renderer**, which draws "Aa" and "Quick fox" in the font; artists by their `folder.jpg` (`FolderImage`), else one of their first five assets (a theme provider, `ContentsThumbnail`). `thumbnail_max` 1024, `thumbnail_default` 256.
   - Views (#83), all grids: **Assets** (all three types), **Artists**, **Images**, **Fonts** (sorted by family, then style), and **Archives**. The asset views turn on `inherit_tags`, so a tag on an artist counts as its assets' own: tagging a whole artist "Pixel art" is one step, and Images with Pixel art lists that artist's images.
3. **music** — Artist ⊃ Album ⊃ Song, and Artist ⊃ Song (#98, #99).
   - **Albums are folders:** a folder with audio in it (directly, or in disc subfolders) is an album, keyed by its path and linked as its primary `folder` role. Folders named `CD1`, `Disc 2`, `Disk-3` belong to their parent's album and give their songs a disc number when the tags have none. An album is titled from its songs' `ALBUM` tag, else its folder name, and takes `year` and `genre` from them; `artist` and `folder` are not editable.
   - **Songs** are keyed by album folder, disc, track, and title (case and punctuation ignored), so `01 So What.flac` and `01 So What.mp3` are one song whose files are its versions (role `audio`, many). A song already linked to a file is updated in place, so tags and moves follow it. Fields: `track`, `disc`, `artist`, `album_artist`, `album`, `year`, `genre`, `duration` (seconds, shown as `3:25`), `bitrate` (kbps, the best of its versions; theme version 2, #253), `folder`. Double-click opens the file.
   - **Artists:** an album's artist is its songs' `ALBUMARTIST` tag, else their `ARTIST`, worked out again from all its songs whenever one changes; when they disagree (a compilation) it is **Various Artists**. A song by someone other than its album's artist also sits under its own artist, and a song with no album folder (directly in a root) under its artist only. Artists are keyed by normalized name. An album or artist left with nothing inside is deleted.
   - Thumbnails (#100): a **song** shows its embedded art, else its album's thumbnail (`AlbumThumbnail`, only the album parent, not an artist it also sits under), else the audio icon; an **album** its folder image (`folder.jpg`, `cover.jpg`…, in the album folder itself, not a disc folder), else its first songs' embedded art, else the folder icon; an **artist** the thumbnail of one of its first five albums or songs (`ContentsThumbnail`), else the generic icon.
   - Views (#101): **Browse** (albums and songs, Inherit tags and Contained on, starting as a tree sorted by artist, year, title: albums at the top with their songs under them), **Artists** (grid), **Albums** (grid, by artist, year, title), and **Songs** (list, by artist, album, disc, track, title); the album and song views inherit tags, so tagging an album tags its songs for searches. An album orders its songs by disc, track, then title (`contents_sort`), in the tree and on its page; an artist its albums and songs by year, then title.
   - **Opening** (#102): double-click or Enter on a song plays its first version that's on this computer (`double_click = "open_file"`); Ctrl+Enter opens its page. **Play album** (an action on albums: a page button and a menu entry) writes the selected albums' songs in disc and track order to an extended `.m3u8` playlist (UTF-8, `#EXTINF` with length and "artist - title") in the session's temp folder, named after the album (or "N albums"), with a new name if one exists (a player may still hold the last one), and opens it with the program for playlists. Songs with no file on this computer are left out, and the status bar says how many; with none, nothing opens. It changes no data, so there's nothing to undo.
