An SVG drawn with `currentColor` follows the palette like Lucide's; a PNG shows as it is.
  - **Pictures are copied into the keep folder** (`icons/`, `core/icons.copy_icon_picture`, in a worker), so the keep shows them from another computer or a share; nothing is written under a root. The same picture is copied once; another with the same name gets a number, so a file is never replaced and an icon never changes under a name. A picture that's gone shows the standard icon again.
  - **A type's icon:** right-click its search under SEARCHES → **Change icon…** / **Use the theme's icon**. Saved per keep in `keep.toml`, `[appearance.icons]` (type id = a Lucide name or `icons/…`), so a books keep and a comics keep can differ; it shows everywhere the type's icon does (its searches, its tabs and pages, placeholders: the resolver asks the session, `KeepSession.type_icon`, which caches what it found until `keep.toml` changes).
  - **The keep's icon:** **Icon** in Keep configuration's Keep tab (**Change…**, **Use the theme's**), `[appearance] icon`; the theme's `Theme.icon` otherwise. The Open Keep dialog shows it (#559).
  - **A saved search's icon:** right-click it → **Change icon…** / **Use the standard icon**, stored in its definition (§8); one undo step. Without one it shows the standard `bookmark`.
- **Built-in themes:** Movie `film`, Person and Author `user`, Book `book`, Comic `book-open`, Album `disc`, Song `music`, music's Artist `mic-vocal`, Picture and 2D assets' Image `image`, Font `type`, Archive `archive`, Folder `folder`, Paper `file-text`, and so on (picked while building); keeps: movies `clapperboard`, music `headphones`, books `library`, research `graduation-cap`, 2D assets `shapes`, Files `folder`. The keep's icon shows in the Open Keep dialog (#559).

### Colors (M43, #558, #572; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27 and 2026-10-28)

Each keep can have its own look, so a books keep is told apart from a music or movies keep at a glance.

- **Keep configuration → Appearance → Colors:** **Follow the system** (the default: its light or dark mode), **Light**, **Dark**, or a **color scheme** (#572): Tagalot's, the keep's theme's, or the user's own. The Appearance tab also holds the keep's **Icon** (#557), moved there from the Keep tab.
- **Color schemes** (#572, `core/color_schemes.py`; decided in discussion, 2026-10-28): a named set of the five colors below, one set each (a scheme is simply light or dark).
  - **Built in:** Paper, Parchment, Solarized Light, Slate, Nord, Solarized Dark (picked while building; each reads at 4.5:1 or more).
  - **A theme's:** `Theme.color_schemes` (theme API version 26, §9), a list of `ColorScheme(id, name, background, text, panels, accent, dimmed)`, offered only in that theme's keeps and named `"<theme id>.<id>"`. The loader refuses an id that isn't lowercase letters, digits, dashes, or underscores, one declared twice, and a color that isn't `#rrggbb`; `--check-theme` warns about text that is hard to read. No default scheme for new keeps (decided in discussion: a keep starts with the system's).
  - **The user's own:** one TOML file a scheme in the per-user data folder's `color_schemes` (beside `themes`), with `name` and the five colors, hand-editable, offered in every keep and named `"user:<file name>"`. Read once and kept (decided while building: a window becoming active never reads a file); saving updates them, and a file edited by hand is read at the next start. A file that can't be read is left out, with a warning in the log.
  - **Editing:** the five color buttons change the chosen scheme, shown at once (a preview, not saved). **Save** writes the changes, only to the user's own; **Save As…** asks for a name and writes a new scheme of the user's, which the keep then uses (refused when the user already has one by that name). Choosing another scheme, or closing the window, with changes unsaved asks whether to discard them (decided while building: asked only when the user closes the window; a keep closing drops them), so a keep always names a scheme.
  - **The colors #558 wrote** in `keep.toml` (`colors = "custom"` with the five) still show, listed as **This keep's colors** until another choice replaces them; Save As keeps them as a scheme. A keep naming a scheme that's gone shows the system's colors, with a warning in the log, and the list names it as not found.
  - **The session finds the colors** a keep names (`resolve_colors`, at open and when `keep.toml` changes: `KeepSession.colors`), so the window applies them without reading anything.
- **Light and dark keep the native style** (Windows 11's), asked for that scheme with `QStyleHints.setColorScheme`; the system's choice comes back with `Unknown` and a default palette (`QApplication.setPalette(QPalette())`; found while building: `style().standardPalette()` sticks to a grey palette).
- **A scheme's colors** (`ui/appearance.py`): five, `background`, `text`, `panels` (lists and panels), `accent` (selection and links), and `dimmed` (secondary text). A whole palette is derived (`custom_palette`): alternate rows, buttons, bevels, and disabled text are mixes of these; text on the accent is black or white, whichever contrasts more; links take the accent unless it reads poorly on the panels (below 3:1), then move toward the text. They use **Qt's Fusion style**, since the native Windows 11 style ignores much of a palette; the color scheme is set to match the background, and the system's file dialogs and the title bar keep the system's look. A warning shows when text on the background or on the panels contrasts below 4.5:1 (WCAG AA).
- **Saved per keep** in `keep.toml` (`[appearance] colors`, naming the look or the scheme; §4).
- **The application's, not a window's** (decided while building: Qt's palette and style are the application's, and several keeps can be open): the look follows the active keep's window, applied when it opens or becomes active and when its colors change (`MainWindow.apply_colors`); applying the same look again does nothing. The launcher alone has the system's. Changes show at once, before they are saved.
- **Following a change** (found while building): style sheets naming palette colors (`palette(placeholder-text)`) keep the colors they were set with, so every widget's style sheet is set again after a change; the results lists' proxy style (`NoHoverFill`, #473) is made again on the new style (`hover.restyle_views`), and the hover outline takes the new selection color.
- **Named colors** (`ui/colors.py`): what the palette has no role for, errors, warnings and drop outlines, a rating's stars, a difference between compared items, each with a light-background and a dark-background variant chosen from the window's color; dimmed rich text uses the palette's placeholder color instead of `gray`. The rendered Markdown (#553) takes its colors from the palette already.

### Fonts (M44, #565; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27)

Each keep can have its own fonts and text size, so a books keep reads like a book.

- **Three places** (`FONT_PLACES`), each with its own font or the system's:
  - **Titles:** an item's page title, the preview's title, grid cards' titles, the navigation's section headings, and title cards (#415).
  - **Notes:** rendered Markdown notes and glimpses on items' pages (`ui/note_view.py`; the document's default font, made again when the fonts change).
  - **Text:** the rest of the window: the application's font.
- **Text size:** a percent of the system's (50 to 200, `TEXT_SIZES`), applied to the application's font; titles and notes follow it, as they are sized from it.
- **Fonts from** an installed family (a picker with a filter and a preview of the keep's name and first titles, `ui/font_picker.py`), or **From a file…**: a TTF or OTF copied into the keep folder's `fonts/` (`core/fonts.copy_font_file`, in a worker; up to 32 MB), so the keep looks the same from another computer or a share. As with `icons/`, a file is copied once and never replaced; another with the same name gets a number. Nothing is written under a root.
- **Saved per keep** in `keep.toml` `[appearance.fonts]` (§4): `titles`, `notes`, `text` (a family, or `fonts/…`), and `text_size` (left out at 100).
- **A font that isn't there** (a family not installed on this computer, or a file that's gone or Qt can't read) shows the system's font, with a note in Keep configuration; never an error.
- **Google Fonts** (#566, decided in discussion, 2026-10-27): the picker's **Google Fonts** tab searches the bundled list (§3) offline, by name and category (serif, sans serif, display, handwriting, monospace), most used first. A family's preview needs the font, so until it's downloaded the preview is in a font of its kind on this computer, and says so.
  - **Downloaded only when asked:** **Download** fetches the family's files (its variable fonts, upright and italic; else its regular, bold, italic, and bold italic) and its license file from `raw.githubusercontent.com` (`core/google_fonts.py`, which refuses any other host), in a worker, with progress; the tab names the host. Nothing goes online by itself. The fonts' licenses (SIL Open Font License, Apache, Ubuntu Font License) allow copying them into the keep.
  - **In the keep folder:** `fonts/<family>/`, the files named without a variable font's axes (`Literata.ttf`, `Literata-Italic.ttf`, `OFL.txt`), never replaced; `keep.toml` names the first (`fonts/Literata/Literata.ttf`), so a keep opened elsewhere needs no download. A font in a family's folder brings the folder's other font files with it, so bold and italic are the family's own (decided while building). A family already in the keep is offered as **Use** rather than **Download**.
  - **Offline or refused:** the tab says what went wrong and offers the installed fonts and **From a file…** instead.
  - Found while building: Qt names a variable font's family after its default instance where the font does (Literata's is "Literata 12pt"); that is the name shown.
- **Keep configuration → Appearance → Fonts** (decided while building: on the Appearance tab, which M43 added after this was planned for the Keep tab): a button per place showing its font in itself, **Use the system's**, and the text size. Changes apply at once.
- **Themes suggest fonts** (#567, M44; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27; theme API version 27, §9): `Theme.fonts`, a fallback chain per place (`{"titles": ("Literata", "Georgia", "serif")}`), and `Theme.text_size` (percent). The keep's own choice always wins; without one, the first of the chain that is here shows:
  - a family downloaded into the keep (a Google Font, in `fonts/<family>/`; matched by its folder, since Qt may name a family otherwise),
  - a TTF or OTF beside the theme's module (an entry that's a path, `fonts/Wand.ttf`, never outside its folder),
  - an installed family,
  - or a generic family (`serif`, `sans-serif`, `monospace`, `cursive`, `fantasy`), which ends a chain with the system's font of that kind.
  - The session reads the keep's downloaded families and the theme's files a chain names (`core/fonts.theme_font_files`), with the keep's own fonts; `ui/fonts.look_of` resolves each place (`PlaceFont`: its family, and whether it's the keep's, the theme's, or the system's).
  - **A theme never forces a download:** a Google Font in its chain that isn't here shows the next one, and Keep configuration says which aren't on this computer and offers to download the first Google Font among them into the keep (**Download Literata**, as #566; `keep.toml` is unchanged, as the suggestion now finds it).
  - **Keep configuration** shows a place's font as "Literata (the theme's)", with **Use the theme's** (back to the suggestion) and **Use the system's**, which over a suggestion writes `titles = "system"` (decided while building: the system's font must be a choice of its own, or the suggestion would come back). The text size shows the theme's until the keep's own is set; setting it back to the theme's removes the keep's (decided while building: so 100% over a theme's 105% is kept as a choice).
  - The loader refuses `fonts` that aren't tuples of names by place, and a `text_size` outside 50 to 200; `--check-theme` warns about places it doesn't know and font files that aren't beside the theme.
  - **Built-in themes' suggestions** (picked in discussion, then checked for readability while building with each demo keep in light and dark, its Google Fonts downloaded): books titles Literata, Merriweather, Georgia, serif and notes Literata, Georgia, serif, text size 105%; movies titles Oswald, Arial Narrow, sans-serif (decided while building: Bebas Neue, the discussion's pick, is all capitals and read poorly in grid titles and the navigation's small headings); music titles Nunito, Poppins, Segoe UI, sans-serif; research titles and notes Source Serif 4, Georgia, serif; 2D assets titles Inter, Segoe UI, sans-serif; Files none. Found in the check: the preview showed its hint ("Select an item…") in the titles font; hints now use the text's, and only an item's title the titles'.
- **The application's, as colors are** (`ui/fonts.py`): the fonts follow the active keep's window, applied with its colors (`MainWindow.apply_colors`); the launcher has the system's. The session reads the keep's font files at open and when `keep.toml` changes (`KeepSession.font_files`); the UI registers them with Qt (`QFontDatabase.addApplicationFontFromData`, once each). Found while building: Qt passes a new application font's family to widgets with a font of their own, but not its size, so widgets sized relative to the text (titles, headings) set their font again on every change (`fonts.follow`), and lists and grids are laid out again with title cards painted again.

### Fields of your own (M45, #581; decided in discussion, 2026-10-28)

A keep's own fields (§4 `keep.toml`, §5 `entity_field`) are made and changed in Keep configuration's **Fields** tab (`ui/own_fields.py`): a list grouped by type, with **Add field…**, **Change…** (double-click too), and **Remove…**. The dialog asks for the type, label, kind, how it is searched and shown, and whether it is on cards and in lists, and says why a field can't be added as the user types (OK stays off until then); a field's type and kind can't change once made. **Remove…** counts the items with a value and asks first; their values become Extra fields, and Edit → Undo brings the field back (#582). On an item's page the fields follow the theme's in Details and edit in place like them, each edit one undo step (`TagService.edit_field` with `own:<name>`). Front matter, Write to file…, and Save as note… come with #584.

**In lists and searches** (#583): an own field is a list column after the theme's (`list_columns`), shown by default when it says `column`, numeric for numbers, in its display format; it sorts. With `card`, it is a grid card line after the theme's `card_lines` (when the user hasn't chosen lines) and a fact in the preview strip; any own field can be picked as a card line. **+ Field** offers those whose search isn't `none`, with chips as for a theme's. Links show as theme `url` fields do.

Decided while building: text values are always in the search box's index (`search` picks the filter, #583), so a field's search can change without reindexing; the tab is a module of its own beside `keep_config.py`, with its own error line.

**Promoting an Extra field** (#582; decided in discussion, 2026-10-28; `core/promote.py`, `ui/own_fields.PromoteDialog`):

- **Make it a field…** is on every Extra field's arrow menu on an item's page. It reads every item's Extra field of that key, case aside (`key_values`, in a worker), then asks as **Add field…** does, plus the types: each type where the key appears, with its count, the item's own ticked. The label starts as the key, capitalized; the kind as the first of integer, number, date, yes/no, and link that every value reads as, else text.
- **The preview** says how many items have the key and how many of their values read as the kind ("212 items: 205 read as whole numbers and move; 7 don't, and stay Extra fields (below), for you to fix."), listing those that don't (the first 200).
- **Reading values** (`parse_as`): whole numbers may group thousands with commas (`1,995`); numbers are decimal; dates are `YYYY-MM-DD`; yes/no is yes, no, true, false, y, n, 1, or 0; a link is an `http` or `https` address; text is any.
- **Promoting** makes the field for each chosen type (the same name, label, and kind), moves each value that reads as the kind out of `extra` into it, and keeps its provenance: `extra:<key>`'s source becomes `own:<name>`'s (a note's stays `note`, a file's `extracted`, an edit `user`); an Extra field the user typed (no provenance) becomes `user`. Values that don't fit stay Extra fields.
- **Turn back into an Extra field** is on the arrow beside an own field's value on the page, and is Keep configuration's Remove too: values become text Extra fields named by the label, a note's or file's keeping that provenance, the user's becoming plain Extra fields again.
- **Undo:** each is one step (`FieldMove`): whole-item snapshots before and after (which carry Extra fields, own values, and provenance), and `keep.toml`'s fields before and after, which the session writes back as it is undone and redone (`TagService.on_own_fields`, as `on_note_key` does for ignored keys). Since removing a field is now a step, the edits before it stay in the history (#581 dropped them).
- **Notes and Markdown files** fill own fields since #584 (§9 *Notes*), so a note read again after its key was promoted fills the field rather than giving the key back as an Extra field.

### Ratings for any type (M46, #577; decided in discussion, 2026-10-28)

Any type can be rated, as a field of the user's own of kind `rating`: a number from 0 to 5 in half stars (REAL in `entity_field`), searched by range, shown as five stars (its `display` is `stars/5`, as a theme's `rating=5`, #419), so the page's clickable stars, the lists', cards', and preview's `★★★½☆` with the number in a cell's tooltip, chips, sorting, filters, and front matter's `rating:` all come from the theme ratings' code and M45 (#583, #584).

- **Turning it on:** the type's menu in the navigation (right-click a view, beside its icon, #557) has a checkable **Rate …** (**Rate Collections**); on, it adds `[fields."<type>".rating]` with label Rating, card and column on (`KeepSession.rate_type`). Keep configuration's Fields tab offers **Rating** as a kind too.
- **One rating an item:** a type whose theme declares a rating field isn't offered one (the menu entry isn't there; adding one is refused), and a type has at most one of the user's. The movies theme's 10-point scale stays its own.
- **Turning it off** asks first, then turns the field back into Extra fields (`3.5`), one undo step (#582).
- **Values:** an edit, a note's or file's value, or a promoted Extra field must be from 0 to 5 in half stars (`rating_problem`); one that isn't stays an Extra field, as for any kind (#584). Decided while building: the field is named `rating` (else a free name) and labelled Rating (Stars when a field of the type is labelled Rating already).

### Navigation model

- **Select** (single click): updates the tagging panel, the Details tab, and an optional preview (thumbnail + key fields).
- **Preview** (#90, `ui/preview.py`): **under the navigation pane** (#493, M35; decided in discussion, 2026-10-20: chosen over below Tags and Details, or the top of the Details tab, as it stays visible while tagging and the navigation is mostly empty), one for the window, so the results get its full height; it was a strip under every search's results. It follows the current page's selection, like the Tags checks and the Details tab (views, Search all, and a detail page's contents; on a page with nothing selected in its contents, the page's own item). For one selected item, top to bottom: its thumbnail (160 px high, as wide as the column), the title and type, its card fields joined with " · ", its primary file as `root name › relative path` (elided in the middle), and the start of its type's description (`description_field`, #414; about six short lines, the tooltip has more) (`core.detail.load_preview`, in a worker). For several: "N items selected", then their titles, one a line, in the selection's order (the first 25, then "and N more"; #497, asked for in discussion, `core.detail.load_titles` in a worker); for none, a hint, at the top (the picture box hides). Double-click opens the item's page. It sits in a vertical splitter with the navigation: 320 px high at first, or what the user dragged it to (`preview_height` in `ui_state.json`). **View → Preview** shows or hides it, remembered per keep (`preview`, on by default).
- **Open** (double-click/Enter): the entity's detail page, or its file for types that open files (Gestures, above); the menu's **Open page** always opens the page.
- **Drill down** ("Show contents in search"): adds a removable `Within: <entity>` chip to the current search, keeping current tag filters.
- **Back/forward** history like a browser (Alt+←/→, mouse buttons).
- **Breadcrumbs** from the containment hierarchy (Artist › Album › Song); each crumb opens that container's page.

### Views

**Keep launcher.** Recent keeps, create keep (name, location, theme, first root), open folder. Shows theme problems: theme missing, stored version newer or older (offer migration with backup). It is a separate start dialog, shown when Tagalot starts without a keep and from Keep → Open another keep… (Ctrl+O); each keep opens in its own main window, and closing that window closes the keep (in a worker, since it waits for queued writes), so it can be reopened, moved, or deleted while Tagalot keeps running.

- **Recent keeps** show name, theme, and path, read in a worker (keeps may be on slow drives), each with its **icon and a hint of its colors** (#559, M43; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27): its own icon (`[appearance] icon`, a picture in the keep folder found in the same worker), else its theme's (`Theme.icon`, once the themes have loaded: the rows are shown again then, with the themes' names too), else a generic `box`. A keep following the system's colors shows its icon plainly; one that is always light or dark shows it on a light or dark rounded square, and one with a color scheme (#572) on a square of its background, drawn in its text color, with a stripe of its accent (`launcher.keep_badge`; decided while building: a badge rather than a separate swatch, so the list stays one line a keep). An unreadable keep shows the generic icon. **Each keep's name is in its titles font** (#568, M44; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27): its own (`[appearance.fonts] titles`), else its theme's suggestion once the themes have loaded (§12 *Fonts*), else the window's, two points larger than the row's theme and path, which stay in the window's font, dimmed (`launcher._RecentDelegate`). The worker that reads `keep.toml` also reads the font files the keep names and every family downloaded into it (`core/fonts.downloaded_families`), once a run per keep; a keep whose files can't be read shows its name in the window's font. Decided while building: a theme's suggestion of a file beside the theme isn't read for the launcher (its chain goes on to the next font), since the themes load in their own worker. unreadable ones are greyed as "not found" and can be removed from the list (the keep itself is untouched). Double-click or Open opens one.
- **New keep…:** name, location (the keep becomes `<location>/<name>.keep`, previewed as you type; it starts as the folder last chosen in a folder picker, §4), theme, and the folder to watch; Create is enabled once the form is valid. The folder is stored exactly as typed (a UNC share such as `\\nas\music` keeps its form), and the root's name and id come from its last segment (`"My Photos"` → id `my-photos`). The keep is created in a worker and then opened.
- A "⚠ N theme files have problems (details)" line appears when any theme failed to load.

**Keep configuration.** Roots: add, remove, rename, change path, per-machine override, exclude patterns, status (online, item count, last scan, last error), "Scan now". Also thumbnail size, clear thumbnail cache, theme info.

- **Roots** (#109, `ui/keep_config.py`, `core/root_admin.py`): **Keep → Configure keep…** opens a window of its own (one per main window). The roots are listed with their state (online, offline, not watched, not scanned yet). The selected root shows:
  - **Name** and **Folder**.
  - **On this computer:** the per-user override (§4), with Browse and Clear.
  - **Skip:** the exclude patterns, one per line, each optionally followed by ` # ` and a note saying why (#334); a line starting with `#` is a comment, not saved.
  - **Read inside archives** (#519): the root's `archives` patterns (§6 *Archive members*), one per line, saved when the box loses the focus; a change offers a scan.
  - **Status:** state, files and folders known (and how many are offline or missing), last scan, and last error.
  - Buttons: **Scan now** (that root only), **Stop watching** / **Watch again**, and **Remove…**.
- **Saving:** every change is checked and saved at once, in a worker (a **Scan now** clicked while a save is in progress, as when the click itself ends an edit, waits for it, so the scan uses the saved settings): `keep.toml` (atomically), or `settings.toml` for this computer's folder. A refused value is explained under the form, and the field shows the saved value again. Checks: names and folders can't be blank; a folder can't be inside the keep folder (or the keep inside it), inside another root, or another root's folder; and the file must still load.
- **Adding and scanning:** **Add folder…** watches a folder: its id comes from the folder name (`-2`… when taken), its name is the folder as chosen, and it starts with the default excludes. Adding the folder of a root that isn't watched watches that root again. Adding, watching again, or changing a folder or excludes offers a scan.
- **Remove…** asks which it is, with the safe choice selected:
  - **Stop watching it, and keep its items** (§4 "Not watched").
  - **Delete its items from this keep**, with counts: items whose files are all in that root, and containers left with no files and nothing inside, go with their tags. Items with files in other roots only lose these. A second confirmation follows; this can't be undone. The root's per-user override is forgotten too.
- Scans skip roots that aren't watched. The main window refreshes its pages after any change.
- **Tabs:** **Folders** (the above), **Thumbnails**, and **Keep**.
- **Thumbnails tab:**
  - **Largest size:** "The theme's size (N px)", or a size of the keep's own (16 to 2048 px), written as `[thumbnails] max_size`. It applies at once: the resolver makes thumbnails at the new size (the size is part of the cache key, so others are remade as shown), and View → Thumbnail size's presets and zoom follow.
  - **Make new and changed items' thumbnails after each scan** (on by default; `[thumbnails] after_scan`, §6 step 7): off for a huge keep on a slow share, so thumbnails are made only as pages show them. Turning it off stops a run.
  - **Stored:** how many thumbnails are stored and their size, with **Clear…** (§10). It is counted again (in a worker) whenever the tab comes into view or the window gets the focus, and every 3 seconds while the tab is in view, since grids store thumbnails as they show them.
- **Keep tab:** the keep's name (editable) and folder; the theme (name, id, version, built in or its file), the types it holds, and its description; and the theme's **options**. Each option has an editor by type (checkbox, number box, or text box) showing the keep's value or the default, with **Default** to remove the keep's setting.
- **Folder options:** on the Folders tab, each option has a checkbox to override it for the selected folder (unticked: the keep's value, named in the tooltip) and an editor. Ticking starts from the keep's value; unticking removes the override.
- Option changes apply at the next scan; changed values make it read the affected roots' files again (§4).
- Root paths used for thumbnails follow the configuration as it changes (a root added, moved, or overridden), not the paths at opening.

**Dashboard.** Opening screen for a keep: counts per entity type, recently added items, untagged share (links to triage), root health, most/least used tags. Themes can add cards (for example, total runtime).

- **Page** (#113, `ui/dashboard.py`, `core/dashboard.py`): LIBRARY → **Dashboard**, the keep's name over a grid of cards. Every number is a link. A keep opens on it (#115; `main_window.OPENING_PAGE`), highlighted in the navigation, as where Back starts.
- **Items:** each type's plural and count, and the total. A type opens Search all narrowed to it (its "Only" chip).
- **Untagged:** "N of M items (P%) have no tag of their own", counted as Triage's list (dismissed items aren't counted), with a bar. "Tag them in Triage →" opens Triage on Untagged items. When archives have new files to review (#520), "N archives have new files to review: New in archives →" follows, opening Triage on that tab.
- **Folders:** each root as online and scanned when ("just now", "12 minutes ago", "3 hours ago", or a date), offline, not watched, or not scanned yet, with its file count. A root opens Keep configuration on it.
- **Recently added:** a strip of the 12 newest items' thumbnails (by `created_at`), from the window's thumbnail loader. Double-click (or Enter) opens a page.
- **Most used tags:** the 5 tags applied directly to the most items, as full paths with counts.
- **Least used tags:** the 5 least, unused first, not repeating those already shown as most used. A tag opens Search all with it as an include chip.
- **The theme's cards** follow the built-in ones, three to a row (§9 "Dashboard cards").
- **Reading:** one read in a worker; again when the page is shown again, on Refresh, and after tagging, undo, a scan, or a configuration change.

**Search view.** Filter bar with include chips, exclude chips ("but not"), field filters (only valid ones for the scope), `Within` chip, text box, toggles for "Show contained items", "Inherit tags", and "By contents" (`aggregate_up`). Results as grid (thumbnails), list (columns), or tree/grouped. Multi-select supported. **The item under the mouse** (#473, M34; decided in discussion, 2026-10-19) is outlined, not filled: a 1px rounded frame in the selection color over the row (list, tree) or card (grid), an overlay like the drop outline (`ui/hover.py`, `HoverOutline`), while the rows are drawn without the style's hover state (`NoHoverFill`, a proxy of the application's style set on the results views only). Qt's Windows 11 style fills a hovered row almost like a selected one, so after a click and the arrow keys the clicked row, still under the mouse, looked selected too.

- **Results model** (`ui/models/results.py`): setting a search counts the matches and loads the first page (100 rows) in a worker, in one read transaction; the row count is then the full total, so the scroll bar is right at once. Other pages load in workers when the view first asks for one of their rows, which show `…` until then; the 50 most recently used pages stay in memory. Each search has a generation number and older results are dropped. After a scan, open search pages re-run their search, keeping their rows until the new ones arrive. A refresh that finds the same number of results (the usual case after tagging) updates the rows in place, so the selection and scroll position stay; when the count changes, the model resets and the page re-selects the items still listed (on loaded pages). Search all's sections re-select the same way when rebuilt.
- **List layout**: the title column, named by the type's `title_label` when the scope has one; a Type column when the scope has several types; then the `card=True` fields every scoped type has. Clicking the title or a field column sorts by it (the type column doesn't sort). Every column can be resized, as in the tree (#413; the title used to stretch, and Qt never lets a stretched column be dragged): the others start at fixed widths (narrower for numbers), the title takes the width they leave, between 200 and 480 px, and the last shown column fills the rest. The title keeps fitting the width (as the window or the shown columns change) until the user resizes it; then it keeps the user's width until the columns change (`ResultTable.fit_title`). Columns can be moved by dragging their headings, and the tree follows the list's order and the other way round. **Remembered per view** (#417): the order, as `column_order` in `ui_state.json` (`{"view:Books": ["title", "series", "authors", …]}`: every key the view has shown, so a Search all list of one type doesn't lose another's; `merged_order`), and the widths the user dragged, as `column_widths` (`{"view:Books": {"authors": 250}}`, saved once a drag ends; the last shown column, which fills the rest, isn't recorded; a remembered title width stops it fitting). When the columns change, the remembered order is applied (`arranged_keys`): a column it doesn't name (a field the theme added) goes right after the column the theme puts before it, and a column that's gone is skipped. The header menu's **Reset column order and widths** puts the theme's order and first widths back and forgets both, leaving which columns are shown. Search all's sections keep the theme's order and widths. Columns are never sized to contents, which would read every row. The generic theme shows Name, Extension, Folder (the parent folder within the root), Size (as a size: `display="bytes"`), and Modified.
- **Field filters** (#94, `ui/field_filters.py`): a **+ Field** button on the filter row lists the searchable fields every listed type has (`scope_fields` with a `search` kind; disabled, with a tooltip, when there are none, as in a Search all over types without shared fields). Picking one opens a small popup under it: a **choice** field lists its values among the current results with counts, most common first (`core.search.choice_counts`, in a worker; the field's own filter is left out of the counts, so other values stay offered; up to 500), each with a checkbox; a **range** field has From and To boxes (either may be empty, parsed like edited values); a **text** field has "contains" or "starts with" and a box. **Apply** (or Enter) adds a purple chip after the Within/Only chips and before the tag chips ("Extension: .png, .jpg", "Width: 500–1920", "Width ≥ 500", 'Folder contains "ice"'); clicking a chip reopens its popup, × or **Clear** in the popup removes it, and Clear all removes them all. A chip whose field the listed types no longer all have (after an Only chip, say) stays, dashed and grey with a tooltip, and isn't applied until the scope has the field again. Field filters are filters, not view settings: they aren't remembered.
- **Toggles** (#91, #133): **Contained** (show contained items, §8), **Inherit tags**, and **By contents** (a container matches when anything inside it does: albums with a song tagged *Live*; `aggregate_up`, §8) are checkboxes at the right of the filter row, with tooltips saying what each does. A page starts with its view's `show_contained` / `inherit_tags` / `aggregate_up` (off for Search all and a detail page's contents); what the user chooses is remembered per view in `ui_state.json` (`toggles`: `{"view:Images": {"show_contained": false, "inherit_tags": true, "aggregate_up": false}}`) and saved with saved searches. They are view settings, not filters, so Clear all leaves them. When contained items are listed, and the listed types can hold other types, the list gains a **Type** column (an artist's images and fonts are told apart).
- **Kind chips** (#513, M36, `ui/kind_chips.py`; decided in discussion, 2026-10-23): under the filter bar, a row of toggle chips, one per kind in the list (§8 *Kinds*) with how many items have it (`count_by_kind`, in a worker after each search and refresh, as if nothing were hidden). Shown when the list has two kinds or more, or one that's hidden; not over Search all's sections (its one-type list has them). Kinds of items first, then file types, each by label: a file type's extension in capitals (`PDF`, `No extension`), a type's plural (`Folders`), the page's own types **No file** unless contents show (Contained, or the tree). A click hides a kind and the chip stays, unchecked, dimmed, and struck through (tooltip "Show PDF files"); Ctrl+click shows only that kind, and again shows them all. Hiding is `SearchSpec.hidden_kinds`, so counts and paging stay right. In the tree, the chips count what containers hold too (as with Contained), and a container kept for its shown contents is drawn dimmed (`kinds_of`, the row's `@hidden` value). Everything hidden says **All kinds are hidden**. What's hidden is remembered in `ui_state.json` (`hidden_kinds`, under the page's state key: per view, and per tab key on item pages, `contents:<type>:<type>` and `related:<type>`, shared by every page with that tab, like layouts), as the hidden kinds, so new kinds start shown; kinds not in the list now are kept. A contents tab counts what shows, `Books (9 of 16)`, and isn't hidden as empty while its chips hide everything. **Gallery tabs** (#514) have them too, above the scrolling pictures so they stay in view: the kinds are the gallery files' types (an archive is one file of its type, `ZIP`), counted and hidden on screen, as a gallery is already a loaded list (`GalleryView.kinds`, `set_hidden`); each group (heading and pictures) is a box hidden when it's left with nothing, an archive's with its type; the tab says `Scans (2 of 3)`; remembered under `gallery:<item type>:<gallery title>` (`DetailPage.recall_kinds`, `remember_kinds`). A gallery that isn't a tab (on a page without tabs) has no chips.
- **Tree layout** (#91, `ui/result_tree.py`): the third layout button. The top level is the search's own results (the list's lazily paged model), leaving out those another result holds (`nest`, §8); a container's row expands to what it directly holds, loaded in a worker on first expansion (`core.search.child_hits`: every type, in the container's `contents_sort` order (by title unless the theme says otherwise, §9), not re-filtered by includes, text, or fields, but without excluded items, inherited when Inherit tags is on; up to 1,000). Children can expand in turn. When the page refreshes (after tagging, undo, or a scan), expanded containers' children are loaded again: the same items are updated in place (keeping what is expanded and selected), and items that came or went replace the rows. The tree lists contents as children, so **Contained is greyed out in the tree** (its tooltip says why) and the top level isn't flattened; it has a Type column when the listed types can hold others. Selection, the preview strip, tagging (the Tags panel, drops with the dashed outline), double-click, and the right-click menu work on any row; sorting by a column sorts the top level. Search all's sections stay lists (the tree button is disabled there), and a view can start as a tree (`SearchView(layout="tree")`); the choice is remembered like the others. Right-clicking its header shows the list's column menu (`SearchPage.column_menu`): the two layouts show the same columns, and a column shown from the tree starts at the list's width.
- **Grid layout** (#77): cards in wrapping rows of one uniform size, each a thumbnail (or its icon while there is no picture; a faint placeholder while loading) above the title and the **card lines**, elided to fit; the tooltip lists the title and every card line with its label. It shows the same model as the list and **shares its selection**, so switching keeps what is selected, and it accepts dropped tags like the list (dashed amber outline). It is a `QListView` in list mode with wrapping and uniform item sizes, not icon mode, which would store a position for every item.
- **Switching layouts:** two icon buttons at the right of the page header, next to the count. A view starts in its declared `layout` (`grid` or `list`); the user's choice is remembered per view in `ui_state.json` (`layouts`). Search all's sections stay lists (the buttons are disabled there); the choice applies once one type is shown.
- **Card lines:** by default each type shows its theme's `card_lines`. Right-clicking the grid offers **Card lines**, a checkbox for each field of the listed types, and "Use the theme's card lines"; the user's choice is a list of field names (each type shows those it has), remembered per view in `ui_state.json` (`card_lines`). The fields load with each page of rows even when no column shows them. Values show as in list cells until field display formats arrive (#184). A card's tooltip lists its title and card lines, then its type's description (#414), wrapped to 70 characters and cut after four lines (`ResultGrid.card_tooltip`).
- **Thumbnail loading** (`ui/thumbnails.py`): one `ThumbnailLoader` per window resolves thumbnails on its own pool of 4 threads, so thumbnails never delay searches. Painting a card requests its entity; the **newest requests go first** (scrolled-past requests wait, and more than 400 waiting drops the oldest), and up to 600 decoded images are kept (LRU). Arrivals repaint the grid at most every 30 ms. After a scan the loaded thumbnails are forgotten (files may have changed); on closing, the window waits for running thumbnail jobs before the keep closes.
- **Tags column and hiding columns** (#197): every list (and each of Search all's sections) has a **Tags** column, right after the title (and Type), listing the tags applied directly to the item by name (the path when a name is ambiguous), with full paths in the tooltip; it isn't sortable. Tag names load with each page of rows, in the worker (`row_values`, `core.tags.entity_tags`), and refresh after tagging. Right-clicking a column header shows every column with a checkbox (the title can't be hidden). **Tags starts hidden**; what the user shows or hides is remembered per view in `ui_state.json` (`hidden_columns`: `{"search:": [...], "view:Files": [...]}`) and applies to the list and every section of that page. **Every field is a column** (#314; asked for in review): after the title, Type, Tags, and Keywords come the fields the scoped types share that their pages show (not `detail=False` ones), card fields first and shown, then the types' description (`description_field`, #414), shown, 320 px wide, and on one line, the rest **hidden until shown** from the header menu (`ResultColumn.hidden_by_default`), so Books' Link or ISBN, or Music's Bitrate, can be listed and sorted. Showing one is remembered per view as `shown_columns` (`{"view:Books": ["link"]}`), beside `hidden_columns`; a column the user hid stays hidden.
- The heading shows the view's name and "N items" (or "Nothing found", or the error for a search that can't run, such as one sorting by an unknown field).
- **Filter bar** (`ui/filter_bar.py`): a text box and a tag box on one row, with chips below that wrap onto more lines. Typing in the tag box lists matching tags (`TagTree.suggest`: name or alias contains the text, ranked whole name, start of name, start of a word, anywhere, then the same through an alias; each shown with its parent path). **Enter adds an include chip; Shift+Enter adds a "but not" chip**, the same keys the tagging panel uses to apply and remove tags; Up/Down move, Esc closes the list. Include chips are blue, exclude chips red with "not:"; a tag added to the other list moves there; × removes a chip; "Clear all" removes every chip and the text. The text box applies 300 ms after typing stops, or on Enter; Ctrl+F focuses it. Filters apply on top of the view's own spec, keeping its types, toggles, and current sort. The suggestion list is a child widget of the window rather than a popup window, so it never takes focus from the tag box. The tag tree is loaded in a worker and reloaded when the page refreshes. The `Within` chip is described under "Drilling down", the toggles under "Toggles".
- **Global search, grouped** ("Search all", `ui/grouped_results.py`): one collapsible section per type that has matches, in the theme's type order, headed `▾ Album (40)`. Each shows the type's first 8 matches with **that type's own list columns** (Albums show Year, Artists Country), and "Show all 40 →" when there are more. Show all narrows the page to that type with a removable **"Only: Album" chip** (first in the chip row; Clear all removes it too), showing the full lazily paged, sortable list. When only one type matches (always, for a one-type theme such as generic), its full list shows directly, without a chip. Counts and previews come from one worker job in one read transaction. Folded sections stay folded when the results change (for the session, not saved). Section headings and the Only chip use the type's plural name (`Entity.plural`, §9); the Type column uses its singular `label`. A sort chosen on one type's list (Year) is kept while the listed types have that field, and otherwise the page's default sort applies; theme views are never grouped. **One selection across the sections** (#469; found by the user, 2026-10-19): a plain click or key in one section replaces the whole selection, clearing the other sections (`ResultTable.selection_replaced`, from Qt's `selectionCommand`); Ctrl+click and Shift+click add to it, so items of several types can be tagged together.

**Detail page.** Theme-declared sections: fields (inline edit; user edits set provenance `user`; a rating is five stars set by clicking, §9 *Fields*; the keep's own fields follow the theme's in Details, #581, and a page whose theme has no fields section gets one for them, first), role slots (drop files to link), galleries, related entities, and for containers an embedded search of contents with its own filter bar. Header shows breadcrumbs and actions.
- **Details beside Tags** (#468, M34; decided in discussion, 2026-10-19): the page's middle is the header (picture, title, type, note, More) and, below it, the searches as tabs (contents, related, galleries). The other sections (fields, Extra fields, files, From the file, and related links and contents counts where there are no searches) are `DetailPage.details`, shown in a **Details** dock tabbed with **Tags** (`ui/details_dock.py`; tabs on top). Galleries that aren't tabs stay below the header: they're content, not details. A page with no tabs below moves its sections too, so details are always in one place. Fields show each label above its value (`QFormLayout.WrapAllRows`), as the dock is narrow, and the panel never scrolls sideways. On a search, the Details tab shows the one selected item's sections (a `DetailPage` that isn't shown, with no searches of its own, named at the top with a link to its page; edits work as on its page); several selected, or none, say so. The tab chosen last (`side_tab`) and the dock's width (`side_width`, applied once the window has its size) are kept in `ui_state.json`. The header keeps its height in the page's splitter; the tabs get the rest. **The tabs start right under the header** (#491; asked for in discussion, 2026-10-20): the splitter gives the middle what it needs (`sizeHint`: the header and any galleries there), at most 60% of the page (`HEADER_MAX_SHARE`, a taller one scrolls), whenever the page loads or resizes, until the user drags the divider; then it stays where they put it while the page is open.

- **Rendering** (#86, `core/detail.py` + `ui/detail_view.py`): a header with the thumbnail (192 px box, from the window's thumbnail loader) beside the title and type and the item's note (#380, `ui/note_view.py`: its body as rendered Markdown under the title; collapsed, only its main text (#553: paragraphs and lists with their bold, italics, and links; no headings, pictures, tables, code blocks, or rules), capped at the room beside the picture; **Show more**/**Show less** expands it in place to all of it, styled, at most half the window's height, past which it scrolls; an item without a note whose own file is Markdown shows a glimpse of that file there instead (#554, `core/markdown_glimpse.py`), with **Open file**; relative links and pictures resolve from the note's folder, read only; **Open note** under it and in **More ▾**), then the sections top to bottom, full width, in a scroll area; each section is a bold heading with a rule. `load_detail()` reads everything in one worker job. The type's `DetailView` gives the sections; without one, `default_detail_view()` shows its fields, its primary role, and (for a type that can contain others) its contents.
  - **Details** (`Section.fields()`): each field with `detail=True`, label and value; omitted for a type without fields.
- **Re-reading from files** (#96, `core/reextract.py`): **Re-read from file** (a result's right-click menu, for the selection when the item is in it, and a detail page's **More ▾** menu) marks the items' linked files pending and ingests them at once through the theme (`scanjob.ingest_pending`, with the root's theme options), without walking the root: useful after changing a file, or to refresh what a scan read. Fields the user edited keep their values. **Re-read from file, replacing my edits…** asks first ("Edit → Undo can put your edits back"), then clears the `user` provenance on those items so the files' values (title included) win. Either way it is **one undo step**: the step holds each item's title, field values, and provenance before and after, and undo or redo restores them. Items whose files are offline or missing are skipped and counted in the status bar.
- **Extra fields** (#95): every page has an **Extra fields** section, right after Details (first when the type has none), for fields the user keeps on one item, stored in `entity.extra` (names and text values) and searchable as text like the theme's text fields. Each shows its name and value, edited in place like any field (empty removes it), with × to remove it; a row of two boxes (name, value) and **Add** (or Enter) adds one. A name already used on the item, an empty name, or an empty value is refused with the reason in the status bar. Each add, change, or removal is one Edit → Undo step (`core.fields.edit_extra`, recording the whole `extra` before and after). Scans never touch `extra`, except that notes (#379, §9 *Notes*) add Extra fields marked as theirs (provenance `extra:<name>` = `note`), which scans update until the user edits or removes one. On the page (#380) they are marked "• from the note" (an edited one "• edited"); editing one sets `extra:<name>` to `user`, removing one drops its provenance and records `note_key_removal`, and the step's undo puts both back (`ExtraChange` carries the field's provenance and removal before and after). An Extra field the user added themselves has no provenance row and is never the note's.
- **Editing in place** (#93, `ui/field_editor.py`, `core/fields.py`): a field declared `editable` (the default) and the title can be edited on the page. Double-click the value (or the ✎ that appears on hover) for a text box; Enter or clicking away saves, Esc cancels. What is typed is parsed for the field's type (whole numbers and numbers may have thousands commas, dates are `YYYY-MM-DD`, dates and times `YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM` in local time, stored in UTC); empty clears an optional field; a value that doesn't parse stays in the box, outlined, with the reason. Yes/no fields are a checkbox that saves when ticked. A save runs through the DB writer (`TagService.edit_field`), sets provenance **`user`** for that field, so scans never overwrite it, refreshes the search index, and is **one step in the Edit → Undo history**, shared with tag changes ("Undo: Set Family of 'Aileron-Regular.ttf' to 'Aileron Sans'"). Undo restores both the value and its previous provenance, so an undone edit is again the scan's to update. Values you edited show a small "• edited" mark (tooltip: scans won't change it). Refused edits (an empty required field, a value of the wrong type, a field declared `editable=False`) say why in the status bar and the page shows the stored value again. Renaming an entity never renames its file. The built-in themes mark facts that come from the file (extension, folder, size, modified, dimensions, image counts, and the assets2d artist, which follows the folder) `editable=False`; a font's family and style stay editable.
  - **Role** (`Section.role(name)`, titled by the role's `label` or name): each linked file's path, root name, size, and **offline** / **missing** status; the tooltip is this machine's full path. Up to 200 are listed.
  - **Gallery** (`Section.gallery(name)`): the role's images as thumbnails, made in one worker job (`ThumbnailResolver.resource_thumbnail`). Like a file row, a picture opens with its program on double-click (or Enter), and right-click gives the same file menu (#124; galleries had no way to open a picture before movies used one). Since #386 (§9 *Galleries*): groups for subfolders and archives, an archive's pictures listed and drawn in workers and opened from a temporary copy (`ui/gallery_view.py`).
  - **Related** (`Section.related(name)`): links to the related entities, from either side (the relationship's `label` or `reverse_label`, else the other type's plural); a link opens that entity's page.
  - **Contents** (#87): a search of the entity's contents (`within` the entity, scoped to the types the theme lets it contain), below the other sections with a splitter between them (about 40/60). It is an ordinary search page: its own filter bar, list/grid toggle (starting as a grid), sorting, double-click to open, and tag drops. Its hidden columns, layout, and card lines are remembered per container type (`contents:<type id>` in `ui_state.json`), so every artist's page looks the same. **Tagging** applies to the items selected in the contents, or to the page's own entity when none are. `Section.custom` isn't shown yet.
  - **Related sections** (#125) work the same way: each is a search of its own (`related=(name, entity)`; `search_fields` supplies each relationship's link columns as `@<name>.a` / `@<name>.b`), titled by the section (Cast, Filmography), with list/grid/tree, sorting, thumbnails, and tagging, remembered per type (`related:<type id>`). Its header has **Add…** (#260), and each item's menu **Remove from \<section>** (the selection, if the item is in it; one undo step). With contents and related sections, or several related sections, the area below the sections is tabs, and tagging applies to the selection in the current tab. Without a way to embed (no window), a related section is links with an × each.
  - **Contents by type** (#456, M33; decided in discussion, 2026-10-17): the contents are one search per type the item can hold (`contained_types`), each a tab named by the type's plural with its count (**Albums (12)**, **Songs (40)**; `SearchPage.counted`), so an artist's albums aren't lost among their songs. A type's tab lists those items at any depth, as Contents did (an artist's Songs include its albums' songs). A tab whose search finds nothing is hidden; when all are empty and the page has no other tabs, the first stays, saying so (an actor holding no folders shows only their credits and photos). No **Everything** tab (decided in discussion: too messy, and **Show in search** gives everything, on each tab). Each tab sorts by the container's `contents_sort` keys its type has a field for, else by title (`type_contents_order`). Layouts are remembered per container and type (`contents:<container>:<type>`); an item that can hold only one type keeps `contents:<container>`, and its one search, with no tab bar unless there are other tabs, looks as before.
  - **Galleries as tabs** (#456, decided in discussion): when a page has searches below its sections, its galleries are tabs there too, last, each with its count (**Scans (3)**), instead of sections above; made again on each refresh, keeping the tab shown. Without such searches they stay sections.
  - **The tab last used** on a type of page is chosen again when one opens (decided in discussion; `detail_tabs` in `ui_state.json`, by type id: `contents:<type>`, `related:<name>`, `gallery:<title>`), when that page has it and it isn't hidden. Tabs chosen by the page itself aren't remembered.
  - **Labels apart** (decided while building): the movies theme's person side of `cast` and `pictured` became **Movie credits** and **Picture credits** (nouns, so "Add 'Alien' to Al Pacino's movie credits" and "Remove from picture credits" read well), apart from the **Movies** and **Pictures** tabs of what is under the person's folders (§9 *Folder items*), which had read as the same.
- **Opening** (#86, #107): double-click or Enter on a result (list, grid, tree, or a Search all section) opens its page, except that types with `double_click = "open_file"` open the file; the page stays reachable with Ctrl+Enter and the menu's Open page. A page is created once per entity per window and refreshed after a scan. Clicking the navigation's highlighted item (or selecting it) goes back to it, so a detail page is never a dead end before back/forward (#88).
- **Tagging** works on the page's entity: the Tags panel's checks, Enter, Ctrl+T, and New tag apply to it.
- **Breadcrumbs** (#88): above the title, the containers the entity is in, outermost first, each a link to its page (`Aurora Studio ›`). They follow `entity_contains` upward; where an entity has several containers the chain follows the first by title, so it's stable, and the line adds "(also in N others)". A cycle (bad data) or 64 levels stops the chain.
- **Drilling down** (#89): right-clicking a result (in a list, a grid card, or a Search all section) offers **Open** and **Show contents in search**, enabled for types that can contain others (`contained_types`, at any depth). It adds a grey **"Within: Aurora Studio"** chip to the current search, first in the chip row, keeping its tag filters and text; its close button (or Clear all) removes it. What is listed within a container: Search all stays grouped by type; a view keeps its own types where the container can hold them (Images within an artist lists that artist's images), and otherwise lists everything the container holds (Artists within an artist lists its assets, not nothing). A container's detail page has **Show in search** beside its contents, which opens Search all with the chip. The grid's context menu starts with the card's actions, then Card lines and sizes.
- **Back and forward** (#88): every page shown (a view, Search all, a detail page) is recorded; **Back** and **Forward** are on the toolbar and in the **Go** menu, with Alt+Left / Alt+Right (and the platform's Back/Forward keys) and the mouse's back and forward buttons anywhere in the window. Showing the page already current isn't a step; showing a new page after going back drops the forward pages; 100 steps are kept. The navigation pane highlights the current page, and nothing while a detail page is showing (it doesn't list them). The 30 most recently shown detail pages stay alive; an older one is made again when the user goes back to it.

**Archives** (#519, M37; designed in discussion, 2026-10-23; `ui/archive_actions.py`, `ui/archive_dialog.py`, `core/archive_choices.py`). What is read inside an archive (§6 *Archive members*) is chosen from right-click menus; the window's `ArchiveActions` is handed to its pages like the file opener.
- **An archive's commands** (on its row in an item's Files section, on a gallery's archive group's pictures, and in a search result's **Inside the archive** submenu, filled once the item's file is found, offered on types whose primary role takes archives): **Choose what to read inside…**, **Read everything inside** (mode `all`, clearing hidden members; the folder is scanned), and **Stop reading inside** (mode `none`: its members are skipped at once, their items hidden with their tags kept). A command that changes nothing says so ("Everything inside UI pack.zip is read already").
- **Hide inside its archive** (moved here from #524, decided in discussion): on a member's row in an item's Files section and in Duplicates. The member is recorded hidden and skipped at once; the archive keeps its way of reading.
- **Read everything inside archives here:** on a folder's row (an item whose file is a folder): adds `<folder>/**` (the folder's name escaped as Skip in scans does) to the root's `archives` patterns and scans; Keep configuration → Folders → **Read inside archives** shows and edits them.
- **The dialog** (`ChooseInsideDialog`, opened without blocking): the archive's listing, read in a worker, as a tree of folders (with their file counts) and files (with sizes). Each row has a × that hides it; a folder's hides everything under it, or shows it all again when it's all hidden. Hidden rows stay listed, greyed and struck through, like a kind chip that's off; **Show hidden files** (on) tucks them away. **Show all** and **Hide all** start from everything or nothing, so a few files to read or to leave out take a few clicks. **Show thumbnails** (off unless chosen, remembered in the keep's UI state) swaps the tree for a grid of tiles, one per file, 96 px unless the user picks another size beside the box or with Ctrl and the wheel (the zoom steps up to the keep's thumbnail size, which they're made at; remembered, like the box, in the keep's UI state, so each user has their own) (folders are in each tile's tooltip, with its size); clicking a tile hides or shows it, and a hidden tile is faint and struck through. Each file something can draw (pictures, fonts, PDFs...: `member_renderer`) is drawn a batch at a time in workers, through `archive_thumbnails`, cached by the archive's file and time like a gallery's archive pictures; the others show a file icon. Kind chips (`KindChips`, M36) hide or show every file of a type at once, a chip being off when all its files are hidden. Files the theme doesn't read aren't listed: the archive's name's tooltip and the status bar say how many and which kinds ("1 file Tagalot doesn't read here: .txt"); folders, hidden files, and archives inside the archive aren't either (#391). The first time, everything is shown; after, the current choice. **Read these** saves it and scans the folder; **Cancel** changes nothing.
- **Saving** (`save_choices`): an archive read whole stays whole, with the hidden ones left out; any other becomes member by member, the shown ones read and the hidden ones recorded hidden, new (undecided) ones too, since the dialog marks them (#520). Members hidden are skipped at once; the archive is listed again at the scan, which reads the members now wanted. Not an undo step: the dialog, the commands, and Triage's Skipped tab are how it's changed back.

**Tagging panel.**
- **Folds** (#490; asked for in discussion, 2026-10-20): every tag starts expanded. Two small buttons beside the filter box, **Expand all** and **Collapse all** (a drawn box with a plus or minus, `fold_icon`), open or close the whole tree, leaving the top level; the panel's right-click menu ends with them, on a tag or off the tags. They set the folds kept while filtering and across reloads, like the user's own. Folds aren't remembered between sessions.
- Filter box at the top: narrows the tree as you type, keeping ancestors of matches visible; matches aliases, ignoring case and accents (§7). Matches are bold (ancestors shown for context are not); a tag matched through an alias shows it, `Money (finance)`. While filtering everything is expanded; clearing the filter (or Esc) restores the folds the user had. Down moves into the tree, on the first match. The tree starts with the top level open, shows a tag's color as a swatch, and its path and aliases as a tooltip; "No tags yet." / "No tags match …" when empty. It is loaded in a worker (`ui/models/tag_tree.py`, `ui/tag_panel.py`).
- Drag one or more tags onto an item or selection. Dropping on a **selected** row tags the whole selection (rows not loaded yet are looked up in one query); dropping on any other row tags just that row. While dragging, the rows a drop would tag get a dashed amber outline, one per run of consecutive rows (overlay frames, so cell repaints can't break it up). Drops work on the list and on Search all's sections. Each drop is one undoable step (**Edit → Undo**, Ctrl+Z); the status bar says what happened ("Tagged 2 items with 'Favorites'.", or "Already tagged…" when nothing changed, which adds no undo step). Open search pages refresh afterwards, since tag filters may match differently.
- Keyboard: type to filter, arrows to move, Enter applies the highlighted tag to the selection, Shift+Enter removes it. In detail: Enter uses the tags selected in the tree, else the highlighted one, else (while filtering) the first match; from the filter box the text is then selected, so typing the next tag replaces it. Typing letters in the tree goes to the filter box; Esc in the tree goes back to it. **Ctrl+T** (Edit → Tag selection…) shows the panel and puts the cursor in its filter from anywhere in the window. The selection is the current page's selected rows (every section's, in Search all's grouped view; rows not loaded yet are looked up). With nothing selected, the status bar says so and nothing is recorded.
- Selection summary: each tag shows a tri-state check (all / some / none of the selected items). **On an item's page** (#504; found in use) the label above the tree says whose they are: the page's item's ("**Terry Pratchett**'s tags") while nothing is selected in what the page shows below its header, else the selection's, with a link **Back to Terry Pratchett's tags** that selects nothing there (`DetailPage.clear_selection`). The checks are counted again after a scan and after online lookups that changed items, not only when the selection changes: a page with no search below its header got no refresh that did it as a side effect, so its checks went stale. Clicking toggles between applying to all and removing from all. Only tags applied directly count (a parent shows none unless it is applied itself). Counts are computed in a worker (`core.tags.tag_counts`, batched) 150 ms after the selection settles, and again after any tagging, undo, or page switch; stale answers are dropped. Clicking a check doesn't change it directly: it requests the tagging (the same path as Enter), and the check follows once that's done. A line above the tree says how many items are selected; tooltips add "On 1 of the 3 selected items". With nothing selected there are no checks.
- **Right-click a tag**: "Show only items with 'Iceland'" / "Hide items with 'Iceland'" add it to the current search as an include or "but not" chip (Search all opens if the current page isn't a search); "Add 'Iceland' to / Remove it from the N selected items" tag the selection (disabled with nothing selected). Right-clicking one of several tags selected in the tree acts on all of them ("2 tags").
- **Tag groups** (#535, §7 *Tag groups*; decided in discussion, 2026-10-25): **Groups ▾** beside the filter box (its text "All tags", the group's name, or "2 groups") lists **All tags** and each group, ticked when shown. With groups shown, the tree is each group as a heading row (its name, small capitals, dim; not selectable, checkable, or draggable) over its tags lifted to the top level (`group_tops`), their sub-tags under them; a tag in two groups shown is listed under each (the user's idea in discussion: the heading says why the tree looks as it does, rather than greyed ancestors). While filtering, a group without matches is left out and matches outside the groups shown follow under **Other tags**, in place in the tree, so nothing is unreachable; Enter and Down use the first match in that order. Which groups show is each user's, kept in `ui_state.json` (`tag_groups`), never the keep's; a deleted group is dropped from it, and none left shows every tag. Headings start open; folding one is kept across reloads, and Collapse all leaves them open. Right-click a tag → **Groups ▸**: each group, ticked when every tag clicked is in it itself (ticking puts them in, unticking takes them out), and **New group…** (a name, then the group is made holding them, one undo step). Right-click a heading: **Stop showing “…”** and **Show all tags**. The model's rows are nodes of their own, each naming its tag or none for a heading, since a tag can show twice (`ui/models/tag_tree.py`).
- **Only their tags** (#541; asked for in discussion, 2026-10-25): a checkable button beside the filter box (a drawn box with a tick, `checked_icon`; tooltip "Show only the tags the selected items have") narrows the tree to the tags the selected items have, all or some of them, with their parents for context, expanded like a filter. It follows the selection (each summary rebuilds the tree) and combines with the filter box (matches they have). With groups shown, their tags in the groups show under the headings, and the rest under **Other tags**, greyed (the palette's placeholder color; still ticked and tickable). With nothing selected the panel says "Select items to see the tags they have."; with no tags, "The selected items have no tags yet." Unticking a tag there takes it off, and it leaves the list (Undo brings it back). The toggle isn't remembered between sessions.
- "Create tag '…'" appears when the filter text matches nothing. It sits right under the filter (the empty tree hides), and Enter does the same. The text may be a path: `Places > Norway` (">" with spaces around it) or `Places › Norway` creates Norway under Places, creating missing parents too, all as one undo step (`split_tag_path`, `add_tag_path`, `TagService.add_path`); "/" is not a separator, so names like "AC/DC" stay whole. The button shows the path as it will be created. With items selected, the new tag is also applied to them ("…and add it to 2 items"), a second undo step. Shift+Enter never creates.

**Tag manager.** Full tree with usage counts; it has its own tree and details, so the **Tags** and **Details** panel is hidden while it's the current page and comes back as it was on any other (which of the two were open, the tab chosen; #508, asked for in discussion); add, rename, reparent (drag or "Move to…"), merge, delete with subtree options, aliases, colors. Undo within the session. **Icon** in the details pane, beside **Color** (#398): type or paste an emoji (its tooltip mentions Win+. for Windows' emoji picker), or choose a small picture with **Picture…** (#507, shown beside the box); Enter or leaving the box saves it, **Clear** clears either, a refused one is reported in the status bar and the box shows the tag's icon again; a tag without one says which ancestor's it shows ("🌍 from 'Places'"). The tag tree (the Tags panel, the manager, pickers) shows each tag's own icon before its name. **Export tags…** and **Import tags…** on its toolbar save and load the tree as YAML (§7 *Importing and exporting tags*; Export is a menu: **Tags only…**, or **Tags and the items that have them…**, #396); import shows its preview in a dialog (`ui/tag_import.py`) with the delete checkbox, and its **Import** button is disabled when there is nothing to change or the file can't be read. A file that isn't text is reported in a message box; the result goes to the status bar ("Imported tags: 2 new, 1 updated.").
- **Tag groups** (#536, §7 *Tag groups*): the details pane's **Groups** row names the groups the tag is in itself, then those it is in through a tag above it ("Trips; through 'Places': Holidays"), or "None"; **Change ▾** beside it ticks groups (putting the tag in or taking it out) and has **New group…** (a name, then a group holding the tag). The toolbar's **Groups ▾** lists each group as a submenu titled with how many tags it holds ("Trips (3 tags)"): its tags' paths (up to 12, then "and N more", or "No tags yet"), **Rename…**, and **Delete** (no question: its tags stay, and it can be undone), then **New group…** (holding the selected tag, if any). Each is one undo step, through `TagActions`; the status bar says what was done ("Deleted the group 'Travel'; its tags stay.").

- **Layout** (`ui/tag_manager.py`): a filter box and the tag tree on the left, the selected tag's details on the right (path, color, description, what it applies to (§7 *Tag types*), aliases, usage); editing arrives with #68–#70. The tree has two count columns: **Items** (tagged with the tag itself) and **With sub-tags** (distinct items tagged with it or any tag under it). Counts come from `core.tags.tag_usage`: one grouped query, then one distinct count per tag with children (about 24 ms for 300 tags over 50k items), in a worker, and are reloaded after any tagging or undo. Filtering, folds, and the selection behave as in the tagging panel.
- **Add, rename, move** (#68): **New tag…** (top level) and **New sub-tag…** (under the selected tag) ask for a name; tags are renamed in place (F2, double-click, or **Rename**); a tag moves by dragging it onto another tag (or onto empty space for the top level, or between rows for that level's parent) or with **Move to…**, a picker with a filter and a "Top level" choice in which the tag and its sub-tags are greyed out (`ui/tag_picker.py`). A move isn't confirmed first: it can be undone (Ctrl+Z), and the status bar shows the affected item count ("Moved 'Beach' under 'Topics' (2 items)."), which is how §7's "Show the affected item count" is met. Refused operations (a duplicate sibling name, a move under itself) explain themselves in the status bar.
- **Merge and delete** (#69): **Merge into…** picks the target in the same picker (the tag and its sub-tags greyed out, no top level) and says what will happen before confirming ("2 items tagged “Beach” will be tagged “Places › Iceland” instead. “Beach” becomes another name for it, and is then deleted."). **Delete…** confirms; for a tag with sub-tags it offers "Delete X and its N sub-tags" (the default) or "Delete only X; move its sub-tags up to …", and shows how many items will lose a tag for the current choice (with sub-tags: every item with any tag in the subtree; moving them up: only the tag's own items). The counts come from the page's usage figures, so the dialogs open without waiting. Both are undoable, and the status bar repeats the count afterwards.
- **Colors, descriptions, aliases** (#70) are edited in the details pane: **Choose…** (the system color picker) and **Clear** for the color; the description in a text box with **Save** and **Revert** (enabled once it differs); aliases in a short list with **Remove**, and an "Add another name…" box (Enter adds). Each change is one undoable step. Unsaved description text is kept per tag, so neither a reload (after any tag change) nor looking at another tag loses it; Revert discards it. The page's buttons (New tag…, Sub-tag…, Rename, Move…, Merge…, Delete…) are a toolbar across the page, so when it's narrow the ones that don't fit move into its overflow menu (…) instead of widening the page; the navigation pane has a minimum width so no page can squeeze it.
- **Undo / Redo** (#71): every tag operation and every tagging (drops, Enter, checks, the menus) goes on the session's one history. **Edit → Undo / Redo** (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y) name the step ("Undo Merge 'Beach' into 'Iceland'"); the Tag manager's toolbar also has short **Undo** / **Redo** buttons whose tooltips name it, disabled when there is nothing to undo or redo. After an undo or redo the pages, the Tags panel, and the tag manager reload, keeping their selection when the tag still exists, and the status bar says what was undone ("Undid: …"). Steps run one at a time in the order asked, on the same one-thread pool as tagging.

**Triage.** Tabs: resources not linked to any entity; entities with no tags; entities whose linked resources are all missing. Bulk actions: link to entity/role, tag, dismiss (hide until changed).

- **Page** (#110, `ui/triage.py`, `core/triage.py`): TOOLS → **Triage**, tabs, each with its count.
- **Unlinked files:** a table (folder, path, size, offline) of the first 10,000 files no item links; folders and missing files aren't listed. Actions: **Open file**, **Show in file manager**, **Skip in scans…** (adds an exact exclude pattern per file to its root, with `*`, `?`, `[` bracketed; the next scan flags them skipped, which takes them off the list), and **Dismiss**.
  - **Link to item…** (#243, `ui/link_dialog.py`, `core/links.py`): a dialog with a search box. It lists matching items (title and type) among the types with a role that takes every selected file, then offers those roles. Linking is one undo step, and in a one-file role the file replaces what was there.
    - The link is marked `by_user`: scans respect it (§9 ingest context), Re-read leaves the user's files out, and the file leaves the Unlinked list.
    - On the item's page the file row says "linked by you", and its menu has **Unlink from this item** (one undo step; only for links made by hand).
    - A theme may have no role for some files (the music theme's albums hold only their folder, so a stray `cover.jpg` can't be linked); the dialog says so.
  - A theme may read files without linking them (a cover image found in its folder), so some files here are expected.
- **Untagged items** and **Missing files** are ordinary search pages with `SearchSpec.triage` (`"untagged"`: no tag of its own, or none from a container with Inherit tags; `"missing"`: at least one linked file, and none that isn't missing; offline isn't missing). The Tags panel and drops tag their selection, as on any search page.
  - Untagged items has **Dismiss**.
  - Missing files has **Delete…** (after a question; the items and their tags go, never files; one undo step through the whole-entity snapshots of actions, §9) and **Show where they were** (opens the folder the first file was in, or says it's gone too). Both are also in an item's right-click menu (as Delete… and Show where it was), acting on the selection when the clicked item is in it, else on that item.
- **Dismissing** hides until changed: `triage_dismissal(list, target_id, marker)` stores how the thing looked. A file's marker is its size and modification time; an item's is its stored `updated_at`, which edits and re-reads change. The lists leave out rows whose marker still matches. It is one undo step.
- **Pictures** (#426, decided in review; `core/picture_problems.py`): items' pictures not found, and pictures that can't be drawn, one row per item and file (item, type, problem, file), by title.
  - **Not found:** what a theme reported with `ctx.not_found` (a Markdown book's `cover`), until the item has a file in that role. The details under the table give what the file says and each path looked in, in order (beside the file, then the assets folders, §9 books), with **what is there now**, worked out when the list is made rather than at the scan: not there; on disk but not scanned (stat'ed in the worker; scan again, or the theme doesn't read that kind of file); missing since a scan; skipped by the folder's settings; offline; the watched folder unreachable; or here now (**Re-read from file** links it). A value that isn't a path says why (a web address, a drive's path).
  - **Can't be drawn** (decided in review: any item's picture, not only covers): a file linked to an item, in any role, that the thumbnail resolver failed to read (corrupt or unsupported), with the error. It is known once something tried to draw it (a grid, a page), and isn't listed once the file changes.
