  - Actions: **Open page**, **Show the file** (the file that names the picture, or the picture), and **Re-read from file** (after fixing the file or a setting; a new picture beside the book is linked by the next scan).
  - **Ignore** (asked for in review; a menu, for the selected rows): **For this item** (until the row says something else: another value, or the damaged file changed; its `marker`), **"value" in every file** (not-found rows: any item whose file names it, case aside, such as a template's `TBD`), or **Every \<type>** (both kinds of row). Each is one undo step (`PictureIgnoreChange`, `picture_ignore`, in the keep's database like ignored file keywords, not `keep.toml`, so undo needs nothing more). **Show ignored** lists ignored rows too, greyed, saying how ("ignored for this item"), and the tab's count leaves them out; **Stop ignoring** removes every rule hiding the selected row (one undo step).
  - **Placeholders** (decided in review): templates often write `cover: None`. A path key's value that is a placeholder, `None`, `null`, `nil`, `N/A`, `-`, `—`, or an empty `[[]]` (case aside, `readers.NO_PATH`), is no value: `read_front_matter` gives `None` for `cover` and `assets`, and an assets folder setting or field with one is none (`core/assets.py`). So such covers are never reported, and `assets: None` names no folder.
- **Skipped** (#527, decided in discussion; `core/skip_reasons.py`): each rule that skips files now, with how many files and how many items it hides (items whose files are all skipped, §6), so the user can see what is hidden and why. One row per rule, in this order: each Skip pattern of each folder (a file under two is listed under both; the tab's count counts each file once); the notes folder (files it doesn't record); folders the theme doesn't record, and each extension it doesn't read (for every folder); inside archives, members of a skipped archive, of one turned off, hidden members, and members of archives no `archives` pattern covers (§6 *Archive members*); and "Read again at the next scan" for files no rule leaves out any more (a rule removed since the last scan). **The reason isn't stored** (decided in discussion): each skipped file is checked against the rules in force now, as `scanner.walk_scope` and the archive choices decide, so the list always says what to change, with no migration and nothing to go stale. Selecting a rule lists its files (the first 10,000) and says where it lives and how to read them again ("In the Skip box of Photos (Keep configuration → Folders). Remove the pattern…"). No command removes a rule from here yet. Decided while building: the tab is always there, like the others, with an empty state when nothing is skipped (the issue had it appear only when something is).
- **New in archives** (#520, M37, designed in discussion, 2026-10-23; `core/new_in_archives.py`): archives read member by member (`archive_read.mode` `chosen`) holding members not decided on (`archive_member_choice.read` null: members a scan found after the choice, §6 *Archive members*), one row per archive (archive, folder, "3 new: .otf, .ttf", when it changed), the most recently changed first. Archives read whole aren't listed (their new members are simply read), nor those read for nothing, skipped, or missing. New members aren't resources until read, so the Skipped tab doesn't list them.
  - **Review…** (or a double-click) opens Choose what to read inside… (§12 *Archives*) on the selected archive: its new members are bold, marked **(new)**, and start hidden, and the summary says how many are new. **Read these** decides every member listed, so new ones left hidden are recorded hidden and the archive leaves the list; Cancel leaves them undecided.
  - **Hide all new** records the selected archives' new members hidden (`hide_new`) without opening the dialog. Not an undo step, like the dialog's choices; Choose what to read inside… shows them again.
  - The tab's count is the number of archives; the dashboard's Untagged card adds "N archives have new files to review", linking to this tab.
- **Links** (#601, §13 *Linked places*): files in linked folders that differ from the file at the same path in the folder they're linked with, so a scan didn't link them (`core.linked_folders.folder_differences`): folder, path, and where the other is; **Open file**, **Open the other**, and **Show in file manager**. The next scan after they're made the same links them. Broken links and shortcuts are listed first (#604, `core.broken_links`): folder, path, and "Broken: pointed to …"; **Show in file manager** shows the link; the next scan that finds the target clears it.
- Counts and the files table are read in a worker, and refreshed after tagging, undo, a scan, or a configuration change.

**Dedupe.** Groups of exact duplicates (fingerprint) and theme-suggested near-duplicates. Side-by-side comparison of files and entities (format, size, resolution/bitrate, tags). Actions: merge entities (§13), keep both files as versions under one entity, or mark "not a duplicate." Never deletes files.

- **Similar items** (#118): a second tab lists the theme's near-duplicate pairs (how alike, the type, both items), most alike first, with a count on the tab and a note when keys were too loose to compare. Double-click opens the first item, and the menu either one.
- **Compare pane** (#119, `core/compare.py`, `ui/compare_view.py`): the page splits, its lists on top and the compare pane below, which follows the current tab's current row; a refresh (after a scan or a tag change) keeps the same group or pair current while it's still listed. A similar pair shows its two items. An identical-files group shows the items using its copies (one column each, at most 6, saying how many more); when every copy belongs to one item it says so and shows that item, whose files row lists the copies. Each column is headed by the item's name, with its thumbnail, then a row per fact: type, name, the theme's detail fields, the user's own fields, tags, how many items it contains (containers only), and its main files (the primary role's, or every role's without one): their formats, total size, and places (at most 5, then "and N more"; folders count, so an album shows where it is). Rows whose values differ are highlighted (bold label, tinted cells); an empty value shows as a dash. Double-clicking a column (or its heading) opens the item. Its **Merge…** button merges the items shown, **Keep both as versions** does so at once, and **Not a duplicate** hides the group or pair (§13).
- **Page** (exact duplicates, #117, `ui/dedupe_view.py`): TOOLS → **Dedupe** shows a summary ("3 groups of identical files; the extra copies take 12.4 MB") over a tree of groups. Each group shows its first name and copy count, the space wasted, its size, and its check result; it expands to its copies, showing where each is, its size, its items, and same / differs. **Check** verifies the selected groups (a copy's row selects its group); it is greyed out, with a tooltip saying why, until something is selected. **Check all** verifies every group (greyed out with none). A copy's menu has Open file, Show in file manager, and Open \<item>, and double-clicking a copy opens its item. The page refreshes after a scan or a change.

**Activity panel.** Scan progress per root, fingerprint and thumbnail queues, offline roots, ingest and thumbnail errors with the affected paths.

- **Where** (#111, `ui/activity.py`): a dock under the results, which can be moved or floated, hidden until asked for. It opens with **View → Activity** (Ctrl+Shift+A) or the status bar's problem badge ("⚠ N", shown when there are problems). It never opens by itself.
- **Now:** the scan's latest step while it runs (fingerprinting is part of each root's scan), then "Last scan, HH:MM: \<summary>", or why it failed.
- **Thumbnails:** "Making thumbnails: N left" while the background queue runs (§6 step 7).
- **Folders:** each root's state: online, offline with the reason, not watched, or not scanned yet. It is read in a worker when the panel opens, after a scan, and after a configuration change.
- **Problems:** a table, newest first: when, what (Can't read, Not added, Warning, No thumbnail, Offline, Scan failed), where (folder › path, or the file's path), and the message.
  - **Show in file manager** (also double-click) for rows with a path.
  - **Copy** puts every row on the clipboard as tab-separated text.
  - **Clear** empties the log.
- **The log** (`core/activity.py`, `KeepSession.problems`): kept for the session only, the newest 1,000. It is safe from any thread, with a version counter the window checks once a second.
  - Scans add what each root's report holds: unreadable folders and files, ingest failures, the theme's warnings, and offline roots.
  - The thumbnail resolver adds files it couldn't make a picture of (`ThumbnailResolver.report`), once each per session, since failures aren't retried.
  - A scan that fails outright is added too.

## 13. Dedupe and entity merge

- **Exact duplicates:** resources with equal fingerprints (optionally verified with a full hash).
  - **Grouping** (#117, `core/dedupe.py`): files with a fingerprint, not missing or skipped, that share it with another. Each group lists its copies with the items using them. Groups come most space wasted first (size × extra copies). Offline copies are listed but can't be checked. Files inside archives (§6 *Archive members*, #524) are grouped too, by the fingerprint made from their contents when they were read, so a font in a zip and a loose copy of it are one group; **Check** reads a member from its archive (up to 256 MB), keyed by the archive's size and time as they are when checked. Duplicates never removes a file, so nothing here touches an archive; hiding a member is done where choosing what to read inside an archive is (#519).
  - **Checking** reads a group's files whole (blake2b) only when asked, in a worker, and splits a group whose files share a fingerprint but differ in the middle. The results are *same*, *differs*, or *couldn't read* (offline, no path here, or an error).
  - Full hashes are kept for the session only (`KeepSession.verifier`), keyed by each file's id and its size and time **as they are when checked** (a quick `stat`, not the last scan's values), so a file changed even since the last scan is read again. Nothing is stored in the keep. A copy that changed since the last scan says so ("changed since the last scan; press F5"); a scan updates the groups.
- **Near-duplicates:** the theme's `similarity(a, b)` hook scores candidate pairs within a type (music: normalized artist + title + duration; images: a perceptual hash). The core only compares candidates produced by cheap blocking keys the theme supplies, never all pairs.
  - **Theme API** (#118): `Theme.blocking_keys(type, record) -> Iterable[str]` (cheap, from the record's fields; the default gives none), `Theme.similarity(type, a, b) -> float` from 0 to 1, and `Theme.near_duplicate_threshold` (default 0.9).
  - **Finding** (`core/dedupe.near_duplicates`): each type's items are read once as records and bucketed by key; pairs sharing a key are scored once each. Pairs at or above the threshold are listed, most alike first. A key shared by more than 200 items is skipped (too loose to help, and slow) and counted. A theme hook that raises is logged and skips that item or pair.
  - **music:** songs keyed by normalized artist and title. Two on the same album are different tracks (versions are already one song), so they score 0. Otherwise the score is 1 within 2 seconds of length (or with a length unknown), falling to 0 at 30 seconds apart.
  - **assets2d** (theme version 2): images get a 64-bit difference hash (`phash`, a field not shown on pages), computed when the image is read (decoded small: JPEGs with `draft`). Keys are the hash's four 16-bit quarters, so hashes within 3 bits always share one. The field also holds the picture's average color (6 more hex digits; transparent parts count as mid grey), because the difference hash sees only light and dark: two pictures whose colors are more than 40 apart (RGB distance) score 0, so a recolored icon of the same layout doesn't pair. Otherwise similarity is the share of bits that match; the threshold is 58/64 (at most 6 bits differ). Existing keeps read every file again once after the upgrade, to fill in the hash.
- **Entity merge:** the kept entity absorbs the other's tags, role links, containment edges, relationships, and `user`-provenance field values (conflicts are shown for the user to choose). The merged entity id is recorded so saved searches and history can redirect. Files are never touched.
  - **Doing it** (#120, `core/merge.py`): items of one type only. The kept item takes the others' tags, containers and contents (cycles are refused by the closure), and relationships (in a `many=False` relationship it keeps its own partner). Their files move to it as links made by hand (`by_user`), so scans never move them back; in a one-file role that the kept item already fills, the other's file is left linked to nothing and Triage lists it as unlinked. Hand-set values (fields, the title, extra fields) come along; where two items' hand-set values differ, the user picks one in the dialog (the kept item's by default). Extracted values are the kept item's own. The others are deleted and recorded in `entity_merge` (§5).
  - **Staying merged:** a scan that reads a merged item's file again doesn't recreate it, whether the theme finds items by key (`ctx.upsert`, music's songs) or through their files (`ctx.entities_of`, assets2d's pictures; §9). The kept item's values keep coming from its own files. Found testing on the assets demo: remembering keys alone let a merged picture come back.
  - **Redirects:** a page open on a merged item (or opened from history) shows the item it went into, with a note, while the merge stands (each refresh asks for the item opened, so after an undo the page is that item's own again); a saved search *within* a merged item searches within the kept one (`search.py`, one step through `entity_merge`).
  - **Undo:** one step (`MergeChange`): whole-item snapshots before and after of every item touched (the kept one, the merged ones, and their neighbours), plus the `entity_merge` rows written and repointed.
  - **Dialog** (`ui/merge_dialog.py`): the compare pane's **Merge…** (two or more items; greyed out, saying why, when their types differ) opens it. Radio buttons choose the item to keep (the left column by default; each named with its first file's place, since titles often match). The plan (`plan_merge`, in a worker, again when the kept item changes) says what it gains ("1 tag, 1 file and 2 containers"), lists conflicting hand-set values with a radio button per item, and lists files that will be left linked to nothing.
- "Keep both as versions" links the second file to the kept entity under the same primary role.
  - **Done as a merge** (#121): Merge already moves the other items' files into the kept item's roles, so a song's copies become its versions. **Keep both as versions** (or *Keep all*, for more than two) in the compare pane merges into the left item at once with the defaults (its own hand-set values win; no dialog), and is offered only when that is what happens: items of one type whose primary role holds several files (`Comparison.versions`). For one-file roles (pictures) only Merge… is offered, since a second file has nowhere to go.
- **Not a duplicate** (#121, `core/not_duplicates.py`): the compare pane's **Not a duplicate** hides the current identical-files group or similar pair from the Dedupe page (`dedupe_dismissal`, §5). A group is remembered with its files and shows again once they change (a third copy, one gone), since the user judged only the copies there were; a pair stays hidden. Each tab's summary says how many are hidden; **Show dismissed (n)**, beside the tabs, lists them greyed, where the button reads **Show as a duplicate again**. Marking and unmarking are each one undo step (`NotDuplicateChange`).

### Linked places (M48, #597; decided in discussion, 2026-10-30)

One file or folder can be in several places: a symbolic link, a junction, a hard link, a Windows shortcut, or a link a share resolves itself and shows as an ordinary file or folder (a Linux server's symbolic links over SMB). Users organize with these to file one thing in several places (a bundle's folder, and the author's), and want it tracked as one thing that still shows up in each place. **Linked places** make that one item; Merge (above) stays for two copies that are one thing (two downloads of a book).

- **One item, several places** (#598): the places stay resources of their own (each with its root and path, its status, offline or missing on its own), linked as a group to its first place (a new table, core format 33: each linked place, the place it is the same as, and how it was linked: a symbolic link, junction, hard link, shortcut, by the user, or inside linked folders; linking to a linked place links to its group's first place). The places share one item, so its tags, ratings, notes, and the user's values are shared, never copied, and a search lists it once.
  - **Filed where each place belongs:** a scan reads each place as the theme reads any path, but a linked place makes no item of its own: it joins the group's item, and the containers the theme finds from its path (an author's or an artist's folder) are added to that item, so it is listed under each, as before the link, and inherits tags from all of them. Its fields come from the group's first place (the same file).
  - **Shown and opened:** the item's page lists its other places ("Also at …"). Opening a file, or revealing it, uses the first place that can be reached; an item is offline or missing only when every place is. Identical files that are places of one item aren't duplicates (Duplicates leaves them out). A moved place keeps its link (move detection, §6).
  - **As built** (#598; `core/places.py`, `core/place_links.py`): **`linked_place`** (§5) holds each linked place with its group's first place and how it was linked. `link_place` links a place (to a linked place's first place; a first place linked elsewhere brings its places along; a file only to a file, a folder to a folder), and folds an item the place was a file of into the group's item of its type in that role, as a merge with the defaults (the group's item's hand-set values win); the containers the group's item gains that way are recorded as the place's. The place is read again at the next scan.
  - **Place mode** (`IngestSession(place=(place, first))`): a scan reads the root's linked places after its other files, one at a time, once their first place has been read (a first place with no item leaves nothing to file: the place is marked read; members of an archive that is a place are marked read too). `ctx.entities_of(place)` gives the first place's items (links by hand included). Writes to the group's items' values, links, keywords, notes, and relations are dropped (the first place, the same file, gives them), a link to the place itself is dropped, and nothing that takes away is done (`uncontain`, `unrelate`, `unlink`, `delete`): a theme that moves an item to the folder its path names would otherwise take it out of the first place's. A `contain` touching a group item is applied and recorded in **`place_contains`** (§5) unless the item had the edge from its first place already; edges the place recorded and no longer gives are removed, unless another place gives them or the user put them there. Other scans' `uncontain` leaves an edge a place gives.
  - **Elsewhere:** `detail.role_files` lists each file's places after it (`FileRow.place_of`), so the page shows them ("Also at …", indented, no size), and opening or revealing an item's file takes the first one there (`choose_file`); Triage's missing files leave out an item with a place still there, unlinked files and Duplicates leave places out, the compare pane counts a file's size once, and move detection never takes a place for a new file, and moves a place's link, its group's, and its containers with it (`scanner._move_places`). Inside places (#616): an archive that is a place brings its members along, each linked (`inside`) to the member at the same path in the first place's archive, when linking (`_link`) and after each scan reads members (`link_place_members`); unlinking the archive unlinks them, and **Unlink this place** gives each an item of its own too (a merged item isn't made again when its file is read, so the members need copies as the archive does). A thumbnail whose file can't be read (its folder offline) is drawn from another place of it that can (`_reachable_place`, read through shortcuts). Gallery folders needed nothing: a linked folder's pictures are places, filed nowhere of their own (checked with the movies theme's folders and movies).
- **Real links are linked automatically** (#599): symbolic links to files and folders, and junctions, are linked to their target; folders behind them are walked now (they were skipped, §6), each path inside linked to the target's, with a guard against loops (a link to a folder it is inside, or to one already walked). Hard links are linked when the disk says two names are one file (NTFS, Linux disks); shares that can't say fall back to matching contents.
  - **As built** (#599, `core/real_links.py`): the walk (`walk_root`) reports each path reached through a link with its real path and how (`WalkEntry.target`, `how`: `symlink`, `junction`, `inside`); folder links are walked when their target is in one of the keep's roots (`link_roots`, real paths; the scan passes them all, `scan_root(roots=...)`), and not when the target holds a folder on the way there (the real paths of the branch are kept, so links leading to each other stop too) or is gone. With `hard_links` (always on Linux and macOS, where the listing's `stat` says; on Windows only for local roots, since it costs a second `stat`), a file with more than one name gets its `(device, file number)`. After the root's diff is applied and moves found, `sync_links` links each such path to the resource at its real path (in whichever root holds it, the deepest first, matched ignoring case on Windows) and the names of one file to one of them (a first place already, else one with an item); a link already made isn't made again, and one the user made isn't changed. A path linked this way that the walk finds as itself again is unlinked (`unlink_place`: the containers only it gave go, and it is read as a file of its own). Decided while building: links to a path not recorded (skipped, or an extension the theme doesn't take) do nothing; hard links are found within a root's walk only; the paths in a linked folder are fingerprinted as any (twice the reading for a linked folder's files on a first scan).
- **Windows shortcuts** (#602): a `.lnk` file is read as a link to its target (its relative path, else its local path, else its network path; a small reader, no new dependency), not listed as a file of its own: a shortcut to a file is a place of that file, one to a folder is walked as a linked folder.
  - **As built** (#602, `core/shortcuts.py`): a reader of the Shell Link format ([MS-SHLLINK]: the header's flags and the target's attributes, LinkInfo's local and network paths, the relative path in the string data), on any OS. `shortcut_target` takes the first of the relative path (from the shortcut's folder), the local path, and the network path that is there. The walk (`walk_root`) reads each `.lnk` file: one that isn't a shortcut is listed as any file; one whose target is gone is left out (#604 lists them); one to a file is listed at the shortcut's own path with the target's extension, size, and time (`how` `shortcut`), so the theme's extensions decide as for the target; one to a folder is walked as a linked folder (inside the keep's folders, with the loop guard), its entries' paths going through the shortcut (`Jane/Book.lnk/x.pdf`, `inside`). `sync_links` then links them as it does symbolic links (`shortcut` is one of the ways the scan makes and unmakes itself). Paths through a shortcut exist only through it, so scans read them with `on_disk` (fingerprints, and the path a theme reads). Decided while building: a place keeps the shortcut's name (`forest.png.lnk`), the path on disk; a deleted shortcut is a missing place, as a deleted file is missing (rule 7).
- **Links the share hides** are linked by the user, from their contents:
  - **Link as one** (#600): Duplicates' **Identical files** and the compare pane offer it beside Merge…. Places that were separate items become one as Merge makes them (the user's values chosen the same way); the places are linked, so later scans keep them together. **Unlink** on the item's page separates a place again: it becomes an item of its own, with a copy of the shared tags and the user's values. Linking and unlinking are undo steps.
    - **As built** (#600, `core/place_links.py`): `link_as_one` links each copy after the first to the first's group (`how` `user`), the group's first copy as listed (by folder, then path) being the first place; the copies' items are merged into the first's item of their type as `link_place` does (its hand-set values win: no dialog). The compare pane's **Link as one** (Identical files only) is greyed once **Check** found the copies different. `split_place` (the **Unlink this place** entry of an Also at row's menu) gives the place an item of its own per group item: a copy of the group item's snapshot (§9 *Actions*: title, values, the user's values and own fields, tags, a note typed in the app, relationships but a one-partner one's other side), with no ingest key, linked to the place in the same role, in the containers the place gave; the place is then unlinked (`unlink_place`) and read as any file. Both are a `PlaceChange`: the place tables' rows about the resources before and after, the merges, and the copies' snapshots; undo restores the snapshots and merges, then the rows. `link_place` (scans, #599) returns one too, unrecorded. Decided while building: the copy has no ingest key, so a theme keying items by their details (music's songs) can find the group's item again and make the place a version of it.
  - **Linked folders** (#601): Duplicates' **Linked folders** tab suggests folders with the same name whose files match by name, size, and fingerprint. A preview lists the pairs that match, the files that differ, and those on one side only. **Files that differ block linking**, with a warning: two places of one file can't really differ, so either they are copies, one edited, or the reads were wrong (the file changing between them, a network glitch); **Check again** reads them whole, which settles the second. Linking pairs what's inside by path in each folder, subfolders too. A file that turns up later in both folders is linked by the scan when its fingerprints match (asked in discussion); one that differs goes to Triage instead.
    - **As built** (#601, `core/linked_folders.py`): a new table, **`linked_folder`** (core format 34, §5), holds each linked folder with the folder it is linked with (roots and paths: a theme may not record folders as resources). `suggest_linked_folders` looks at files sharing a fingerprint (not empty, not linked places, at most 20 to a fingerprint: more is a common file, a licence, that says nothing) with the same name, and for each two takes every pair of folders up from them whose names still match (`Bundle/Book/Shop` and `Authors/Jane/Book/Shop` give `Shop` and `Book`), leaving out a pair when the pair just above it matches completely (nothing differs, nothing on one side only: linking it links them), so a folder that matches inside one that doesn't is suggested on its own (asked for in review); folders inside each other, and pairs linked already or inside a linked pair, are left out. `folder_pair` compares what's inside by path: the same (size and fingerprint, or folders in both, or linked already), differing (a file and a folder count as differing), and on one side only. `link_folders` records the pair (the second folder linked to the first, in order of root name and path) and links each path of the second that matches as a place of the first's (`how` `inside`, merging items as `link_place` does), refusing while files differ, except pairs `confirm_same` (**Check again**: both read whole, as Dedupe's Check) found the same; one undo step, the `PlaceChange` carrying the `linked_folder` rows before and after (found in review, fixed in #616: the next scan's `sync_links` took the pairs for links found as files again and unlinked them, its `inside` being one of the ways it unmakes; it now leaves paths in linked folders alone). After a root's links are synced, `sync_linked_folders` links the scan's new and changed paths inside a linked folder to the same path in the other, when that is there and matches (the one there before keeps the item; when both are new, the second folder's joins the first's); a file that differs is left, and Triage's **Links** tab lists it (`folder_differences`). Dedupe's **Linked folders** tab dismisses a pair as **Not the same folder** (`dedupe_dismissal` kind `folders`). Decided while building: only new and changed paths are linked by a scan, so a place the user unlinked stays unlinked; folders aren't followed when they move (the pair is kept by path); archive members, gallery folders, and thumbnails inside places are still to come (#616).
- **Targets outside the keep's folders** (#603): a link or shortcut whose target is outside every root. The first scan that finds such links asks once ("3 links point outside the keep's folders. Include them?"; asked in discussion); included, a target is read as if it were where the link is (its paths stay relative to the link's root, §5), left out, it is skipped. The answer is kept in `keep.toml` and can be changed in Keep configuration.
  - **As built** (#603): `[folders] outside_links` in `keep.toml` (`true`, `false`, or absent: not asked, and skipped meanwhile; `KeepConfig.outside_links`). The walk (`walk_root(outside_links=..., on_outside=...)`) reports each link or shortcut to a file or folder outside the keep's roots (real paths) and reads it only when included: a file at the link's path, a folder walked with the loop guard, its entries at paths through the link (`inside`, whose real paths are in no root, so they aren't linked places: files of their own). Each scan's report lists them (`ScanReport.outside_links`); after a scan, the window asks when the keep has no answer and some were found, saves the answer, and scans again on Include. Keep configuration's Keep tab has **Links outside** (Ask at the next scan that finds some, Include them, Leave them out). Decided while building: Escape is Leave out (asked once); links to files outside, read before #603, are skipped until included, and missing meanwhile (rule 7).
- **Broken links** (#604): a link or shortcut whose target is gone (deleted, renamed, moved, or on a drive that isn't connected) is listed in Triage with where it pointed, so the user can fix it on disk; a scan that finds the target again clears it.
  - **As built** (#604, `core/broken_links.py`): the walk reports each symbolic link, junction, or shortcut whose target is gone (`walk_root(on_broken=...)`: its path, how, and where it pointed; a shortcut's paths it tried, one per line), and leaves it out. After a root's walk, the scan replaces the root's rows in **`broken_link`** (core format 35, §5) with what it found, so a link whose target is back drops off; an offline root keeps its rows. Triage's **Links** tab lists them before the files that differ (Problem: "Broken: pointed to …"); Open file says the target is gone, and **Show in file manager** shows the link. Decided while building: a broken link that was a place before stays a missing place (rule 7), listed here too; links skipped because they lead outside the keep (#603) aren't broken.
- **Nothing is written** under a root (rule 1): links are read, never made, changed, or removed; linking is recorded in the keep only.

## 14. Future work

- **The books theme and file keywords** (M20, #293): §7 *File keywords*, §9 *Built-in themes* (books), §4 *Writing back to files*.
- **Works from a link alone**: a book with no file at all, made by pasting an address ("New book from link…"). It needs works made by hand, an exemption from Missing files, and an answer for where the link lives; link files (§9 books) cover the common case meanwhile.
- **Archive members** (M37): read as resources since #391 (§6 *Archive members*), and shown, opened, drawn, and compared wherever a file is since #524; chosen from right-click menus and the Choose what to read inside… dialog since #519; new members of archives read member by member reviewed in Triage's **New in archives** since #520.
- **Online pictures**: covers per edition from Open Library, people's and films' pictures from TMDb (with a key), on the online-details hooks (§9) and with the keep's consent; kept in the keep's cache, never written to the user's folders.
- **Write to file… for notes**: tags applied in Tagalot written into an item's note (folder note or notes folder), in writable folders.
- **Research** (M21, #306): designed in review; see §9 *Built-in themes*, research. Later: papers without files (from a library export's entries alone, as works from a link alone above), reading Zotero's database directly, more citation styles.
- **Online details** (M22, #307): designed in review; see §9 *Online details*. Later: movies (TMDb) and music (MusicBrainz) on the same hooks, and covers from Open Library.
- **Search inside documents** (M23, #308): designed in review; see §8 *Search inside documents*. Later: opening a file at the matched page, ordering by relevance, OCR for scanned PDFs.
- **Rust hot spots** via PyO3 if profiling justifies them (profiled in #131: not yet, §3 Performance).
- **Entry-point theme packaging** and a theme template/cookiecutter.
- Scheduled background rescans.

## 15. Open questions

1. ~~Should `aggregate_up` be exposed in the main filter bar or only in an advanced menu?~~ Answered (#133): the main filter bar, as **By contents**, beside Contained and Inherit tags; a view can start it on.
2. ~~How should entity merges interact with tag inheritance when the two entities have different parents?~~ Answered (#120): the kept item is in every container either was in, so it inherits tags from all of them (§13).
3. ~~Is there a need for tags scoped to one entity type, or are all tags global to the keep?~~ Answered (#135): optional, per tag, inherited down (§7 *Tag types*).
4. ~~Packaging: PyInstaller vs. Briefcase vs. Nuitka for distributable builds.~~ Answered (#129): PyInstaller (§3 Packaging).

## 16. Decisions log

| Date | Decision |
|---|---|
| 2026-09 | Python + PySide6 + SQLAlchemy 2.0 + SQLite; Rust only for isolated hot spots. |
| 2026-09 | Tags attach to entities, not files. Entities link to resources through roles. |
| 2026-09 | Containment is a DAG with a core-maintained closure table. |
| 2026-09 | Tag inheritance and "show contained items" are per-view defaults with user toggles. |
| 2026-09 | Themes are single Python files importing only `tagalot.themes.api`. |
| 2026-09 | Files open via the OS default handler; per-user overrides by extension. |
| 2026-09 | The app never modifies, moves, renames, or deletes user files. |
| 2026-09 | Tag sibling-name uniqueness is enforced by a unique index on `(coalesce(parent_id, 0), lower(name))`, plus a `casefold()` check in `tags.py` for clear errors. A plain `(parent_id, lower(name))` constraint would not cover root-level tags, because SQLite treats NULLs as distinct. |
| 2026-09 | Closure maintenance recomputes the ancestors of affected nodes (descendants of each changed edge's child) from `entity_contains` with a recursive CTE, for both additions and removals. Path counting was rejected because it cannot maintain minimum depth. `rebuild_all()` serves as the repair action and test oracle (§6). |
| 2026-09 | Theme entity classes are plain declarations (annotated fields, no SQLAlchemy). When a keep opens, the core builds the theme's tables as Core `Table` objects in a fresh `MetaData`. Themes access data only through `ctx` with `EntityRef` handles, and relationships are declared with `related()`. This isolates themes (a broken theme affects only its keeps), allows reloading, and keeps SQLAlchemy out of the public API. Rejected: theme classes as ORM subclasses in a shared registry, because mapping at import lets one broken theme break every keep. Added `entity.ingest_key` for idempotent re-ingest (§5, §9). |
| 2026-09 | keep.toml: strings are written as TOML literal strings where possible so Windows and UNC paths stay hand-editable; root paths are stored as raw strings; unknown keys are ignored with a warning and dropped on rewrite; writes are atomic (§4). |
| 2026-09 | Per-user settings live in a non-roaming config dir and never block startup: unparseable files are set aside as `settings.toml.invalid`, bad entries are skipped with a warning (§4). |
| 2026-09 | Keep creation requires a new or empty folder and refuses a keep and root that contain each other. Network detection for the keep-location warning uses UNC/`GetDriveTypeW` on Windows and mount file-system types on Linux/macOS, and fails open (§4). |
| 2026-09 | Network keeps use `journal_mode=DELETE` instead of WAL. Read-only engines set `query_only`. The pysqlite driver's own transaction handling is disabled so DDL runs inside transactions (§4). |
| 2026-09 | Core schema conventions: `resource.mtime_ns` as integer nanoseconds; UTC-aware timestamps; enum columns as text with CHECK constraints; link rows cascade on delete, while deleting a tag with children or a root with resources is refused (§5). |
| 2026-09 | Core schema versions: a newer keep is refused; an older one needs user confirmation, then is backed up with SQLite's backup API and migrated in a single transaction; `keep.toml` `format_version` tracks the database (§5). |
| 2026-09 | Root reachability: a root is online if its folder can be listed within 10 s, checked on a daemon thread so a hung share can be abandoned; offline marks only `ok` resources `offline` (§6). |
| 2026-09 | Walking: exclude globs are case-insensitive with `**` semantics and prune matching folders; symlinked folders and junctions are not followed; read errors are reported and skipped (§6). |
| 2026-09 | Scan diff: resources under unreadable folders are not marked missing; returning files keep their id; `last_seen_at` moves only for resources actually seen. Newly excluded resources became `missing` until #152; they are now flagged `skipped` (§6). |
| 2026-09 | Fingerprints are 16-byte blake2b digests; files up to 128 KiB are hashed whole; results are stored only if size and mtime still match (§5). |
| 2026-09 | Move detection matches unlinked new files to missing files by fingerprint across roots with no time window; ambiguous groups pair only by unique file name, never by guess (§6). |
| 2026-09 | The DB writer is a dedicated thread applying `Callable[[Connection], T]` jobs in their own transactions and returning futures; scans are applied in parts of at most 500 changes (§6). |
| 2026-09 | `scan_root()` orchestrates §6 steps 1–4 per root: one fingerprint pass per scan, move detection limited to the scan's new files, and a `ScanReport` for the UI (§6). |
| 2026-09 | The tag tree is cached as an immutable snapshot, reloaded only after explicit invalidation by tag operations; tag names compare with `str.casefold()` (§7). |
| 2026-09 | Tag merge merges clashing children recursively and is refused into a descendant; promoting children on delete is refused on name clashes; operations validate against a fresh tree inside their transaction (§7). |
| 2026-09 | Tag undo/redo restores snapshots of the tag tables plus the operation's own `entity_tag` delta instead of computing inverse operations (§7). |
| 2026-09 | `SearchSpec` is frozen and uses tuples (hashable); field filters are `TextFilter` (contains or starts-with)/`RangeFilter`/`ChoiceFilter`; its JSON is versioned with tagged dates (§8). |
| 2026-09 | Search: a deleted tag in a spec is a subtree of itself (include matches nothing, exclude excludes nothing); field names resolve through a column mapping (core: title/created_at/updated_at); results always tie-break on entity id (§8). |
| 2026-09 | Closure API: `apply` (removals first, ordered cycle checks, returns rejections), `add_entities` for self rows, and `detach` before deleting entities; affected ids use a temporary table (§6). |
| 2026-09 | Containment search: `within` is strict; `aggregate_up` matches tags and text via non-excluded descendants while field filters and exclusion apply to the container; `show_contained` adds descendants regardless of types, subject only to exclusion (§8). |
| 2026-09 | Search performance: set tests are uncorrelated `IN` subqueries over materialized CTEs; closure cycle checks run only for edges into entities that have children. Measured at 50.5k entities: 1–47 ms for single options, ~105 ms with every option at once (§6, §8). |
| 2026-09 | Theme API v1: stdlib-only module; fields as annotated `field()` attributes; frozen declaration objects for roles, containment, relationships, views, sections; `IngestContext` as a protocol implemented by the core (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Theme tables: non-nullable scalar fields get database defaults, non-nullable dates are rejected, range/choice fields are indexed, names are namespaced by theme id, and all problems are reported together (§5). |
| 2026-09 | Theme loading never raises: unique module names per file, per-file problems, first duplicate id wins, built-in placeholders skipped, full declaration validation plus a trial table build (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Theme versions mirror core versions (keep.toml + `schema_version`, backup, one-transaction migrate). The core applies additive schema changes on every open, so `Theme.migrate()` now defaults to a no-op (API change) and is only for data changes (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Ingest context: provenance-respecting upserts (title included), `IngestError` for theme mistakes, single-valued roles and `many=False` relationships replace, containment and index updates batched until `flush()` (§9). |
| 2026-09 | `IngestContext` gains `update(ref, …)` and `entities_of(resource, role)` (before API v1 ships): file-based themes identify entities by their linked file, so moves keep tags and a new file at a vacated path can't take over a moved file's entity (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Scan ingest: `resource.ingested_at` tracks pending resources so failures retry next scan; batches of 100 per writer transaction, with per-resource retry on failure; theme file reading moves to a worker `prepare()` hook later (#176) (§6). |
| 2026-09 | The UI works with an open keep through `KeepSession`; Qt workers deliver every result on the GUI thread via a relay object; scans report progress through a callback (§6). |
| 2026-09 | Main window: collapsible grouped navigation (fold state per keep in `ui_state.json`), "Scan now" in a slim toolbar and Keep menu (F5), scan progress in the status bar. User theme files are always compiled from source (no `.pyc`), so a quick same-size edit can't reload stale code (§9, §12). |
| 2026-09 | New roots get default exclude patterns for OS and NAS leftovers (`.DS_Store`, `._*`, `Thumbs.db`, `@eaDir`…), written explicitly into `keep.toml` so they stay visible and editable (§4). |
| 2026-09 | Search results: a lazily paged table model (count first, pages of 100 on demand, 50 kept); the list layout's columns are the title, the type for mixed scopes, and the scope's common `card` fields; theme fields enter searches as primary-key scalar subqueries, so sorting and filtering need no joins (§8, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Filter bar: one row with a text box and a tag box, chips wrapping below; Enter includes a suggested tag, Shift+Enter excludes it; text applies after 300 ms (§12). The demo keep is created scanned and tagged, so tag filters can be tried before the tagging panel exists. |
| 2026-09 | Search all groups results by type: a section per type with its count, first 8 matches, and own columns; "Show all" narrows to that type with an "Only" chip; one matching type shows its full list directly (§8, §12). `make_demo_keep.py --media` builds a three-type demo keep with a tiny theme installed in the user themes folder. |
| 2026-09 | Theme API (still `API_VERSION = 1`, additive with defaults): `Entity.label` and `Entity.plural` name types in the UI; the plural defaults to regular English rules, and a blank value is a theme problem (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Closing a keep's window closes the keep (it stayed open until Tagalot quit); the read-only engine uses no connection pool, so a query finishing after the close can't keep the file open. GUI smoke tests drive the app end to end (launcher → new keep → F5 → search; rescans; an offline root; the command line), and every test runs with private settings and user-themes folders (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Tagging panel tree: filtering shows matches (bold) with their ancestors, all expanded, and restores the user's folds when cleared; alias matches name the alias (§12). Finding tags ignores accents as well as case, like the search box; name uniqueness still uses case-folding only (§7). |
| 2026-09 | Tags get an optional description (core format 2, the first core migration): shown in tooltips and matched when finding tags (panel filter, suggestions), not when searching items; edited in the tag manager (M7) (§5, §7). |
| 2026-09 | Tagging by drag and drop: a drop on a selected row tags the selection, elsewhere just that row; drops are undoable (Edit menu, Ctrl+Z) and record only their own rows; tag operations run one at a time off the GUI thread (§7, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Result lists get an optional Tags column (hidden by default, next to the title when shown) and a header menu to show or hide any column, remembered per view in `ui_state.json` (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Keyboard tagging: Enter / Shift+Enter in the Tags panel apply or remove tags on the page's selection; Ctrl+T focuses the Tags filter from anywhere (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Selection summary: tri-state checks on the tagging panel count direct tags only, recomputed in a worker after the selection settles or tags change; a click requests apply-to-all or remove-from-all (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Creating tags from the Tags filter: a typed path with " > " or "›" nests the new tag, creating missing parents in one undo step, and the new tag is applied to any selected items (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Tags panel context menu: add the tag to the search (include or "but not"), or apply / remove it on the selection; covers every selected tag in the tree (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Tag manager: tree with Items / With sub-tags counts plus a details pane for the selected tag (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Tag manager moves aren't confirmed first; they're undoable and report the affected item count in the status bar (§7, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Tag merge and delete dialogs explain the effect and count affected items from the tag manager's usage figures (no database wait); deleting a tag with sub-tags defaults to deleting the subtree (§7, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Tag colors, descriptions, and aliases are edited in the tag manager's details pane; unsaved descriptions are kept per tag across reloads (§12). |
| 2026-09 | `thumbs.db` is written by thumbnail workers directly (lock-serialized), not through the keep's DB writer, since it is a disposable cache; it is versioned with `user_version` and recreated when damaged or from another version. Rows also store the image's width and height (§10). |
| 2026-09 | Thumbnail providers are subclassable (`ThumbnailProvider.candidates(entity, ctx)`) and only choose resources; the core renders a resource by its kind. So cache keys use the renderer's id and version rather than the provider's, and the memo stores just the chosen resource. Resource kinds are classified by extension in the API (`kind_of`). Ingest links clear the memo (§9, §10). |
| 2026-09 | `FolderImage` looks only at scanned resources (via `ThumbnailContext.folder_files`), never lists folders itself, so its choice can be memoized and shares aren't walked again. `EmbeddedAudioArt` on an entity without audio (an album) tries its first three contained items with audio, by title (§10). |
| 2026-09 | Archives are recognized by content, not extension. The budget is by size (64 MB per image, 256 MB of solid data before it) rather than time, since py7zr and rarfile can't be interrupted. A missing rar tool is logged once and the archive shows its icon, rather than reporting every rar file (§10). |
| 2026-09 | Grid layout: thumbnail cards sharing the list's model and selection, switched by header buttons (remembered per view). Card text is the title plus lines the theme declares per entity (`card_lines`) and the user can change per view. Thumbnail sizes: the theme sets `thumbnail_max` (cache resolution; a keep can override it in `keep.toml`) and `thumbnail_default`; the user picks presets from View → Thumbnail size or zooms with Ctrl+wheel, remembered per keep. Thumbnails load on a dedicated 4-thread pool, newest request first (§4, §9, §10, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Clearing the thumbnail cache is in the Keep menu until the Keep configuration window exists; it keeps the memoized sources and retries files that failed (§10). |
| 2026-09 | Themes read files in an optional `prepare(batch)` run by the scan worker outside the write transaction; `ingest` gets the results through `ctx.prepared(resource)`, so its signature is unchanged. Done in M9 (for assets2d) rather than M12 (§6, §9). |
| 2026-09 | assets2d models kinds as separate types (Image, Font, Archive) under Artist rather than one Asset with a kind field, so each has its own fields and views scope by type without a view-filter feature. Artists are keyed by folder name across roots; fonts get a rendered sample thumbnail; thumbnails default to a 1024 max, 256 start (§9, §10). |
| 2026-09 | Theme options: declared with `option()`, set per keep in `[theme.options]` and per root in `options = { … }`, read in ingest with `ctx.option()`. Roots record the values they were ingested with (core format 3 adds `root.ingest_options`) and are ingested again when they change. Ingest can delete entities it made (`ctx.delete`, with `ctx.contents`/`ctx.linked` to see what's left), so assets2d removes artists a level change leaves empty (§4, §6, §9). |
| 2026-09 | assets2d's asset views inherit tags from the artist (`inherit_tags=True`); the Artists view doesn't (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Detail pages: a header (thumbnail, title, type) above the sections stacked full width; types without a `DetailView` get fields, primary role, and contents. Until M13, double-click opens every item's page (§12). |
| 2026-09 | A container's page embeds a full search of its contents below the sections (a splitter between); its view settings are shared by all pages of that type; tagging applies to the selected contents, else the page's entity (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Back/forward history of every page shown (100 steps; 30 detail pages kept alive); breadcrumbs follow the first container by title and mention the others (§12). |
| 2026-09 | A Within chip keeps the current search and its tag filters; a view keeps its types if the container can hold them, else lists everything it holds; Search all stays grouped. Contents (on detail pages and within) include every depth (§12). |
| 2026-09 | The preview is a strip under the results (not a dock): thumbnail, title, type, card fields, primary file; View → Preview toggles it for the keep (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Contained / Inherit tags are checkboxes on the filter row, remembered per view and kept by Clear all. The tree layout's top level is the results; containers expand to their direct contents (all types, by title, minus excluded items), so Contained is off in the tree. A Type column appears whenever listed contents may be of other types (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Fields and titles are edited in place on detail pages (double-click or ✎; Enter saves, Esc cancels); each edit sets provenance `user` and is one step in the shared Edit → Undo history, which restores the previous provenance too. File-derived fields in the built-in themes are `editable=False`. Datetimes typed without a zone are local time, stored in UTC (§5, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Field filters: a "+ Field" button with the scope's searchable fields, a popup per field (choice values among the results with counts, excluding the field's own filter; From/To; contains/starts with), and purple chips that are kept but greyed when the scope loses the field (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Extra fields are a section on every detail page (after Details), text only, edited in place, added with a name/value row, each change one undo step (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Re-reading items from their files: two menu actions (keep edits, or replace them after asking); it ingests the linked files at once without a walk, and is one undo step snapshotting title, fields, and provenance before and after (§5, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Fields may declare a display format, `bytes` or `duration`, applied everywhere values are shown but never to sorting, filtering, or editing; the built-in themes show Size as a size (§9, §12). Range filters still take the raw number; accepting units ("> 10 MB") could come later. |
| 2026-09 | Music theme: albums are folders, with disc folders (`CD1`, `Disc 2`) part of their parent's album; songs are keyed by album, disc, track, and normalized title so formats of one song are versions; an album's artist is ALBUMARTIST else ARTIST, and "Various Artists" when its songs disagree, with compilation songs also under their own artist; empty albums and artists are deleted (§9). Within an ingest batch the last `contain`/`uncontain` of an edge wins (§9 ingest context). |
| 2026-09 | Containers order their contents with `Entity.contents_sort` (theme API), used by the tree layout's children and the container's page; the tree's top level leaves out matches held by another match (`SearchSpec.nest`), so a view of albums and songs shows songs under their albums rather than twice. Music views: Browse (tree), Artists and Albums (grids), Songs (list) (§8, §9). |
| 2026-09 | Opening files: an item opens the first available file of its primary role; Open file, Show in file manager, and Open with… are in result menus, the detail page's More menu, and each file row's menu; Open with… is Windows' own dialog, else a program picked once (§11). |
| 2026-09 | Handler overrides are made from the Open with submenu (Always open .ext files with… / Stop using …), filled once the file is known; templates split on spaces with double quotes and no backslash escapes, a template without placeholders gets the path appended; per-role and custom templates are hand-edited in settings.toml (§11). |
| 2026-09 | Theme actions get an `ActionContext`: the ingest context's writes (one undo step per run, by whole-entity snapshots of what it touched), plus contents in order, files with paths, a temp folder per session, and open/reveal/message outputs carried out after the commit. They run in the DB writer and appear in result menus and as detail-page buttons (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Double-click and Enter follow the type's `double_click`; Ctrl+Enter does the other; the menu's "Open" is now "Open page", with the double-click choice shown bold; the preview strip's double-click keeps opening the page (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Play album writes `.m3u8` (UTF-8) rather than `.m3u`, since paths and titles may not be ASCII; a new file per run, so a player holding the last playlist doesn't block the next (§9). |
| 2026-09 | Removing a root is the user's choice each time: stop watching it and keep its items (the default; `watched = false` in keep.toml, reconnected by watching or adding the folder again), or delete its items after counts and a second confirmation. Configuration edits are saved immediately. Scans never delete (§4, §12). |
| 2026-10 | Theme authors (#130): a guide (docs/THEMES.md), a working template theme shipped with the app, and `--new-theme` / `--check-theme` commands, rather than a cookiecutter or an in-app theme editor (§9). |
| 2026-10 | Packaging with PyInstaller (#129), after building with PyInstaller, Nuitka, and Briefcase on Windows: fastest build (57 s), smallest (147 MB), startup as from source; per-OS one-folder builds in CI, each smoke-tested with the new `tagalot --check`, attached to the `v*` release whose tag matches the code's version (0.5.0); installers and signing later (§3). |
| 2026-10 | Keyboard shortcuts reference (#128): Help → Keyboard shortcuts (F1, Ctrl+/), a window that reads the menus' keys from the menus themselves and lists the keys that work in place from one table (`ui/shortcuts.py`); a test keeps every key to one command (§12). |
| 2026-10 | Saved searches (#127) keep the page's own search and its filter bar separately, so opening one rebuilds removable chips, plus the layout; Ctrl+S updates a saved search's page in place, Save as makes another; names are unique ignoring case; save, rename, and delete are undoable (§8, §12). |
| 2026-10 | Related sections are embedded searches (#125, asked in review): a `related` filter on searches (from either side, kept in saved searches), so a filmography or cast gets list/grid, thumbnails, sorting, and tagging like a container's contents; remove moved from an × per link to the items' menu (§8, §12). |
| 2026-10 | Movies views (#125): Movies (tags inherited from collections), Actors, and Collections, as grids; actor pages with their filmography; collections list their movies by year (§9). |
| 2026-10 | Relationships by hand (#260): recorded in one core table, `user_relation` (core format 9), rather than a column on each theme relationship table; any related item can be removed, and the removal sticks; new items can be made from the Add dialog, and scans never delete items without an ingest key. The movies theme's cast labels were swapped (its movie pages said Filmography): `label` titles the section on an A's page, `reverse_label` on a B's (§5, §9). |
| 2026-10 | Video details (#259) with pymediainfo (new dependency, §3): runtime, quality, resolution, codec, and audio languages, the best version's; an .nfo's runtime wins. Movies theme version 2, so keeps read their videos again once (§9). |
| 2026-10 | Kodi .nfo files (#258) give movies their details, cast, and sets, winning over file names; read with `xml.etree` (no new dependency; expat's own entity-expansion limits guard against hostile XML). The theme API gains `ctx.related(name, entity)` so a theme can replace what it related before. Actor photos make their actor, since a theme can't find a file by path (§9). |
| 2026-10 | Movie pictures (#124): posters and screenshots by Plex/Kodi names (in a movie's folder, or `<name>-poster` beside loose videos); collections also get a `poster` role for their folder picture, so it isn't left unlinked; a movie's page shows a screenshot gallery (§9, §10). |
| 2026-10 | Movies (#123): Plex/Kodi layout. A movie is a folder named like one or holding one movie's videos, or else videos side by side whose names match once part, quality, and edition suffixes are set aside; its videos are its versions. Extras (Plex's suffixes and folder names) aren't movies. A collection is a folder below the root holding two or more movies' folders. Cast from Kodi .nfo files plus hand editing, and video details through pymediainfo, are their own tasks (#258–#260). |
| 2026-10 | Music theme version 2 (#253): songs record their bitrate in kbps, labelled "Bitrate (kbps)" rather than adding a display format to the theme API. A song with several versions keeps the best of their rates, so the value doesn't depend on which file was read last; a missing version still counts until it's removed. Keeps read every file again once after the upgrade (§6, §9). |
| 2026-10 | Keep both as versions (#121) is a one-click merge into the left item, offered when the primary role holds several files; Not a duplicate hides a group (until its files change) or a pair, stored in `dedupe_dismissal` (core format 8), undoable, with Show dismissed to review them (§13). |
| 2026-10 | Entity merge (#120): merged items are remembered in `entity_merge` with the files they had (core format 7), and a theme's `upsert` of a merged item's key, or `entities_of` one of its files, returns a ref whose writes are dropped, so the item stays merged through scans and the kept item's values come only from its own files. Moved files become links made by hand; in a filled one-file role they're left unlinked (Triage). A dialog picks the kept item and settles conflicting hand-set values; one undo step (§5, §9, §13). |
| 2026-10 | Compare (#119): a pane under the Dedupe page's lists, following the current row, rather than a window or a page, so moving through the list compares as you go and the merge buttons have a home. An identical-files group compares the items using its copies (§12). |
| 2026-10 | Near-duplicates (#118): two theme methods, `blocking_keys` and `similarity`, with a `near_duplicate_threshold`. Images store a difference hash and their average color in a hidden field; pictures far apart in color don't pair (the hash alone paired the demo's recolored icons). A theme version bump now re-reads every file once, through the root's ingest record, which notes versions past 1. Music ignores same-album pairs (§6, §13). |
| 2026-10 | Exact duplicates (#117): grouped by fingerprint, most space wasted first; checking by full hash happens only when asked and is kept for the session, not stored, so the keep's schema doesn't change (§13). |
| 2026-10 | A keep opens on its dashboard (#115). GUI tests keep starting from Search all through a conftest fixture, and one test checks the real opening page (§12). |
| 2026-10 | Theme dashboard cards (#114) come both ways: declared statistics and most common values computed in SQL (`stat`, `top_values` in `Theme.dashboard`), and `@dashboard_card` methods given a read-only `DashboardContext` as an escape hatch. A failing card shows its error. Top values link by the field's search kind, and fields that aren't searchable aren't linked (§9, §12). |
| 2026-10 | Dashboard (#113): a grid of cards whose numbers link onward (types to Search all narrowed, untagged to Triage, folders to Keep configuration, tags to a search), with a thumbnail strip of the 12 newest items; least used tags list unused ones first and don't repeat the most used (§12). |
| 2026-10 | Links made by hand (#243) are marked `by_user` (core format 6) and invisible to themes from the file's side (`entities_of`), so re-reading a file never renames or regroups the user's item. Themes can't unlink them or displace them in a one-file role, and `linked` still counts them. Made with Link to item… from Triage (search, then a role that takes the file); undone and unlinked from the item's page (§5, §9, §12). |
| 2026-10 | Files a scan now leaves out (excludes, extensions, the theme's folder rule) are flagged `skipped` rather than marked missing or deleted: a new boolean column (core format 5) rather than a status value, since status values have a CHECK constraint SQLite can't change without rebuilding the table. Status stays as last seen; seeing the file again in scope clears the flag (#152, §6). |
| 2026-10 | Activity panel (#111): a bottom dock, hidden until opened from View → Activity (Ctrl+Shift+A) or the status bar's problem badge, never popping up by itself. It shows the scan's progress or summary, the thumbnail queue, folder states, and a session-only problem log (newest 1,000) from scans and thumbnails, with Show in file manager, Copy, and Clear (§12). |
| 2026-10 | Triage (#110): unlinked files, untagged items, and items with all files missing. Dismiss hides until changed, through a marker in a new `triage_dismissal` table (core format 4), undoably. Deleting missing items is undoable. Skip in scans adds an exact exclude pattern. Hand-made links are deferred to #243, since scans don't yet respect them (§5, §8, §12). |
| 2026-10 | Background thumbnails after a scan (§6 step 7, never built until #241): entities with no remembered source or whose remembered file was re-ingested since the scan began, newest first, on one session-owned thread separate from the grids' workers; on by default, `[thumbnails] after_scan = false` turns it off. |
| 2026-10 | The Keep configuration window has Folders, Thumbnails, and Keep tabs (and Notes since #381); the thumbnail size applies at once, theme options (keep-wide and per folder) at the next scan; Clear thumbnail cache moved from the Keep menu to the Thumbnails tab (§10, §12). |
| 2026-09 | Keep launcher is a separate start dialog; one main window per keep; new keeps store the watched folder exactly as typed and derive the root's name and id from its last segment (§12). |
| 2026-09 | Text search uses an FTS5 table with the trigram tokenizer (substring matching, case- and diacritic-insensitive) kept in sync by the DB writer. A word-based tokenizer was rejected because it cannot match inside words ("bey" would not find "Abbey"). The roughly 5× larger index (about 20 MB per 50k entities) is acceptable (§8). |
| 2026-10 | Profiling (#131, §3 Performance): on a 9,341-file share, scanning is dominated by per-file open and read latency, done one file at a time, then by image decoding (native already) and SQLAlchemy statement overhead in ingest. No Rust; instead parallel fingerprinting, `prepare`, and background thumbnails, and batched ingest statements (#273 to #276). `scripts/profile_scan.py` reproduces the measurements. |
| 2026-10 | Explicit theme schema migrations (#173, §9 "Theme schema versions"): `Theme.migrate_schema(from_version, ops)` with `rename_field`, `change_type` (default or theme-supplied conversion; one bad value fails the whole upgrade), and `drop_field`, done by the core (`core/theme_migrate.py`) before the additive step and `migrate()`, in the same transaction. Renames carry field provenance and saved searches along. `API_VERSION` becomes 2, and a theme using `migrate_schema` must state `api_version = 2`. Entity types themselves still can't be renamed or dropped (their type ids are stored on every item); no theme has needed it. |
| 2026-10 | Installers and an icon (#269, §3 Packaging): an Inno Setup per-user setup on Windows, a DMG on macOS, and an AppImage on Linux, each installed and checked in CI; the zips stay for running without installing. The icon is a tag on a folder, an SVG rendered to every format by `scripts/make_icons.py`, with the results committed. Signing waits on certificates (#279). |
| 2026-10 | Fingerprints are hashed 8 files at a time (#273, §3 Performance, §5 Fingerprints): 77 s down to 15.5 s on a 9,341-file share. More threads gained little (12 s at 32); results stay in queue order and errors are reported on the scan's thread. |
| 2026-10 | The theme's `prepare` runs on 8 threads, 10 resources per call, reading the next batch while the writer ingests the current one (#274, §3 Performance, §6). The assets2d first scan on the share went from 192 s to 107 s. Theme API: `prepare` calls may run at once, so they keep state local (documented in `api.py` and THEMES.md; built-in themes already did). |
| 2026-10 | The background thumbnail queue makes 4 thumbnails at once (#275, §3 Performance, §6 step 7): 4 times as fast on the share. 8 would be 7 times, but would compete with the grids' own 4 threads for the share while someone browses. |
| 2026-10 | Folder pickers start where a folder was last chosen in any of them, remembered across sessions in `settings.toml` (`last_folder`, §4); a new keep's location starts there too, rather than the home folder (asked for in review). |
| 2026-10 | Cheaper ingest (#276, §3 Performance): the ingest session caches what only it can change during its transaction (next id, keys, items it made, user-edited fields, values written, whether any merges exist) and runs prebuilt Core statements. 14.1 statements per new file became 6.2; 50,000 files ingest in 13 s instead of 122 s. Tests that change the database behind an open session's back don't reflect the app (all writes go through the one writer), so one such test was restructured. |
| 2026-10 | `aggregate_up` is the filter bar's **By contents** toggle beside Contained and Inherit tags, remembered per view and in saved searches; themes can start a view with it on (`SearchView(aggregate_up=True)`, theme API additive) (#133, §8, §12, §15). |
| 2026-10 | MediaInfo parses one video at a time (a lock in the movies theme, #292): since `prepare` runs on several threads (#274), concurrent parses crashed the test process on macOS twice. Native libraries a theme uses must be thread-safe or guarded; PDFium (planned for books, #297) needs the same. |
| 2026-10 | Planned in review: a **Books** built-in theme (M20, #293): Book and Comic works with files as versions and per-file sources, authors (merging joins one writer's spellings), series, universes, and collections; EPUB, comic archives, PDF, and Markdown first, then DOCX, ODT, and Pages; new dependencies pypdfium2 and PyYAML (§3, §9). |
| 2026-10 | Planned in review: **file keywords** (§7): files' own tags become Tagalot tags only by matching defined tags (full path, alias, or unique name); mapping a keyword adds it as the tag's alias; unmatched ones are surfaced (a page, a TOOLS count, a scan notice, the item page, Triage); a file-derived tag removed by hand stays removed for that item. Aliases were chosen over a separate mapping table: one concept, already undoable and shown in the tag manager; the cost is one tag per keyword. |
| 2026-10 | Planned in review: **writing back** front matter, the one exception to never modifying user files: only in folders marked writable, only by an explicit action, with a preview, a backup in the keep folder, and a changed-file check; only the metadata block changes (§4). |
| 2026-10 | Planned in review: link files (`.url`, `.webloc`, `.desktop`) are a books format, giving a work its `link`; a `"url"` field display opens http(s) links in the browser. Works from a pasted link with no file are future work (§14). Tagalot still never fetches pages. |
| 2026-10 | Tag types (#135, §7 *Tag types*, core format 10): any tag can be limited to some item types, inherited by its sub-tags, which can only narrow them; setting a limit asks before taking the tag off items of other types (undoable); tagging skips and reports items a tag doesn't allow; the tagging panel hides tags the selection can't have (decided in review). Stored as a column on `tag`, so tag undo covers it with no new machinery. |
| 2026-10 | File keywords, the core (#294, §7, core format 11): `ctx.keywords` stores what a file says about an item; matching by path, alias, or unique name; file tags are derived `by_file` rows recomputed after scans and tag operations (and their undo), so undo history holds only the user's rows and removal records. A removal by hand is remembered with its undo step; a merge moves file tags and removals to the target. |
| 2026-10 | File keywords, the page (#295, §7, §12): TOOLS → File keywords with an unmatched count, Map to tag / Create tag / Ignore (each undoable), and a scan notice for new unmatched keywords. The music and movies themes report genres as file keywords (both theme version 3; asked in review), so existing keeps read their files again once. |
| 2026-10 | Planned in review: after Books (M20) come **Research** (M21, #306), **Online details** (M22, #307, with per-keep consent before the first lookup), and **Search inside documents** (M23, #308, opt-in, Words or Substrings, switchable by rebuilding from stored text) (§14). Books' format readers become public `themes.api` helpers so Research and third-party themes share them (§9 books). |
| 2026-10 | Online details designed in review (M22, #307, §9): only the core goes online, for services themes declare (theme API version 5); automatic after scans and on demand; looked-up details rank below files' curated metadata (note, sidecar, export) and above PDF guesses; per-keep consent naming each service and what it is sent; `urllib`, cached in the keep, rate-limited. |
| 2026-10 | Online lookups in the core (#339, §9 *Online details*, §5): theme API version 5 (`OnlineSource`, `OnlineResponse`, `Theme.online_sources`, `online_requests`, `online_details`); core format 14 adds `online_response`, which keeps every answer, 404s included; `[online] lookups` in `keep.toml`; a service that can't be reached is given up on for the run; extraction replaces `fetched` values; lookups aren't undo steps. |
| 2026-10 | Look up online (#340, §9 *Online details*): after scans, the items made or changed; the consent question only when something would be fetched; allowing looks up the whole keep once; Not now holds until the keep is reopened; Look up online is offered on every type of a theme with services (the theme decides per item). |
| 2026-10 | Research online details (#341, §9 research): Crossref by DOI, arXiv only for papers without one; each service a source in `origins` ranked 25 (between export and PDF), kept through scans; descriptive details only, never identifiers. |
| 2026-10 | Books online details (#342, §9 books): Open Library by ISBN fills only empty fields (year, publisher, language, description), never title, authors, series, or identifiers. |
| 2026-10 | Books: covers and authors' photos from Open Library (#484, M35, §9 books): by the cover and photo numbers its answers give (service Open Library Covers); an author's Open Library ID kept in a new Author field, found from a book with one author (several can't be paired) and checked against the record's names; the core looks up, in the same run, items the answers set details on (a second round). |
| 2026-10 | Gallery tabs' kind chips (#514, §12 *Kind chips*): the same chips over a gallery tab's pictures, filtered on screen; an archive is a file of its type, so hiding ZIP hides its group. Decided while building: the chips sit above the scrolling area (the tab is a widget holding chips and the scroll area), and galleries that aren't tabs have none. |
| 2026-10 | Kind chips (#513, §12 *Kind chips*; M36, decided in discussion, 2026-10-23): chips under the filter bar hide and show kinds, toggled in place, remembered per view and tab. Decided while building: Search all's one-type list has chips too; in the tree the chips count containers' contents (as with Contained) and kept hidden containers are dimmed; tab memory uses the tabs' existing state keys, so a related tab (an author's Books) shares its choice across pages, like its layout. |
| 2026-10 | Hiding kinds of items (#512, §8 *Kinds*; M36, asked for and decided in discussion, 2026-10-23): `SearchSpec.hidden_kinds` leaves out an item when every one of its kinds (the file types of the files in its primary role, else its type) is hidden, so a book with an EPUB and a PDF stays while either is shown; well-known extension aliases are one kind; in a tree a hidden container stays while it holds a shown item. Not saved with searches. Decided while building: an item's own files are those of its primary role, so a cover or gallery picture gives no kind; the core always gives a file-less item its type, and the UI labels the page's own type **No file**. |
| 2026-10 | A keep's environment (#544, M35, §9 *Online details*, §4; asked for in discussion, 2026-10-25): named variables per keep for an online service's key, set in the Keep tab, kept in the user's `settings.toml` under the keep's id (not the keychain, not the keep folder), ahead of the real environment; values hidden unless shown; the variables services need are suggested. Revises #485: a token is written only there, when the user types it. |
| 2026-10 | RAR tools off PATH on Windows (#452, M39, §10 *Archives*): WinRAR's UnRAR and 7-Zip found in their programs folders when not on PATH; `tagalot --check` names the tool and warns about bsdtar; a `.cbr`'s ComicInfo that needs a missing tool is reported (`NoRarTool`), not read as absent. Not done: trying the next tool when one fails. |
| 2026-10 | Remembered column order and widths (#417, M40, §12 *List layout*; asked in review of #413): a view's column order (`column_order`, every key it has seen, so Search all keeps each type's) and the widths the user dragged (`column_widths`, by key; not the last shown column, which fills the rest) are kept per view in `ui_state.json`; columns new to the view go after their theme neighbour, gone ones are skipped. **Reset column order and widths** in the header menu forgets both, leaving which columns are shown. Decided while building: Search all's sections, which show a type's first items, keep the theme's order and widths. |
| 2026-10 | The Open Keep dialog shows each keep's icon and a hint of its colors (#559, M43, §12 *Keep launcher*; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27): its own icon, else its theme's, else a generic one; decided while building: its colors as a badge behind the icon (its background and text, an accent stripe for its own colors; light or dark squares for Light and Dark), the rows shown again when the themes load. |
| 2026-10 | Keep configuration fits on the screen (#593, M47; asked by the user, 2026-10-29): each tab scrolls when it's taller than the window (`QScrollArea`), so the window no longer has to be as tall as its tallest tab (Notes, Keep: 703 px); it opens at 820 × 720, wider when a tab needs it, within 90% of the screen's free area and centered on it, so its top and buttons are on screen. |
| 2026-10 | Each keep remembers its window's size (#594, M47; asked by the user, 2026-10-29): saved in the keep's `ui_state.json` (`window`: width, height, maximized) when its window closes, its size when not maximized; it opens at that size within the screen's free area (at least 640 × 400), maximized if it was. Decided while building: the size only, not the position, which another screen layout may not have; in `ui_state.json`, so each keep has its own, as asked. |
| 2026-10 | Linked places designed (M48, #597, §13 *Linked places*; decided in discussion, 2026-10-30): one file or folder in several places (symbolic links, junctions, hard links, Windows shortcuts, links a share shows as ordinary files) is one item whose places stay resources of their own, linked as a group (core format 33); tags and the user's values shared, listed once in a search, filed under the containers each place's path gives. Real links and shortcuts are linked automatically; links a share hides are linked by the user (**Link as one** for files, **Linked folders** with a preview), and files that differ block linking with a warning and **Check again**; files appearing later in linked folders are linked when their fingerprints match. Targets outside the keep's folders are included after asking once; broken links go to Triage. Merge stays for two copies of one thing. |
| 2026-10 | `tagalot --backup` (#608, M49, §4 *Rules*): `keep.db` (SQLite's backup API, made one file) and `keep.toml` into `<to>/<keep>/<date>_<time>/`, checked with `integrity_check` before it counts; `--keep-last N` prunes only sound copies; a line per run in `<to>/backup.log`; exit codes 0, 1 (failed or damaged), 2 (usage). Fixed on the way: a scan no longer unlinks what Link folders linked (#601's pairs were taken for links found as files). |
