The walk reports links and shortcuts whose targets are gone, each scan replaces its root's rows, and Triage's **Links** tab lists them with where they pointed. Decided while building: Show in file manager shows the link; an offline root keeps its rows. |

Dedupe's **Linked folders** tab suggests folders with the same name from files sharing a fingerprint, previews them, and links them (`how` `inside`), blocked while files differ unless Check again reads them the same; scans link new and changed paths in linked folders that match, and Triage's **Links** tab lists those that differ. Decided while building: the folder pair is its own table (themes needn't record folders); only new and changed paths are linked by scans, so the user's Unlink sticks; a fingerprint shared by more than 20 files, or an empty file, suggests nothing. Decided while building: linking folds the place's earlier item in as a merge with the defaults, its containers then the place's; pages list places after their file, and opening takes the first one there. |

| 2026-10 | A glimpse of an item's own Markdown file (#554, M42, §12; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27): an item whose primary role's first file is Markdown (`.md`, `.markdown`, not inside an archive; `EntityDetail.markdown_file`) and that has no note, typed or a file (the note wins), shows a glimpse of it in the header's note. Not a reader: its table of contents if it has one, else its opening (up to the end of its second paragraph or list, headings and pictures on the way included, at most 1,500 characters, cut at a word), with a first heading repeating the title left out. A table of contents is one the file has (decided while building, the question left open in discussion): a list of two links or more whose every item links to a heading of the file (`- [Chapter 1](#chapter-1)`), with the heading above it when that names it (*Contents*, *TOC*); or a marker Markdown tools expand into one (`[TOC]`, `[[_TOC_]]`, `{:toc}`, `<!-- toc -->`), shown as an outline of its headings three levels deep. A file with headings but neither shows its opening. Read when the page opens, in its worker (`read_note`), never at scans: no keep format change, always current; nothing while it can't be read (offline, gone, front matter that doesn't parse). Rendered as notes are (#553), its pictures and links from its folder and its assets folders. **Open file** (instead of **Open note**) opens it; so does a link to one of its headings. **Add a note** still types a note, which then takes its place; the file's text isn't edited in place (no ✎, no double-click). |
| 2026-10 | Rich Markdown in the page's note (#553, M42, §12; decided in discussion, 2026-10-27): the note is rendered as GitHub-flavoured Markdown by Qt (`ui/markdown_doc.py`), one parse giving both forms. Collapsed, its main text only: paragraphs, lists, and quotes keep their bold, italics, and links; headings, pictures, tables, code blocks, and horizontal rules are taken out of a copy of the document (counted, `Hidden`). **Show more** shows whenever something is left out, not only when the text is too tall; a note left with nothing says what it holds ("A table and 2 pictures"). Expanded: all of it, up to half the window's height (following the window), past which it scrolls. Styled from the palette, and again when it changes: tables with borders and a bold, shaded header row; headings with space above; code in the system's fixed-width font on a shade of the background; quotes indented and dimmed; links in the link color; pictures scaled down to the note's width once a resize pauses (`PICTURES_DELAY`, 250 ms) and only when the width changed by more than a scroll bar's width (found in review: a picture whose height followed the width as it changed, whether rescaled at each change or by a layout percentage, showed and hid the page's scroll bar, which changed the width again; Qt's layout bounced endlessly and overflowed its stack, crashing the app; `tagalot` now enables `faulthandler`, so such a crash prints where Python was); the text's own color set in the document, not left to the widget (whose style sheet kept the old colors when Windows switched to light mode, found in review). Heights are measured at the note's whole width, so whether its scroll bar shows can't change the outcome (in review, a note near the limit flipped its scroll bar endlessly, then the app crashed); the page fits its header whenever the note's height changes (`NoteView.resized`). HTML in a note is read as Qt reads it, so its pictures and tables are hidden like Markdown's (rather than parsing without HTML). Not supported: footnotes, math, diagrams, Obsidian callouts (shown as quotes). |
| 2026-10 | Your own front-matter key aliases (#411, M41, §4, §5, §9 *Notes*; decided in discussion, 2026-10-26, as recommended): `[notes.key_aliases]` in both `settings.toml` and `keep.toml` (the keep's winning), `key = "a field's name or label"`, for every type with such a field; only keys that would be Extra fields, in notes and Markdown files' own front matter, the file's own key for the field winning; added in Keep configuration → Notes and from an Extra field's arrow menu (**Read this key as a field…**); applied at the next scan, which reads notes and Markdown files again when the aliases changed (core format 30, `note_key_alias`); Write to file… keeps the aliased key. Decided while building: a value an alias gives that doesn't convert stays an Extra field (and is reported, in a note). |

| 2026-10 | Tags panel shows tag groups (#535, M38, §12 *Tagging panel*; decided in discussion, 2026-10-25): Groups ▾ with All tags or any groups, the user's choice in `ui_state.json`; each group a heading over its tags lifted to the top level (the user's idea, over keeping greyed ancestors); a tag in two groups listed twice; filtering adds Other tags; right-click Groups ▸ ticks groups or makes one. | 2026-10 | Triage's **New in archives** (#520, §12 *Triage*; designed in discussion, 2026-10-23): archives read member by member with members not decided on, with Review… (the dialog, new members bold, marked (new), and hidden) and Hide all new, and a line on the dashboard's Untagged card. Decided while building: the dialog's OK now decides every member it lists, so a new one left hidden is recorded hidden rather than staying undecided as #519 had it (the dialog marks it, so the user has seen it; otherwise reviewing could never empty the list); the tab sits before Skipped; new members aren't resources until read, so the Skipped tab never lists them. |

Decided in discussion (2026-10-24): hiding a member of an archive read whole keeps it whole, so members added later are still read; Hide is offered on Files rows and in Duplicates. Decided while building: the dialog on an archive not read whole makes it member by member; members hidden are skipped at once, the rest read by a scan of the folder; choices aren't undo steps; a search result's commands are in an **Inside the archive** submenu filled when shown. Asked for in review: the dialog's **Show all** and **Hide all**, and **Show thumbnails** (off by default and remembered per keep; a grid of tiles in place of the tree, since 48 px pictures in tree rows were too small to tell apart, 96 px by default with a size each user picks for the keep, since telling similar pictures apart can take more; drawn for any file a renderer takes, so `archive_thumbnails` now draws fonts and PDFs inside archives too, not only pictures). |

Decided while building: Duplicates has no command that keeps or removes a file, so there is nothing to turn into hiding a member; hiding one, with a way to show it again, comes with choosing what to read inside an archive (#519). Decided while building: hiding is worked out from the files, not stored; a partial index of skipped files (core format 28) keeps searches as fast as before (50k-item benchmark); tag usage counts still include hidden items, as tag changes reach them. |

Decided in discussion: a member's `relpath` is the archive's and its path inside it; members count in the archive's folder for themes, which read an extracted copy (`ResourceInfo.archive`, `archive_path`, theme API version 22); a hidden member is skipped, keeping its tags; files the theme doesn't take and archives inside archives are left out; thumbnails, Open, and file rows are a second issue (#524). Decided while building: an archive is listed again only when its size or date change; a member over 256 MB isn't read. |
| 2026-10 | Tag icons that are pictures (#507, §7 *Tag icons*, §5; asked for in discussion, 2026-10-21): a small picture (PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP, or SVG drawn to PNG) as a tag's icon instead of an emoji, kept in the keep like tag pictures (core format 26, `tag_icon_picture`, at most 64 px). Decided in discussion: animated GIFs play in tag trees only, badges show their first frame; export names the picture's file under `icon` (`<file name> pictures/<tag path> icon.png`, beside the tag pictures), import tells a file from an emoji by its picture extension, as an emoji is one character. Near-duplicates also block by title, and works whose writers are known and share no one aren't alike. Books theme version 2, so existing keeps read their books again once (author lists come out right); ingest never merges two items a keep already has (tags, edits, and links may be on both), so pairs split before are offered by Dedupe's similar items instead. No books theme version bump: files of a newly accepted extension were never recorded, so the next scan finds them as new. `tagalot --check` lists no MOBI reader: like the EPUB one it needs no library that a build could lack. |

No schema change (a new key; a store without it counts as version 1). |
| 2026-10 | Notes and galleries designed (§9 *Notes*, *Galleries*; discussed in review, 2026-10-10). Notes: Markdown files describing items: inside an item's folder (`<folder>.md`, else `index`, else `README`, also `.markdown`; not beside it), named by a theme (`ctx.note`), or in one notes folder per keep (typed by `tagalot-type` or subfolder, matched by title and `aliases`, chosen explicitly, possibly inside a content folder and then skipped as content). A note gives the title (winning over a folder's name), tags as file keywords, fields (below the theme's file reading, above online details), other keys as Extra fields marked as the note's (removal remembered per item), and a body shown in the page's header (capped, **Show more**). Keys `tagalot-…` are reserved; ignored keys come from built-in defaults, `settings.toml`, and `keep.toml`. In-app notes for items without a note file, and **Save as note…** into a separately writable notes folder (never replacing a file; layout `folders` or `one-folder`); the keep keeps only what it last read from a note file. Galleries: picture roles filled from named subfolders and archives, or from a folder or archive beside a notes-folder note; archives' pictures listed on the page, not made resources (archive members as resources stays future work, opt-in by folder pattern). Theme API 8 for `ctx.note` and `gallery`/`folders`. |
| 2026-10 | Folder notes built (#379, §5, §9 *Notes*): core format 15 (`entity_note` with the folder looked in and the file's size and time, `note_key_removal`, `note` provenance; `field_provenance` rebuilt for its CHECK), theme API 8 (`ctx.note`). Decided while building: note-named files are recorded only in folders the theme records (its `dirs` rule), not in every folder; files never replace a note's value with an empty one (themes write every field on each read) nor a folder note's title, and online details never replace a note's value; a value a note stops giving is cleared and the item's primary files are ingested again at once so the theme's value comes back; a note that can't be read leaves the item as it was. |

| 2026-10 | Assets folders (#418, §4, §9 books *Assets folders*; asked in review): a Markdown file's relative pictures and links not beside it are looked for under its item's own assets folder (front matter `assets`, an editable field the user's value wins on, written back by Write to file…), then the watched folder's default (`[[roots]] assets`), then the keep's (`[markdown] assets`), all inside the watched folder. Only the key `assets` (not Obsidian's settings file). The theme API gains `ctx.resource_at(…, assets=)` (`API_VERSION` 9, chosen over a separate method), so the books theme finds covers there (it and the template state 9); the defaults reach ingest with the theme options (`__assets__`, set only when there is one), so changing one reads the folder again. Books theme version 4 (the Assets folder field). |
| 2026-10 | Keyword splitting (#420, bug found in review): a list's items are one keyword each, never split on spaces (`- Science Fiction`), and spaces beside a path separator don't split a path (`Locale > Fantasy > Castle`), which had become three keywords, one matching nothing. Files already read keep their old keywords until they change and are read again. |
| 2026-10 | Front matter's `synopsis`, `blurb`, and `abstract` are a description too, after `description` and `summary` (#409, §9 *Markdown*); Write to file… keeps the key the file uses. Books theme version 3, so keeps read their files again once. A note's `synopsis` is still an Extra field: notes match fields by name and label only (#411 is for aliases of your own). |
| 2026-10 | The list's columns can be moved by dragging their headings, as the tree's could (found in review with #413, §12); the list and tree keep one order (`SearchPage._same_order`). The order isn't remembered: it lasts until the search's columns change. |
| 2026-10 | The tree's header has the list's column menu (found in review with #413, §12): the two layouts show the same columns, and a column shown from the tree starts at the list's width. |
| 2026-10 | The list layout's title column can be resized (#413, §12, found in review): instead of stretching (which Qt never lets the user resize), it fits the spare width between 200 and 480 px until the user resizes it, and the last shown column fills the rest, as in the tree. A fixed starting width was tried and dropped while building: on a narrow window it pushed columns out of view, which the stretching title never did. |
| 2026-10 | Tag import and export as YAML (#395, §7, §12): nested like the tree; shorthand lists hold names only (decided in review); import matches by path ignoring case and accents and by alias; a name matched with other case or accents takes the file's spelling (asked in review), an alias match never renames; it merges: given properties replace, aliases are added, missing ones stay (decided in review); problems are left out with their line; deleting unnamed unused tags is an off-by-default checkbox; one undoable step. |
| 2026-10 | Notes in the built-in themes and demos (#384, §9): movies' `ctx.note` for a movie's own folder (theme version 4); assets' Artist gains a Website field for its note to fill (chosen in review) and a Folder field, so Biggest artists counts a renamed artist's assets (assets theme version 3); the movies and books demos come with their notes folder chosen (chosen in review), and `make_movies_demo`/`make_books_demo` take `notes=False` for tests that choose one. |
| 2026-10 | Notes on Skip entries (#334, §4, §12): `[roots.exclude_notes]` beside a plain `exclude` list (chosen over turning `exclude` into tables, which older builds would refuse), edited after ` # ` in the Skip box, and written by Tagalot when it skips an export or a Triage file; `exact_pattern` brackets `#`. Themes ignoring files with a reason is left out. |
| 2026-10 | Projects: containers made by hand (#329, §5, §9; chosen in review over a Project–Paper relationship): `Entity.made_by_hand` (API version 4) gives New …, Add to …, and Remove from …; containment by hand is recorded in a new core table, `user_contains` (core format 13), so scans keep it, and undo and merges carry it. Projects are ordinary containers: tags inherit, Within works. |
| 2026-10 | Research actions and citations by hand (#322, §4, §9; decided in review): `ActionContext` gains `copy_text`, `open_url` (http/https), and `save_text` (API version 4); saving an export inside a watched folder is allowed after a warning, never over an existing file, and the file is added to that folder's Skip list so it isn't read back (why it was skipped is #334). A relationship of a type with itself has a direction (two sections: Cites, Cited by; `SearchSpec.related_side`), or none with `symmetric=True` (Related). |
| 2026-10 | Literature notes (#321, §9 research): Markdown notes naming a paper by citation key, DOI, arXiv ID, or PubMed ID are its `notes`, its best-ranked source, and their tags its file keywords. `read_last` may be an ordered tuple (still API version 4, unreleased), so notes are read after the bibliographies whose citation keys they use. |
| 2026-10 | Bibliographies describe papers (#320, §6, §9 research; asked in review): `Theme.read_last` (API version 4) reads bibliography files last in a scan and again when other files of their root were read, so a library export meets PDFs added later; chosen over hidden items per entry. Each source's details are kept per resource in the paper's hidden `origins` field and merged by rank (sidecar, export, PDF, named file, bare name), replacing #318's `titled` and `credited` fields (the theme hadn't been released). Venue arrives here, from bibliography entries. |
| 2026-10 | Bibliography readers (#319, §9 research): `read_bibliography` (and `BIBLIOGRAPHY_EXTENSIONS`) in the API, from a new standard-library-only module `themes/bibliography.py`: Tagalot's own BibTeX/BibLaTeX reader (macros, `#`, LaTeX accents and commands, names in both orders, braced corporate names, file fields of Zotero, Better BibTeX, JabRef, and Mendeley), RIS, and CSL-JSON, returning the same keys. Kinds add conference paper and web page; arXiv-only entries are preprints. |
| 2026-10 | Research theme, papers from PDFs (#318, §9 research): Paper and Author, keyed by DOI, arXiv ID, PubMed ID, else title and first author; identifiers from the first page's text (`pdf_text`, `find_identifiers` join the API); titles and authors from document info, Zotero-style file names, or the bare name, the better source winning whichever file is read first. Venue moves to #320 (only bibliographies give it) and Project to #329 (it needs making items and containing papers by hand), rather than shipping empty views. PDF and Word keywords keep phrases whole (`split_keywords(spaces=False)`). |
| 2026-10 | Ordered relationships (#317, §5, §9; API version 4, core format 12): `related(..., ordered=True)` with a `position` on the link table, `ctx.relate(..., position=n)`, `ctx.related` in order from the `B` side, the `@position` sort for a `B`'s embedded related search, and Move up / Move down by hand. A hand-made order is kept by a new core table, `user_order` (asked in review: chosen over positions on `user_relation`, which would have made every author the user's, and over letting scans win), so scans leave it alone and append new items. |
| 2026-10 | Research theme designed in review (M21, #306, §9 research): authors in order through **ordered relationships** (API version 4); bibliography files read by Tagalot's own reader (BibTeX, RIS, CSL-JSON), replaceable by bibtexparser later without changing the helper; both sidecars and library exports (Zotero, Better BibTeX, Mendeley, JabRef) describe papers, matched by file path, DOI, arXiv ID, or PubMed ID; precedence: user, note, sidecar, export, PDF, file name. No release before it (0.7.0 waits). |
| 2026-10 | Writing back to files (#299, §4, §3, §9; decided in review): `writable = true` per root (Keep configuration asks before turning it on); **Write to file…** on items of the theme's `write_back` types previews each Markdown file's front matter, refuses files changed since they were read, backs files up into the keep, and changes only the block. The theme maps edited fields to keys (`Theme.front_matter`, API version 3); tags and user-edited fields are written. YAML is edited round-trip with the new dependency ruamel.yaml; TOML by replacing the written keys' lines. Not undoable from Edit → Undo; the backups are. |
| 2026-10 | Every field is a list column (#314, §12; asked for in review of #313): fields not on cards are offered in the header menu, hidden until shown; showing one is remembered per view as `shown_columns` in `ui_state.json`. Fields pages don't show (`detail=False`) aren't offered. |
| 2026-10 | Books theme, office documents and link files (#298, §9): `read_office_info`, `office_cover`, and `read_link_file` join the API, and the `"url"` field display is built, both under API version 3 (not yet released, so no new version). A `"url"` value is a link on the item's page only; elsewhere a click selects the item, so it reads as text. Field values on pages are now always plain text, never markup. A Pages package folder isn't read. |
| 2026-10 | Books theme, PDF and Markdown (#297, §3, §9): pypdfium2 and PyYAML added (imported when used; PDFium behind one lock). `API_VERSION` becomes 3 for `ctx.resource_at` (asked in review: chosen over finding covers by naming convention or leaving them out), so a Markdown book's `cover` links the picture it names, whichever is scanned first; the books theme states `api_version = 3`, and so does the template. Readers `read_pdf_info`, `pdf_cover`, `read_front_matter`, `split_people`, and `split_keywords` join the API. PDFs render their first page by extension, as EPUBs do. |
| 2026-10 | Books theme, EPUB and comics (#296, §9 books, §10): readers are public API helpers in `themes/readers.py` (`read_epub`, `epub_cover`, `read_comic_info`), re-exported by `themes.api`, standard library only when loaded (the comic reader borrows the core's archive reader when called). Credits are four relationships (books' and comics' writers and artists), since a relationship joins two types. A work's one file replaces what it said; with several files each only adds. EPUB covers render by extension, not by adding EPUB to the archive kind. Whole-number floats show without ".0" (a book's number in its series). |
| 2026-10 | File keywords, everywhere else (#295): an item's page shows what its files say and what became of each keyword (Map, Ignore, Restore a removed file tag, undoable); Triage lists items with unmatched keywords; the tagging panel italicizes tags the selection has only from files; the tag manager counts uses from files. |
